| restore | partial | `--source`, `--staged`, `--worktree`, path restore with shared pathspec magic (`plain prefix`, wildcard with literal path/directory-prefix fallback for bracket-like names, `:(top)`/`:/`, `:(glob)`, `:(literal)`, `:(icase)`, `:(exclude)`/`:!`/`:^`) including symlink tree/index entries restored as real symlinks on supported platforms (link target bytes are not followed), `--pathspec-from-file`/`--pathspec-file-nul`, `--overlay`/`--no-overlay` (real toggle, last wins — overlay only creates/updates source paths and never removes tracked paths absent from the source; default no-overlay removes them), and `--no-progress` (accepted no-op — Libra's restore renders no progress meter) are supported; unsupported platforms fail closed with an explicit symlink diagnostic rather than writing a regular file; conflict-stage restore `--ours`/`-2` and `--theirs`/`-3` write the chosen merge stage to the working tree only (index left unmerged), a plain restore over an unmerged path is refused (`LBR-CONFLICT-001`, exit 128), and `--ignore-unmerged` skips unmerged paths; a modify/delete conflict (the requested side deleted the file — its stage absent) removes the working-tree file and exits 0 in the default no-overlay mode (restoring a deletion means deleting; matches `git restore`), while `--overlay` instead errors `does not have our/their version` (exit 128); mid-rebase `--ours`/`--theirs` follow Git's swap (stages read verbatim, so `--ours` = the branch rebased onto / new base, `--theirs` = the commit being replayed), whereas merge/cherry-pick keep ours = HEAD / theirs = incoming; `--ours`/`--theirs` operate only on unmerged paths — a non-conflicted pathspec is skipped (or `PathspecNotMatched` when every pathspec is non-conflicted), and Libra intentionally does not fall through to Git's stage-0 (index) restore for such paths so a dirty file is never silently reverted; `--merge` (and `--conflict=merge|diff3`) rewrites the working tree for unmerged paths with the conflict markers rebuilt from the index stages — Libra's whole-file `ours`/`theirs` markers (generic `ours`/`theirs` labels — `diff3` also emits the base block; `zdiff3` is not supported; unlike `libra merge`/`cherry-pick`, which now write line-level markers, restore's index-stage rebuild stays whole-file), not Git's line-level 3-way; only the `--progress` meter is not exposed Remaining unsupported interactive options (`-p`/`--patch`) fail with `LBR-UNSUPPORTED-001` (D15).; `LBR-UNSUPPORTED-001` diagnostic for unsupported interactive options restored files carry the tree/index entry mode's permission bits under the process `umask`, and replacing an existing file clears a stale execute bit (plan issues/470 FM-01, ADR-FM-02). `restore --staged` removes intent-to-add entries (issues/476 WT-07). |
| status | supported | common Git status surface plus `<pathspec>...` filtering (shared pathspec engine (honors global --literal-pathspecs): plain prefixes, default wildcards, `:(top)`, `:(exclude)`, `:(icase)`, `:(literal)`, `:(glob)`), `--porcelain` v1/v2, `-u`/`--untracked-files` (`no`/`normal`/`all`; bare = `all`, short attached values `-uno`/`-uall`/`-unormal`), `--short`, `--long` (explicit default; conflicts with `--short`/`--porcelain`), `--branch`/`-b` (+`--no-branch`), `--show-stash`/`--no-show-stash`, the `status.*` display config defaults (`status.showUntrackedFiles=no|normal|all`, `status.short`, `status.branch` — short format only, porcelain headers still need an explicit `-b` so porcelain stays config-immune, `status.showStash`, and the config-only `status.relativePaths` — `false` keeps repository-root-relative paths, and `core.quotePath` (strict Git boolean, default `true`; human-short and non-`-z` porcelain v1/v2 paths C-style-escape control bytes, `"` and `\`, plus `>0x7F` octal escapes under the default — with `false`, bytes above `0x7F` are written raw on those surfaces, non-UTF-8 names included, while control characters, `"` and `\` stay escaped — and `-z` records stay raw) — all six read through the strict local→global→system cascade, validated up front and failing closed with `LBR-CLI-002`/`LBR-IO-001` before any output, CLI flags always winning), `-z`/`--null` NUL-terminated machine output (bare `-z`/`--null` with no explicit format forces porcelain v1 (a config-selected `status.short` counts as a format); conflicts with `--long` and the cache modes; v1/v2 records have no trailing newline, and rename-capable records avoid the human `old -> new` arrow under `-z`; a detected rename is ONE porcelain-v2 `2 XY … R<score> <new>\t<old>` record — HEAD columns describe the origin, index columns the staged destination (an unstaged `.R` copies the index entry into both), `RM`/`RD` ride in the second XY column, and `-z` emits `<new> NUL <old> NUL` with raw bytes), `--find-renames` (full Git raw score grammar on the CLI: bare integer = `0.<digits>`, `N%` literal, decimals; the embedding API keeps the narrower 0–100 percent field), `--renames`/`--no-renames` (rename detection is **on by default** at 50% similarity, matching Git; the three spellings obey true last-one-wins in argv order — a later `--find-renames`/`--renames` re-enables after `--no-renames`; renames are matched by the shared diffcore engine — exact by blob id, then unique basename, then bounded inexact spanhash — on repo-relative keys so detection is correct from any subdirectory; the Libra dirty-cache `--cached`/`--check-dirty` extensions do not run rename detection at all and REJECT every rename flag with `LBR-CLI-002` rather than silently disabling it; the per-side cap comes from `status.renameLimit` (falling back to `diff.renameLimit`) through the same strict cascade — a non-negative integer, `0` disables the cap, default 1000 like Git; exceeding it keeps exact AND unique-basename renames while skipping only the exhaustive inexact stage, with a `rename_limit_product_skipped` warning; unstaged rename destinations are by definition untracked paths and pair only under the config-only Libra extension `status.renameUntracked` — strict boolean, default `false`, so a tracked→untracked move stays `D` + `??` like Git), `--column`/`--no-column` (toggle column layout; `--no-column` countermands `--column`, last wins, and status is not columnar by default so `--no-column` alone is a no-op), and `--ahead-behind`/`--no-ahead-behind` supported; tracked symlinks are compared as symlinks (target bytes/mode changes are dirty, dangling symlinks still exist and are not reported as deleted); Libra dirty-cache extensions (lore.md 1.1, mutually exclusive, also conflict with `--porcelain`/`--short`/`--ignored`): `--scan` (normal full status + atomic cache rebuild, TOCTOU-guarded on the index fingerprint + HEAD, scan lock with stale-steal), `--cached` (consume the snapshot — O(dirty), no worktree walk, no HEAD-tree loads; any freshness doubt degrades to the full status with a hint; snapshot semantics: post-scan worktree-only edits are invisible until rescanned or marked via `libra dirty`; unrelated to Git's `--cached`), `--check-dirty` (re-verify only the cached set, pruning rows proven clean); default status JSON gains no keys — the modes add `mode`/`freshness`/`cache_state`/`cached_paths`(+`checked_paths`/`stale_paths`); io_blocked partial contract (§B.6.0.1): a path status cannot inspect (EACCES/I-O) is neither a deletion nor clean — text formats fail closed with `LBR-IO-001`, `--json` succeeds with `data.io_blocked[]` (`{path:{display,raw_base64},staged,reason,rename}`, raw-byte-sorted, deduplicated) plus `base_scan_complete`/`rename_detection_complete`/`complete`, `is_clean=false`, and `--exit-code` reporting dirty, while a blocked `--scan` never replaces the dirty cache and `--check-dirty` keeps unverifiable rows; structured warnings are one frozen `{code,message,source}` schema (sources `config`/`probe`/`rename_detect`/`worktree`/`metadata`/`cache`; `probe` marks candidates that may never have been SEEN, `rename_detect` candidates seen but unscorable; one warning per `io_blocked[]` entry) delivered on stderr in text modes (even under `--quiet`) and in `data.warnings[]` under `--json`, with exit arbitration fatal ≻ 9 (`--exit-code-on-warning`) ≻ 1 (dirty) ≻ 0 on every output path; non-UTF-8 names never fail status — the base `??` row is kept (RAW bytes under `-z` on Unix, and on non-`-z` surfaces octal-escaped when `core.quotePath` holds (the default), raw when `false`) and only rename candidacy is skipped with `rename_path_encoding_unsupported` (non-UTF-8 rename *scoring* is deferred, i.e. rename detection is partial for such names, not status); argv itself is read as `OsString`, so a non-UTF-8 argument never aborts the process — an overridden `--find-renames=<non-UTF-8>` is simply never interpreted (last-one-wins), the same value as the WINNING occurrence is a `LBR-CLI-002` usage error, and a non-UTF-8 *pathspec* is refused by the parser with that same code (documented narrowing: `StatusArgs.pathspec` stays `String` for source compatibility); upstream ahead/behind counts (short `[ahead N, behind M]`, porcelain v2 `# branch.ab`, the long tracking sentence, JSON `upstream`, and `branch -vv`) come from the painting walk shared with merge-base (`internal::merge_base::ahead_behind` — the same numbers as `rev-list --left-right --count <upstream>...HEAD`, shallow boundaries treated as roots via `ShallowSet`; #486 / issues/474 CL-03); when the counts cannot be computed (a commit in either history cannot be read) or the branch is unborn, no counts are shown — porcelain v2 omits `# branch.ab`, JSON reports `ahead`/`behind` as `null` — and an unreadable history or shallow list adds a structured `upstream_counts_unavailable` warning (source `metadata`; Git dies on a malformed shallow list) with `core.filemode=true` (Unix default) a mode-only worktree change is reported as modified in every output format, while `false` ignores mode-only differences (entry-type changes such as file <-> symlink stay visible) (plan-20260918 FM-04) Git's short `-M[<n>]` rename-threshold spelling is supported alongside `--find-renames[=<n>]`/`--renames`/`--no-renames`, with true last-one-wins argv order and cluster support (`-sM90`); a non-numeric value fails closed with `LBR-CLI-002` (issues/476 WT-02, v0.23.29). Intentional extension (ADR-FM-06, issues/470): the human long format appends a one-shot note when tracked 100755 files lost their execute bit with identical content, with the candidate count and a `libra restore -- <file>...` repair command (`restore -- .` over five files); `--short`/`--porcelain`/`--json` and `core.filemode=false` stay unchanged. Intent-to-add entries (`add -N`) render as Git's unstaged `.A` (` A` v1, `1 .A N...` v2, `new file:` long, `unstaged.new`/`unstaged.intent_to_add` JSON) (issues/476 WT-06). Missing or corrupt nested HEAD trees fail closed with `LBR-REPO-002` / exit 128, the affected tree OID and a `libra fsck`/object-restoration hint; no partial status is printed in human/JSON/machine/porcelain modes. Gitlinks remain leaves without requiring local target commits. Explicit text/eol clean comparison is shared with add/status; representation-only CRLF/LF differences do not create tracked changes or false dirty preflight failures. Attribute changes invalidate prior clean results; mode/type and real edits remain visible. text=auto shares binary detection and historical CRLF index protection with add; see the add reference for renormalize, the 32 MiB local index evidence limit and remaining configuration boundaries. Legacy symlink worktrees read info attributes from their resolved metadata directory; attribute-file symlinks fail closed and repository mutations still require layout migration. |
| clean | partial | `-n` (preview writes no objects and records no operation — ADR-CO-05 #483), `-f`, `-d`, `-x`, `-X`, `-e`/`--exclude`, and `<pathspec>...` positional filtering through the shared pathspec engine (glob, `:(exclude)`, `:(top)`, `:(icase)`, `:(literal)`, `:(glob)`, subdirectory-relative; exclusions only narrow the untracked-only deletion set, `-n` preview == `-f` deletion set) are supported; `-i` is intentionally different/not exposed. **Intentionally different (W1, lore.md 2.4):** files materialized by an active `libra layer` overlay are never removed, under EVERY mode including `-x`/`-X`, and a directory is kept when an overlay file exists anywhere beneath it (`-d` removes whole trees). Overlay content lives outside the repository, so only `libra layer apply` could restore it — `libra layer unapply` is the way to remove those files. Git has no equivalent concept. If the layer store cannot be read, `clean` REFUSES rather than deleting: an unreadable ownership query is indistinguishable from "nothing is protected". |
| stash | partial | `push` / `pop` / `list` / `apply` / `drop` / `show` / `branch` / `clear` supported; `stash show` supports the file-level summary (`--name-only` / `--name-status`) and `-p`/`--patch` (unified diff of the stashed changes, via the shared diff engine). `stash push` supports `-m`, `-u` / `--include-untracked` (with `--no-include-untracked` to countermand it; last one wins, and untracked files are excluded by default so `--no-include-untracked` alone is a no-op), `-a` / `--all`, `-k`/`--keep-index`, and `-- <pathspec>` (stash only the changes to the named paths/dirs — file or directory, `.` selects the whole tree — leaving the rest of the working tree intact; a pathspec matching nothing tracked is `LBR-CLI-003`; `-u`/`-a`/`-k` cannot be combined with a pathspec — `LBR-CLI-002`); included untracked/ignored files are stored in a third stash parent and restored by `apply` / `pop`. `apply` / `pop` perform the three-way restore against the CURRENT working tree (not HEAD), so unrelated uncommitted changes — including the paths a pathspec push left behind — are preserved; by default they leave already-tracked index entries intact (restored tracked changes appear as unstaged working-tree changes) and re-stage paths the stash index added relative to the base commit; `apply` / `pop --index` three-way-merges the stash index onto the current index (`LBR-CONFLICT-001`, `conflicts in index. Try without --index.`, no writes); `stash branch` applies with `--index` semantics (issues/476 WT-11). Worktree posture since W2 (§C.4.3): the stash STACK (`refs/stash` + reflog) is deliberately repository-shared — an entry pushed in one worktree lists/applies in any other — while push/apply/pop snapshot and mutate ONLY the acting worktree's own index/workdir; every stack mutation serializes on a cross-platform stack lock, and pop/`stash branch` delete their applied entry through a single by-id CAS `do_drop` (a concurrent stack change keeps the entry and reports instead of deleting the wrong one or rolling back the successful apply); `stash branch` preflights the branch-name collision and switches HEAD via the fallible API before applying. All stash subcommands run in linked worktrees (the W0 guard is lifted). Deferred: `create` and `store` (see [docs/development/commands/_compatibility.md#d8-stash-create](docs/development/commands/_compatibility.md#d8-stash-create) and [#d9-stash-store](docs/development/commands/_compatibility.md#d9-stash-store)) Remaining unsupported interactive options (`stash -p` / `stash push -p` and `--[no-]auto-advance`) fail with `LBR-UNSUPPORTED-001` (D15); `stash show -p` stays the non-interactive viewer.; `LBR-UNSUPPORTED-001` diagnostic for unsupported interactive options; `stash push -- <pathspec>` matches through the shared pathspec engine (plain names, wildcards, and `:(glob)`/`:(literal)`/`:(icase)`/`:(exclude)`; `.` = whole tree) worktree materialization is mode-aware — files are created with the entry mode's permission bits (`100755` executable, `100644` plain) under the process `umask`, atomically replaced, and the index/tree entries keep the mode (plan issues/470 FM-02). An omitted subcommand is `push` (`libra stash`, `libra stash -u`, `libra stash -- <path>`); an unexpected first token is a usage error with Git's wording (issues/476 WT-08). An omitted subcommand is `push`; the no-initial-commit precheck runs before the change check and is silent under `--quiet` (exit code only, `--json` keeps the envelope) (issues/476 WT-08/WT-09). Default `stash push` messages are `WIP on <branch>: <abbrev7> <subject>` (`<subject>` is the HEAD commit subject after stripping a vault `gpgsig` header and leading blanks); `-m` and rebase/merge autostash names become `On <branch>: <msg>`; detached HEAD uses `(no branch)`; legacy unprefixed reflog messages still list/show/apply (issues/476 WT-10). `stash push` refuses an index containing an intent-to-add entry with 128 / `LBR-CONFLICT-002` and Git's `Entry '<path>' not uptodate. Cannot merge.` (issues/476 WT-07). Explicit text/eol clean comparison is shared with add/status; representation-only CRLF/LF differences do not create tracked changes or false dirty preflight failures. Attribute changes invalidate prior clean results; mode/type and real edits remain visible. text=auto shares binary detection and historical CRLF index protection with add; see the add reference for renormalize, the 32 MiB local index evidence limit and remaining configuration boundaries. |
| lfs | partial | built-in Libra LFS command; reads Git/Libra attributes sources for `filter=lfs` (`core.attributesFile`, per-directory `.gitattributes`, `.libra_attributes`, and the worktree-local `info/attributes` — `.libra/info/attributes`, plus `.git/info/attributes` in Git- or dual-layout trees) while `track`/`untrack` write the root `.libra_attributes`; it still does not implement Git LFS smudge/clean filters/hooks (see [docs/development/commands/_compatibility.md#d5-git-lfs-gitattributes-filter--hooks-bridge](docs/development/commands/_compatibility.md#d5-git-lfs-gitattributes-filter--hooks-bridge)); `lfs.lockEnforce` (lore.md 2.8, Libra extension — git-lfs has no equivalent config): opt-in `off`(default)|`warn`|`block` gate on `add`/`commit` against locks held by OTHERS (server-side ours/theirs split via `POST locks/verify`; holding the lock is permission), covering staged new+modified+DELETED paths (deletions never reach the push-time OID check — this gate is their only guard); zero overhead when unset or no LFS-tracked path is staged; warn prints per-lock stderr warnings + records the warning (exit-code-on-warning contract) and proceeds; block aborts BEFORE any blob/index write on `add` (atomic; after `-a` auto-staging on `commit`, matching pre-commit-hook semantics) with LBR-CONFLICT-002 + hints; matrix: 404=no locking API→no-op (mirrors push), 403→warn proceeds/block fails AuthPermissionDenied, transport/5xx→warn proceeds/block FAILS CLOSED (LBR-NET-001 — an opted-in hard guarantee must not silently degrade; escape hatches named), explicit offline intent→skip+recorded warning BOTH modes (deletion residual documented), detached HEAD→skip+warning, no remote at all→structural no-op, fresh no-upstream branch does NOT skip (falls back to remote.origin.url); invalid value=hard usage error (never silently off); `--dry-run`/`--porcelain` previews never touch the network; push-time verification stays the authoritative backstop (TOCTOU acknowledged) |
| ls-files | partial | default cached listing plus `--cached`/`-c`, `--deleted`/`-d`, `--modified`/`-m` (tracked symlinks are inspected as symlinks, so dangling symlinks are not deleted and target-byte changes are modified), `--stage` / `-s`, `--abbrev[=<n>]` (fixed-length object-name abbreviation in `-s`/`--stage` output; bare = 7), `--others`/`-o`, `--exclude-standard` (standard Git/Libra ignore sources: `.gitignore`, the worktree-local `info/exclude` — `.libra/info/exclude`, plus `.git/info/exclude` in Git- or dual-layout trees — `core.excludesFile`, and `.libraignore`), `-i`/`--ignored` (list only the ignored set — `-i -o` ignored untracked files, `-i -c` tracked files matching an exclude pattern; requires `-o`/`-c` and an exclude source — `--exclude-standard` or an explicit `-x`/`-X` pattern — else exit 128, matching Git), `<pathspec>...` (shared pathspec engine (honors global --literal-pathspecs): plain prefixes, default wildcards, `:(top)`, `:(exclude)`, `:(icase)`, `:(literal)`, `:(glob)`), `--error-unmatch` (unmatched positive pathspec exits 1 with `LBR-CLI-003`), `-z` text output, status tags `-t`/`-v` (H/R/C/?/M/S; skip-worktree entries always tag `S` and are excluded from `-d`/`-m`, matching Git's `if (ce_skip_worktree(ce)) continue;`; `-v` equals `-t` because the lowercase assume-unchanged spelling is unsupported), unmerged-only `-u`/`--unmerged`, `--full-name` (accepted no-op; Libra always prints repo-root-relative paths), explicit exclude sources `-x`/`--exclude <pattern>` and `-X`/`--exclude-from <file>` (gitignore syntax; filter the `--others` listing and count toward the `-i` ignored set), `--eol` (prefix each cached entry with `i/<eol> w/<eol> attr/<attr>` line-ending info — `lf`/`crlf`/`mixed`/`none`/`-text` for the index blob and the worktree file, byte-compatible with `git ls-files --eol`; `attr/` is always empty because line-ending attribute reporting is not implemented), and JSON/machine output supported; pathspecs resolve from the caller's CWD (`:(top)` anchors at repo root) and pathspecs outside the repo are rejected; resolve-undo and sparse-checkout integration are not exposed `ls-files -s` lists intent-to-add entries as the empty blob at stage 0 (issues/476 WT-06). | `--debug` prints Git's cached-stat block (ctime/mtime as `<s>:<ns>`, dev/ino, uid/gid, size/flags) after each cached record; untracked `-o` rows print the path only (issues/490 TC-0046 support).
| log | partial | common Git log surface plus repeatable `--author`/`--committer` (patterns within one identity field OR; fields AND with each other and message filters; unaffected by `--all-match`; case-insensitive substring matching unchanged) and `--no-abbrev` (full object names in human output, including `%h`/`%p`, `--parents`, and in-range `--children`; the last of `--no-abbrev` and `--abbrev=<n>` wins; JSON `short_hash` stays 7 characters) and the named `--pretty`/`--format` presets `oneline`/`short`/`full`/`fuller`/`reference`/`raw` (`medium` is the default with a full commit id) and custom placeholders including `%H`/`%h`, `%P`/`%p`, `%s`/`%f`, `%b`/`%B`, `%n`, ASCII/control `%xNN`, `%%`, `%an`/`%ae`/`%ad`/`%aI`/`%at`, `%cn`/`%ce`/`%cd`/`%cI`/`%ct`, `%d`/`%D`, `%m`, and common `%C...` color placeholders (unknown placeholders remain literal; `%C(always,...)`/`%Creset` follow Git color-policy behavior), plus `-z`/`--null` NUL separators for log records and `--name-only`/`--name-status` path fields, `--range` revision expressions, `--all`, `--reverse`, `--author-date-order` (full-history global author-date sort; does not use the date-priority walk; timestamp-only, no topological constraint; a limit does not stop early), `--date-order` (parity no-op for the default committer-date priority walk, conflicts with `--author-date-order`). Default and `--date-order` walk newest-committer-time first and break same-second ties by ascending discovery order. Eligible `-n`/`--max-count` (with `--skip`) stops after `skip + N` commits and abbreviates hashes from the printed commits (minimum 7); skewed or same-second histories can differ from a global timestamp sort, and a missing ancestor beyond that limited frontier no longer fails the command, `--no-expand-tabs` (accepted no-op; Libra never expands tabs in commit messages), `--notes[=<ref>]`/`--no-notes` (render a `Notes:` block and expand `%N`; optional ref), `--no-mailmap` (accepted no-op; Libra's log never applies a mailmap), `--no-show-signature` (accepted no-op; Libra's log never displays commit signatures inline), `--follow`, `-L`, `--parents`/`--children` (append parent / in-range child ids after each commit hash), `--fixed-strings`/`--extended-regexp`/`--basic-regexp` (grep dialect; BRE is the default), repeatable `--grep` (any pattern may match by default), `--all-match` (every pattern must match; with no patterns it filters nothing), empty grep patterns match every message, `-i`/`--regexp-ignore-case` (case-insensitive across all grep patterns), and `--invert-grep` (keeps commits matching none of the patterns and takes precedence over `--all-match`; human/JSON/machine share this selection, matching subject/body/trailers while excluding a PGP/SSH gpgsig/gpgsig-sha256 header immediately after committer and preserving actual message leading whitespace; other imported header layouts retain existing parsing); all patterns are compiled before filtering, and invalid patterns return `LBR-CLI-002`/129 with the first invalid pattern reported in command-line order; `--patch-with-stat` (diffstat block then patch, Git's synonym for `-p --stat`; an explicit `-p --stat` likewise shows both), and positional revision-range syntax (`log A..B` / `A...B` / `^A` / a bare `<rev>`, followed by optional pathspecs; a bare name that is both a revision and a path is rejected as ambiguous — use `--range`) supported; `format.pretty`/`log.date`/`log.follow` use strict local→global→system defaults with matching CLI overrides (`--no-follow` included), fail-closed invalid/read errors, single-existing-file human+JSON follow while directory filters remain unchanged, and repo-root path normalization; high-bit raw `%x80..%xff` byte emission, `--expand-tabs[=<n>]` tab expansion, `--show-signature` signature display, and exact function-range tracking remain partial; Libra extensions (lore.md 1.9): `--trailer <KEY[=VALUE]>` (repeatable AND filter over the commit's Git-qualifying trailer block — key ASCII case-insensitive, `=VALUE` exact on the unfolded value; Git has no such flag, nearest is a fragile `--grep='^Key: '`) and `--only-trailers` (show only each commit's trailer block, key-filtered when combined with `--trailer`; nearest Git equivalent `--pretty='%(trailers)'`, whose placeholder is not yet implemented); `--json log` gains an additive `trailers: [{key,value}]` field (empty array when none; `body` unchanged); `--` pathspec filtering uses the shared pathspec engine (plain prefixes, wildcards, and `:(top)`/`:(glob)`/`:(literal)`/`:(icase)`/`:(exclude)`; `--literal-pathspecs`/`GIT_LITERAL_PATHSPECS` honored); history walks treat commits listed in `.libra/shallow` as roots (missing parents are not followed; corrupt shallow metadata fails closed as `LBR-REPO-002`). **Positional parse failures (MX-02):** a positional token before `--` that resolves no revision, names no existing path, and carries no pathspec magic/glob syntax fails with `ambiguous argument '<x>': unknown revision or path not in the working tree` (129, `LBR-CLI-002`) instead of silently exiting 0; a genuinely ambiguous short object name fails with the shared `short object ID <p> is ambiguous` candidates diagnostic (129, `LBR-CLI-003`); tokens after `--` are always pathspecs and never errors (`log -- <nonexistent>` exits 0 with no output); repeatable `-s`/`--no-patch` clears only earlier `-p`/`--patch`, `--stat`, `--shortstat`, `--patch-with-stat`, `--name-only`, and `--name-status` options, while later output formats remain effective; a name output option conflicts with active `-s`/`--no-patch`, or both name options remain active after option-order resolution, is rejected (`LBR-CLI-002`, exit 129); human-readable `--decorate`, `%d`, and `%D` decorations are listed in reverse order of their full ref names, with `HEAD` first and shown only on the commit that HEAD points to, while JSON `refs` order remains unchanged (issues/534 LO-05/LO-06); repeatable `--decorate-refs`/`--decorate-refs-exclude` with one complete pattern per value, an implicit refs/ prefix, full-ref-name matching only, wildcard (`*`/`?`/`[`, Git wildmatch) versus exact or component-boundary prefix matching, and excludes winning over includes; commas are literal and short names no longer match, and `HEAD` is filtered by its literal name so `HEAD -> <branch>` appears only when both HEAD and the branch pass (issues/534 LO-07/LO-08) | `--grep` now uses Git's dialect family (POSIX BRE by default, `-E`/`-F`/`--basic-regexp`; `-P` reports engine-not-enabled) and matches only the message body (signature block excluded); multiple patterns OR unless `--all-match`; `grep.patternType`/`grep.extendedRegexp` apply when no dialect flag is given. Built-in `log`/`show` formats now display `core.notesRef` notes by default (`Notes:` / `Notes (<ref>):`, body indented four spaces), with `--notes[=<ref>]`/`--show-notes[=<ref>]`, `--no-notes`, `notes.displayRef`, and `%N`; `--json` gains an optional `notes` array. Default/medium layout matches Git: one blank line between records and none after the last, unpadded day of month in `Date:`, custom/`oneline`/`reference` records separated by a single newline, and `--oneline`/`--pretty`/`--format` last-one-wins.
| service | intentionally-different | Headless local service (lore.md 1.11): `run` (foreground; `--host` restricted to literal loopback IPs at parse AND bind time — no outward TCP port ever; `--port 0` default with the real address published in `.libra/service/service.json`; single instance via lock file; Ctrl-C/SIGTERM graceful shutdown), `status` (pid/URL/health; exit 1 when not running), `events` (SSE tail, NDJSON under `--json`). Notification v1: `{seq,type,at,data}` envelopes, at-most-once (resync event on lag, seq restarts with the service; durable facts live in SQLite). Endpoints: /api/health (loopback), /api/service/events + /api/service/dirty/mark + /api/service/notify (loopback + 0600 token `X-Libra-Service-Token`; 256KiB body cap; marks go through the validated DirtyCache owner API — repo-escaping batches refused). Git has no equivalent (`git daemon` is the network wire protocol — the opposite). Exit 0 / 1 / 128 / 129 |
| shortlog | partial | basic author summary, email, count sorting, time filters, single revision or range (`A..B`/`A...B`/`^A`), `-c`/`--committer` grouping, `--group=author`/`--group=committer`/`--group=trailer:<key>` (group by a commit-message trailer value), `--merges`/`--no-merges` (mutually overriding), `--top`/`--min-count`/`--reverse`, `--author` filtering, and `-w[<width>[,<indent1>[,<indent2>]]]` subject wrapping (defaults 76/6/9; width 0 indents without wrapping), and `--format <format>` (render each commit line with a custom template — the same `%`-placeholder subset as `log --format` — instead of the subject), and stdin pipe input (`git log | libra shortlog`: with no revision and a non-terminal stdin carrying data, summarize the piped `git log`/`libra log` output — `medium`/`fuller` format, grouping/display options only; walk-only filters `--since`/`--until`/`--merges`/`--no-merges`/`--format` ignored as in Git; empty/terminal stdin falls back to the `HEAD` default; still runs inside a repository) supported; `--group=trailer:<key>` now parses via the shared Git-faithful trailer engine (lore.md 1.9) — a single-paragraph message or a non-qualifying final paragraph no longer contributes groups, agreeing with `git shortlog --group=trailer:` (tightened from the old loose last-paragraph scan); history walks treat `.libra/shallow` commits as roots (same helper as `log`; issues/474 CL-03) |
| show | partial | object/commit display, `--name-only`, `--name-status`, `--stat`, `--patch-with-stat` (the diffstat block followed by the full patch — Git's legacy synonym for `-p --stat`; reuses the `--stat` diffstat), `--summary` (condensed create/delete file mode summary, like `diff --summary`; created/deleted files only), `--oneline`, `--pretty` / `--format`, `--abbrev-commit`/`--no-abbrev-commit` (toggle the header hash abbreviation; `--no-abbrev-commit` countermands `--abbrev-commit`, last wins, and the full hash is the default so `--no-abbrev-commit` alone is a no-op), `--no-expand-tabs`/`--no-mailmap`/`--no-show-signature` (accepted no-ops — Libra's show expands no tabs, applies no mailmap, and never displays commit signatures inline), `--notes[=<ref>]`/`--no-notes` (render a `Notes:` block and expand `%N`), and revision peel suffixes (`show HEAD^{tree}`/`^{commit}`, same expressions as `rev-parse`), the named pretty presets `short`/`full`/`fuller`/`reference`/`raw` (rendered distinctly, matching Git's preset structure; `medium` is the default), `--raw` (the raw `:<old-mode> <new-mode> <old-sha> <new-sha> <status>\t<path>` diff format, ids abbreviated to 7), and path filters supported; `format.pretty` and `log.date` provide strict local→global→system human-output defaults, with `--oneline`/`--pretty`/`--format` and the new `--date` winning; JSON is config-immune; the positive `--expand-tabs`/`--notes`/`--mailmap`/`--show-signature` are not separately rendered/exposed; `-- <PATHS>...` filters through the shared pathspec engine (plain prefixes, wildcards, and `:(glob)`/`:(literal)`/`:(icase)`/`:(exclude)`), and `--` with no revision implies `HEAD` | Built-in `log`/`show` formats now display `core.notesRef` notes by default (`Notes:` / `Notes (<ref>):`, body indented four spaces), with `--notes[=<ref>]`/`--show-notes[=<ref>]`, `--no-notes`, `notes.displayRef`, and `%N`; `--json` gains an optional `notes` array. `show --stat` shares the `log --stat`/`diff --stat` renderer (per-file bars plus `N files changed, X insertions(+), Y deletions(-)`). Default header keeps the blank line between `Date:` and the indented message; `--oneline`/`--pretty`/`--format` are last-one-wins.
| show-ref | supported | branch/tag/HEAD listing, `--heads` / `--branches`, `--hash[=<n>]` / `--no-hash`, `--abbrev[=<n>]` / `--no-abbrev`, `--dereference` / `--no-dereference` (annotated tags add `refs/tags/<name>^{}` recursively peeled to their target), `--verify` / `--no-verify`, `--exists` / `--no-exists`, `--head` / `--no-head`, and `--exclude-existing[=<pattern>]` supported |
| for-each-ref | partial | `--heads` / `--tags` / `--remotes` / `--all` / `--format` / `--sort` (`refname`/`objectname`/`version:refname`/`committerdate`/`authordate`/`creatordate` — date keys peel annotated tags to the commit; `creatordate` uses an annotated tag's tagger date; each reversible) / `objectsize` (sort by the ref object's byte size, with the `%(objectsize)` atom) / `*objectname` / `*objecttype` / `*objectsize` (an annotated tag's dereferenced object id / type / byte size, with the matching `%(*…)` atoms; empty deref sorts first) / `--count` / `--points-at` / `--contains` / `--no-contains` / `--merged` / `--no-merged` / `--exclude` / `<pattern>` and `--shell`/`--perl`/`--python`/`--tcl` output quoting modes, and the `%(*objecttype)` / `%(*objectsize)` deref atoms (the dereferenced object's type/byte size for an annotated tag; empty for non-tag refs) and the `%(align:<width>[,<position>])`…`%(end)` alignment block (pads to a column width — left/right/middle; no truncation; nestable) and the `%(if[:equals|:notequals])`…`%(then)`…`%(else)`…`%(end)` conditional block (plain `%(if)` trims whitespace; equals/notequals compare raw; nestable, including inside align), and the commit-graph atoms `%(tree)`/`%(tree:short)`/`%(parent)`/`%(parent:short)`/`%(numparent)`, and date `:<format>` modifiers (`%(committerdate:iso)` etc. — `default`/`short`/`iso`/`iso-strict`/`rfc`/`unix`/`raw`/`relative`) plus the `%(creatordate)` atom supported; `%(color:<spec>)` (ANSI color/attribute escapes, gated on `--color`) supported; `%(raw)`/`%(raw:size)` (the raw decompressed object content and its byte size — `%(raw)` rejected with `--shell`/`--python`/`--tcl` like Git; text objects only, a non-UTF-8 object is rejected rather than lossily transcoded) supported; `%(describe[:opts])` (runs `git describe` per ref — `tags`/`abbrev=<n>`/`match=<glob>`/`exclude=<glob>` options; `%(worktreepath)` (the absolute path of the worktree that has the ref checked out, empty otherwise; resolved across ALL worktrees from each worktree's own scoped HEAD row, so a branch checked out in a linked worktree reports that worktree's path even when the command runs elsewhere — a branch checked out nowhere, or a detached worktree, is empty) supported; the remaining niche atom `%(deltabase)` and the `local`/`human`/`format:<strftime>` date modifiers are not exposed; known limitation: when the repository holds a nested tag (a tag of a tag), the `--contains` / `--no-contains` filters fail with `LBR-REPO-002` and `--merged` / `--no-merged` / `--points-at` mis-list that tag, because these filters peel only one tag level (#498 DEFER-TT-08) |
| ls-remote | partial | heads/tags/refs filtering, patterns, `--get-url`, `--sort=refname`/`version:refname`, `--exit-code`, and `--symref` supported; `--symref` prefers advertised capabilities and falls back to resolving HEAD from advertised branch tips for transports such as local Libra |
| ls-tree | partial | Commit/tree listing, recursive listing, current-directory-relative path prefix filters, `--full-name`, `--full-tree`, `REV:path` tree-ish syntax, JSON, common output flags, and partial `--format` atom support (including `%(objectsize)` and `%(objectsize:padded)`) are supported; full Git pathspec magic remains incomplete Without -r or -d, a single directory path without a trailing slash lists the directory entry itself; with a trailing slash it lists the directory contents. |
| symbolic-ref | partial | Supports local `HEAD` only; other symbolic refs are rejected because Libra stores refs in SQLite. `-d`/`--delete` is accepted for the git t1401 contract and NEVER deletes anything: `-d HEAD` exits 128 with `deleting 'HEAD' is not allowed` and any other name exits 128 with `Cannot delete <name>, not a symbolic ref` (the NOTHEAD create-then-delete form is an intentional difference, ADR-PL-08) |

defaults subject = current branch, base = its upstream (none → error with hints); `--merge-base` = three-dot; curated `--stat`/`--name-only`/`--name-status`/`--exit-code` + `-- <paths>`; `--json` emits the diff schema; `branch <flags> diff` is a REFUSED reserved verb (never silently creates a branch named `diff`; escape hatch `libra switch -c diff`); `branch reset <branch> <target>` (lore.md 1.13, Libra extension porting Lore's branch reset): moves a LOCAL branch tip through the authoritative SQLite txn (reference + branch reflog entry; index/worktree untouched — pinned by test); the FIRST enforcement consumer of protect/archive metadata — both flags re-checked fail-closed INSIDE the txn (garbage values count as protected; LBR-POLICY-001; no `--force` — lift via `metadata unset`, auditable); the checked-out branch is refused (in-txn re-checked) with a `libra reset` hint; identical re-runs within the operation-log 5s dedup window are refused; `reset` joins `diff` as a reserved verb (escape hatch `switch -c reset`); `branch.sort` config default honored (strict cascade, `--sort` wins; config neither implies `--list` nor suppresses the unborn-HEAD line; a non-branch start-point is refused with Git wording but exit **129** (`LBR-CLI-003`; Git 128 — intentional, ADR-HF-02) and creates nothing; `--track` is ignored with `-d`/`-m`/`--list` or when used alone; default list order is refname (current branch is not promoted); `-v`/`-vv` pad the name column to the longest displayed name, including the detached first line `* (HEAD detached at <abbrev7>)`; `-a` prefixes remote-tracking names as `remotes/<remote>/<branch>`; `branch -d` refusals (not fully merged, missing branch, currently checked out) exit **1** (`LBR-REPO-003` / `LBR-CLI-003` unchanged; `LIBRA_FINE_EXIT_CODES=1` does not override); a local branch as upstream (`remote=.`); invalid `-u` targets exit 129 (Git: 128); `-t/--track[=direct|inherit]` and `--no-track` on create; `--track <commit>` exits 129 (Git: 128); listed in refname order with `-v` columns aligned; other refusals keep their documented 128/129 exit codes; `branch -d` refusals exit 1; `-a`/`-r` list a remote HEAD symlink line (`[remotes/]<remote>/HEAD -> <remote>/<branch>`) when the remote HEAD symref is cached (issues/474 CL-15) | (issues/526: `-f`/`--force` resets an existing branch to the start point only when it is not checked out in any worktree and protect/archive metadata permits the reset; `--create-reflog` selects the default transactional reflog write for `branch <name>` and `branch -f`; forced resets report `reset` with old/new tips in human/JSON output; `--no-sort` clears the command-line keys and the configured `branch.sort`, with `--sort` winning when it comes later.)
| bundle | partial | `create <file> [<rev>...]` accepts explicit revisions plus `--all`/`--branches`/`--tags`, writes a bounded full Git v2 bundle through a private synced temp file, advertises a `HEAD` line for `--all` and an explicit `HEAD` revision (issues/474 CL-08), preserves annotated tag-object heads, and packs their reachable closure with the repository hash kind. `verify` checks header, prerequisites, PACK v2, and the complete trailer checksum; `list-heads` reads advertised heads; `unbundle` validates and installs a SHA-1 (idx v1) or SHA-256/blake3 (idx v2) pack+index pair; cross-kind bundles fail closed with no residual writes, verifies an already-installed pair on repeat, prints heads, and intentionally does not update refs (matching Git). System Git can clone the result. Exit 0 / 1 (`verify`/`list-heads` invalid/unreadable/missing prerequisite) / 128 (create/unbundle/repository/IO); input/output cap 1 GiB. `libra clone` accepts a Git v2 bundle after ruling out a repository directory (`<path>.bundle`, then `<path>`); the default destination drops `.bundle`; `HEAD` / default-branch / no-branch selection matches Git; `remote.origin.url` is the bundle path; `--depth` on a plain-path bundle is ignored with the local-clone warning (issues/474 CL-09). Prerequisite/thin/incremental create and exhaustive verify entry decoding are deferred |
| tag | partial | lightweight tags, message-based annotated tags (via `-m`/`-F`), an explicit `<commit>` target for creation (`libra tag <name> <commit>`: any commit-ish, recorded unpeeled like Git — naming an annotated tag points the new tag at that tag object, so an annotated or signed tag becomes a nested tag and a lightweight tag another name for that tag object, while `<tag>^{}` tags its commit; a target that resolves to a tree or blob is refused, which Git allows, because Libra cannot transfer such tags yet; an unresolvable or non-commit target and `<commit>` misuse exit 129 (`LBR-CLI-003` / `LBR-CLI-002`) where Git exits 128, an unborn HEAD or an unreadable / corrupt target exit 128 (`LBR-REPO-003` / `LBR-IO-001` / `LBR-REPO-002`), and Git's nested-tag advice is not printed), `-F`/`--file` (annotated message from a file or stdin), force, delete, list, `-n`, `--points-at <object>`, `--contains`/`--no-contains`, `--merged`/`--no-merged` (these five filters peel each tag through its whole tag chain — a tag of a tag matches by its final commit — and drop tags whose chain ends at a tree or blob, as Git's ref-filter does for `--contains`/`--no-contains`/`--merged`/`--no-merged`; `--points-at` peels its argument to a commit and compares it with each tag's final commit, whereas Git compares the unpeeled argument with every object in the chain, so tag / tree / blob arguments differ — tracked with #533; a broken chain or tag cycle fails closed with `LBR-REPO-002` and an unreadable chain with `LBR-IO-001`, where Git's reachability filters silently skip such tags and its `--points-at` may stop with a malformed-object error), `--sort`, `--column[=<options>]` (comma/space-separated `always`/`auto`/`never` + `column`/`row`/`plain` (fill order; `plain` = one column) + `dense`/`nodense` (column widths); column-major + nodense by default, laid out by terminal display width, byte-compatible with `git tag --column`; `--no-column` countermands it — equivalent to `--column=never`, last one wins, and tags list one-per-line by default so `--no-column` alone is a no-op), vault-PGP `-s`/`--sign` (with `--no-sign` to countermand it; last one wins, and tags are unsigned by default so `--no-sign` alone is a no-op), `-v`/`--verify`, and `-e`/`--edit` (compose or edit the annotated-tag message in an editor; comments stripped, an empty result aborts), and `-a/--annotate` creates an annotated tag (alone opens the editor; with `-m`/`-F`/`-e` it is annotated; combined with `-d`/`-l`/`-v` it is a usage error) supported; `-u` and Git GPG interoperability are not exposed; `tag.sort` config default honored (strict cascade, `--sort` wins; a configured sort never turns creation into a listing; unset lists `refname`-ascending matching Git — previously insertion order; invalid value fails closed `LBR-CLI-002` / unreadable store `LBR-IO-001` before output; repeated values apply only the last of the winning scope — Git stacks them) | Revocation and expiry are evaluated at the **signature's own creation time** (verification accepts any certificate in the repository allowlist: active key, generated fallback, archived `vault.gpg.history.<FPR>.pubkey` entries).
| commit | partial | common Git commit surface plus `--date` (author date; overrides `GIT_AUTHOR_DATE`), `GIT_AUTHOR_*` / `GIT_COMMITTER_*` identity and date env overrides (Git env wins over config unless `user.useConfigOnly=true`; existing `LIBRA_COMMITTER_*` remains a lower-priority fallback), `--cleanup`, `--dry-run` (no message required; skips hook/editor, rerere, and post-commit automation side effects), `--fixup`, `--squash`, `-C/-c` (reuse message AND author metadata), `--trailer`, `--reset-author`, `-e/--edit` (open the editor even with `-m/-F/-C`; bare non-dry-run `commit` opens it too), `-v/--verbose` (staged diff in the editor template, stripped at the scissors line so it never enters the message; dry-run prints it directly), `--porcelain` (machine-readable status v1 preview of the would-be-committed state; like Git it implies `--dry-run` and does not create the commit; inert under `--json`), and `commit.status` / `--status` / `--no-status` (status is included by default; only an applicable editor/stripping-cleanup path reads the strict local→global→system Git boolean, with the last explicit CLI toggle overriding/bypassing it and invalid/unreadable config failing before auto-stage; `-m`, dry-run/porcelain, JSON, and non-stripping cleanup bypass the key; status is seeded only when an editor opens and cleanup strips comments, so `verbatim`/`whitespace`/`scissors` never leak it; `--dry-run -a` uses an isolated task-local index and persists no temporary auto-stage blob/LFS/tree object, so the live index is never replaced; verbose preview budgets changed HEAD/already-staged/auto-staged diff blobs before loading (32 MiB per blob, 64 MiB charged total, 4,096 objects) and repository scratch is capped at 256 MiB across runs; changed blobs without a bounded constant-memory local preflight (including remote-only or pack-without-index) are refused before loading and previews never rebuild pack indexes; real LFS auto-stage atomically replaces stale backups and honors `--sync-data` staging/destination durability, including write-through replacement on Windows), and the `commit.cleanup`/`commit.verbose` config keys (the default cleanup mode / verbose flag when the CLI flag is unset; an explicit `--cleanup`/`-v` overrides the config; config cascade local→global; an invalid value is fatal) supported; `commit.verbose` is on/off only (a `bool-or-int` value enables verbose when non-zero, but Libra's `-v` has no verbosity level — `commit.verbose=2` behaves like `true`, with no `-vv`/unstaged-diff rendering — and there is no `--no-verbose` to force verbose off for a single commit); `-t/--template` (use FILE as the initial message — seeds the editor, or used directly with `--no-edit`; falls back to the `commit.template` config; ignored when a message source is given; an unedited template aborts the commit), and `--no-gpg-sign` (force an unsigned commit — skips Libra's vault GPG signing for this commit; vault signing runs when `vault.signing=true` (the init default) and a vault unseal key is available, so this is a no-op only when signing would not have happened anyway) supported; clean `--amend --no-edit` rewrites `HEAD` and refreshes the committer date even when tree/parents/author/message are unchanged, so it never prints a successful amend summary for an unchanged ref; `-S`/`--gpg-sign[=<keyid>]` (force-sign with the repository vault key; optional key id ignored; last explicit signing flag wins) and `--allow-empty-message` (bypasses empty-message and unedited-template checks; does not imply `--allow-empty`); trailer-writer fixes (lore.md 1.9): `-s` combined with `--trailer` now forms ONE Git-parseable trailer block (previously two paragraphs), `--trailer` always separates from the body with a blank line, and `--cleanup=strip`/`default` now collapses consecutive blank lines instead of deleting every interior blank (Git-faithful — multi-paragraph messages and user-typed trailer blocks survive) | A successful non-dry-run commit that updates HEAD also clears a stopped single-commit cherry-pick or revert, and marks a remaining multi-commit sequence as concluded. During an in-progress merge, a regular commit records HEAD plus the merge targets as parents, prefills the saved merge message (`-m`/`-F` override; `--no-edit` keeps `# Conflicts:` comments), then clears merge state and applies a held autostash. `--amend` during a merge is refused (128). `--dry-run` previews without writing or printing a fake commit hash. After `merge --squash`, commit is single-parent, prefills `SQUASH_MSG`, and deletes that file. Partial `commit <path>` / `-o` is recognised and refused with `LBR-UNSUPPORTED-001` (exit 128). An empty staged tree classifies the working tree like Git: clean (`nothing to commit, working tree clean`), untracked-only (`nothing added to commit but untracked files present`), or unstaged tracked (`no changes added to commit`); `-s` does not change the refusal. Deleting the LAST tracked file is not an empty staged tree — the staged deletion must be committed, matching Git (`git rm <last-file> && git commit`), and is never reported as `nothing to commit` (issue #497); only a genuinely empty repository (no committed tree and no staged changes relative to `HEAD`) reports `nothing to commit (create/copy files and use 'libra add' to track)`. A comment-only `-t` template reports `aborting commit due to empty commit message`; an unedited non-empty template still reports `you did not edit the message`. Exit 128 / `LBR-REPO-003` is unchanged. Remaining unsupported interactive options (`-p`/`--patch`/`--interactive`) fail with `LBR-UNSUPPORTED-001` (D15). concludes an in-progress merge with a two-parent commit `LBR-UNSUPPORTED-001` diagnostic for unsupported interactive options `--allow-empty-message` is supported `-a` stages mode-only worktree changes when `core.filemode=true` (Unix default) and ignores them when `false` (plan-20260918 FM-04) Intent-to-add entries are excluded from the staged set and from every written tree; an only-ITA commit refuses with `no changes added to commit`, while `commit -a` stages the real content and clears the flag (issues/476 WT-07). Explicit text/eol clean comparison is shared with add/status; representation-only CRLF/LF differences do not create tracked changes or false dirty preflight failures. Attribute changes invalidate prior clean results; mode/type and real edits remain visible. text=auto shares binary detection and historical CRLF index protection with add; see the add reference for renormalize, the 32 MiB local index evidence limit and remaining configuration boundaries. | (issues/525: `-F -` reads the message from standard input; `-S`/`--gpg-sign[=<keyid>]` forces signing even if config disables it and fails without a vault key, with the last explicit signing flag winning; `--short` prints a dry-run status preview without committing; `-u[=<mode>]` validates and applies `no`/`normal`/`all` to status previews; partial commit (`-o`/`--only`, `--pathspec-from-file`, `commit <path>`) is recognised and refused with `LBR-UNSUPPORTED-001` instead of an unknown-option error.)

| rebase | partial | Replayed commits keep the original author and stamp the running user as committer (a fold — `fixup`/`squash`/`amend` — keeps the target commit's author, matching Git); a missing `user.name`/`user.email` fails closed with the same identity error as `commit` rather than writing a placeholder. `--onto <newbase> [<upstream>] [<branch>]`, `--root [--onto <newbase>]` (optional positional is `<branch>`; without `--onto` the root stays parentless and unchanged picks keep their hashes), `--autosquash` / `--no-autosquash` (explicit `--autosquash` skips the already-up-to-date shortcut; last one wins; `rebase.autosquash` does not affect non-interactive rebase), explicit `--reapply-cherry-picks`, and non-interactive P1-07 controls are supported. `--autostash` / `--no-autostash` preserve tracked index/worktree changes in an fsynced held object across success, conflict, continue, skip, and abort, restoring the staged index and unstaged worktree as separate three-way layers; a restore conflict promotes the object to the normal stash list. Repeatable `--exec <cmd>` runs after each replayed commit under a required workspace-write, network-denied sandbox; non-zero/timeout stops with `LBR-CONFLICT-002`, `--continue` retries, and `--skip` keeps the applied commit while skipping its remaining exec commands. `--update-refs` / `--no-update-refs` atomically retarget other local branches in the rewritten range using captured-tip checks, including autosquash/empty/skip mappings, while excluding branches checked out in any worktree. `--fork-point` / `--no-fork-point` select the most specific upstream-reflog tip still ancestral to HEAD, with merge-base fallback. Toggle pairs are last-wins. `--rerere-autoupdate` / `--no-rerere-autoupdate` override rerere staging for the sequence, are last-wins, and persist through `--continue`; omission inherits `rerere.autoUpdate`, and both are no-ops with rerere disabled. `--keep-empty` (default no-op), `--no-keep-empty`, and `--empty=<drop|keep>` remain supported, with Libra intentionally defaulting become-empty commits to `keep`. `-i/--interactive` and `--edit-todo` are supported (`pick`/`reword`/`edit`/`squash`/`fixup`/`exec`/`break`/`drop`, plus `--autosquash`/`rebase.autosquash`, `--root`, `--exec`, and `--autostash`; `-i --update-refs` is a usage error); `--rebase-merges` and `--empty=stop|ask` are not supported; `rebase <upstream>` and `pull --rebase` on unrelated histories replay the local history from its root onto the upstream instead of refusing with `no common ancestor found` (issues/480 HP-12). lore.md 2.6 sequencer mutex remains symmetric across operations. | merge | partial | fast-forward, single-head three-way, and multi-head octopus merge supported; before starting any merge (including an already-up-to-date target or `--dry-run`), an unresolved index without merge state is refused with `LBR-CONFLICT-002`, preserving HEAD/index/worktree and directing resolution + `add` + ordinary `commit`; existing merge state retains continue/abort guidance. This includes criss-cross histories (MG-02): when a divergence leaves SEVERAL merge bases they are folded pairwise, in ascending hex order, into one recursive virtual ancestor that the final three-way merge uses as its base — Git's recursive strategy, so a conflict Git resolves through the ancestor is no longer reported. Inside the fold `-X ours/theirs` is disabled (as in Git), a content conflict is recorded as content with the markers widened by two per recursion level, a change/delete keeps the base version, binary content (Git's NUL-in-the-first-8000 rule) keeps the base's content or the empty blob when there is none, and symlinks/mixed entry kinds keep the base entry (absent when there is none); nesting deeper than 20 levels, or more than 32 bases at one level (checked on the bare ids before any base is loaded, and on the candidates collected while folding), is refused with `LBR-UNSUPPORTED-001` instead of recursing (the fold costs one merge-base walk per already-folded base per step, so width is bounded as well as depth; `-s resolve`, `-s ours`, and `--ff-only` never fold and are never refused for width — a diverged `--ff-only` merge is refused as non-fast-forward as always). Binary content is Git's `ll_xdl_merge` rule: a NUL in the first 8000 bytes OR an input larger than `MAX_XDIFF_SIZE` (1023 MiB). `merge.conflictStyle` is resolved BEFORE a multi-base merge runs (the ancestor's content depends on it), so an invalid value stops a criss-cross merge that would otherwise be clean. The synthetic commit's parents are ALL the real bases folded so far (Git chains one two-parent virtual commit per step — same reachability, different object ids). The virtual ancestor's tree and synthetic commit are ONE-SHOT objects (ADR-MG-04): deliberately absent from `merge-state.json` and therefore not GC roots, so `maintenance gc` may reclaim them and `merge --restart` recomputes them; the merge state records a base only when it is a real single commit. MG-12 accepts two or more targets as an octopus merge: duplicate/reachable heads are reduced while independent parents retain command-line order; non-up-to-date octopus merges never fast-forward; the entry gate requires one common history across HEAD and every target unless unrelated histories are explicitly allowed; each target is merged against the hypothetical merge of the already-incorporated heads (not their all-tip intersection), including every criss-cross LCA, and all trees stay in memory before publication; any conflict fails atomically with LBR-CONFLICT-002 and `Should not be doing an octopus`, leaving HEAD/index/worktree and merge state unchanged; clean `--no-commit` persists every target as a GC root for ordered multi-parent `--continue` or `--abort`; `--dry-run`, autostash, hooks, `-s ours`, `-X`, unrelated histories, shortlogs, and per-tip signature verification apply (trusted unsandboxed external-driver mutations remain outside Libra's rollback boundary). normalized results retain ours-side line endings), `--allow-unrelated-histories` (virtual empty base persisted safely across non-squash conflict restart/continue), and `--log[=<n>]`/`--no-log` (CLI override of `merge.log`, explicit `-m --log` supported, non-squash resolved message persisted across continue); `-m <msg>`, `--ff-only`, `--no-ff`, `--squash` (including on conflict: HEAD stays unchanged and no merge state is recorded; resolve and stage conflicts, then plain `libra commit` creates one parent; `--continue`/`--abort`/`--restart` report `no merge in progress`), `--no-commit` (retains merge state, including conflicts, and finishes with a two-parent `merge --continue`), `--no-edit` (accepted no-op; Both guarded modes preload target objects, reject tracked/untracked overwrite and file/directory collisions, validate index/tree paths against escape or `.libra` metadata access, never follow worktree symlink ancestors (including ignored links), snapshot the exact index bytes plus affected worktree entries through bounded object references, and restore that snapshot if a worktree or final ref/reflog update fails. Network Git (`git://`), HTTP(S), and SSH fetches without `--depth` inherit advertised source shallow boundaries when the boundary commit is present and truly lacks a parent; wanted objects and fetched parent links are validated against the final boundaries before refs move. An unreadable global config store, after the repository scope has no type, keeps the historical local object backend so existing objects can still be read. A future global schema while reading the type keeps the historical local fetch. Unsupported type values such as `gcs` fail the same way. `LIBRA_STORAGE_TYPE=local` still warns and stays local for `ClientStorage::init`; fetch, pull, and clone already reject that exact value. In text output the unreadable-global-store degrade and the `local` (and empty-bucket) warnings print a `Warning:` line; under `--json`/`--machine` they never reach stderr (they join the warning buffer, and `--exit-code-on-warning` exits 9). |
