the weakest whitespace flag, subsumed by `-w`/`-b`/`--ignore-space-at-eol`; approximation vs Git: compares with ALL trailing CRs stripped rather than Git's non-transitive allow-one-remaining-CR rule, so only pathological multi-CR endings differ)/`--ignore-blank-lines` (ignore changes whose lines are all empty — a blank-only change drops out; a blank within `<ctxlen` of a real change rides along; faithful port of Git's `xdl_get_hunk` blank-aware hunk selection)/`--check` (warn on added-line trailing-whitespace / space-before-tab / leftover conflict marker / new blank line at EOF, exit 2)/`-R` (`--reverse`; swap the two sides)/`-a` (`--text`; force the content diff of files detected as binary — a NUL byte in either side, or non-UTF-8 content — suppressing the "Binary files … differ" line; Libra's diff is text-based, so a non-UTF-8 change identical after lossy-UTF-8 conversion still shows the marker)/`--binary` (emit a `GIT binary patch` — full-index header + base85 `literal` chunks for both directions — for binary files; valid and appliable, but the compressed bytes are not byte-identical to Git's, since Libra deflates with `flate2` and always emits `literal` rather than Git's smaller-of-literal/delta; binary files otherwise show `Binary files … differ`, `--stat` shows `Bin <old> -> <new> bytes`, and `--numstat` shows `-`/`-`)/`--no-ext-diff` (disable the external diff driver for this run, forcing the built-in engine)/`--color-moved[=<mode>]` (color moved lines — deleted in one place, added in another — distinctly in colored output: removed → bold magenta, added → bold cyan; bare `--color-moved` and the block modes `default`/`zebra`/`blocks`/`dimmed-zebra` are accepted but approximated by `plain`, coloring every moved line, since Git's conservative moved-block significance/zebra striping is not byte-reproducible; only affects colored output, and `--color=always` now forces diff color even when piped)/`--no-color-moved` (turn it off — the default, countermands an earlier `--color-moved`)/`--relative[=<path>]` (restrict the diff to a directory and strip that prefix from displayed paths; bare `--relative` uses the cwd)/`--no-relative` (a no-op on its own, but takes precedence over `--relative` when both are given)/`--no-indent-heuristic` (accepted no-op — Libra's diff applies no indent heuristic)/`--textconv` (run textconv filters, on by default like Git: a file whose `diff=<driver>` attribute from Git/Libra attributes sources (`core.attributesFile`, per-directory `.gitattributes`, `.libra_attributes`, and the worktree-local `info/attributes` — `.libra/info/attributes`, plus `.git/info/attributes` in Git- or dual-layout trees) names a driver with a configured `diff.<driver>.textconv` command has each side converted by that command before diffing — stat/numstat/name/JSON all reflect the converted content; matched via the shared attributes matcher, last-match-wins, with `-diff`/`!diff`/bare `diff` clearing an earlier driver, and a rename resolving each side's driver independently; a failing textconv command is a fatal error (`LBR-IO-001`, like Git's "unable to read files to diff") rather than a silent raw fall-back; skipped under `--check` and when `diff.external` is active)/`--no-textconv` (diff raw content; countermands `--textconv`)/`-M[<n>]` / `--find-renames[=<n>]` (rename detection — a deleted + added pair similar enough is folded into one rename with `similarity index N%` / `rename from`/`to`, and `R<score>` / brace-compacted `old => new` paths across name-status/numstat/stat/summary; the similarity index matches Git for real content (chunked like Git's rename spanhash but hashed with FNV-1a rather than Git's `HASHBASE`, so only contrived hash-collision inputs can differ); bare `-M` is 50%, a bare integer is read as `0.<digits>` like Git so `-M5`=50%/`-M100`=10%, `-M<n>%` is a literal percent and `-M100%` is exact-only (integer threshold math, no float rounding), invalid scores are a usage error; enabled at 50% by default (matching Git), honoring `diff.renames` through the strict cascade — truthy or unset enables 50%, false disables detection, and `copies` degrades to renames; `diff.context` accepts Git integers and sets the default `-U` width; the rename `index` line uses mode `100644`, rename pairing is score-ranked greedy with a same-basename tie-break (Git's diffcore-rename runs a same-basename pre-pass that can prefer a lower-scored same-basename pair, so the chosen old/new pairs for a multi-rename set can differ), and a pathspec cannot directly follow a bare `-M`/`--find-renames` — put it before the flag or after `--`)/`--no-renames` (turn rename detection off, overriding the default, `diff.renames`, and an earlier `-M`) and `--ext-diff` / `diff.external` (route each file's patch through an external diff driver via Git's GIT_EXTERNAL_DIFF protocol — `cmd path old-file old-hex old-mode new-file new-hex new-mode`, run through the shell; a working-tree new side reports an all-zero hash; `--no-ext-diff` disables it and `--stat`/name/numstat/`-s`/`--check` bypass it) and `--word-diff[=plain|color|porcelain|none]` (re-render the patch at word granularity — `plain` brackets `[-removed-]`/`{+added+}`, `color` highlights in a terminal, `porcelain` is the machine format, `none` disables; whitespace-delimited words; matches Git's structure but, like all Libra diffs, the exact token grouping can differ on ambiguous cases and the `@@` headers use Libra's unified-diff format) supported; the working-tree scan liveness hint (`Scanning working tree ...`, stderr) is deferred until the scan has run longer than 2s (fast local scans print nothing, unlike Git which shows no diff progress at all), only guards the unstaged worktree comparison (never `--staged`/rev-vs-rev/`--quiet`/`--json`/`--machine`), TTY-gated under `--progress=auto` so redirected stderr/CI logs never receive it, erased on completion on a TTY (an explicit `--progress=text` with redirected stderr keeps the bare line text but receives no ANSI escapes), and replaced by immediate `diff_scan.start` NDJSON events under `--progress=json` (#466); positional revisions follow Git's `diff [<revision>...] [--] [<path>...]` grammar — `diff A` (A vs worktree), `diff A B` (≡ `A..B`), two-dot `A..B` and three-dot `A...B` glued ranges (`A...B` diffs from the merge base of `A` and `B` to `B`; unrelated histories error), `diff --staged <commit>` (commit vs index; a range or second revision with `--staged` is rejected), a `--` separator forcing the path reading, and Git's two disambiguation errors (`ambiguous argument '<tok>': both a revision and a filename`; `unknown revision or path not in the working tree`, glob pathspecs exempt) — these exit 129 (`LBR-CLI-002`/`LBR-CLI-003`, Libra's CLI-error convention) where Git exits 128; more than two revisions (Git ≥2.38's merge combined-diff form) is declined; when `--old`/`--new` is given positionals stay pathspecs (documented Libra-only leniency); `--color-words[=<regex>]` is exposed as the redirected-output-aware `--word-diff=color` shorthand with an optional Rust-regex tokenizer (explicit global `--color=never` wins); `--word-diff-regex=<regex>` defines non-overlapping comparison words and implies plain mode when standalone, while the indent heuristic (`--indent-heuristic`) is not exposed; `diff.renameLimit` (per-side cap on the quadratic inexact pass, strict cascade, `0` = uncapped, default 1000 like Git — the cheap stages run BEFORE the gate, so exceeding it keeps exact AND scored unique-basename renames, skips only the exhaustive stage, and warns) and the Libra-only `diff.renameComparisonBudget` (total similarity-comparison budget charged per comparison across the unique-basename and exhaustive stages, `0` = unlimited default — exhaustion stops all further comparisons and discards the exhaustive pass; pairs already scored (exact + already-paired unique-basename) survive, with a warning) bound rename detection with fail-closed validation (`LBR-CLI-002`) with `core.filemode=true` (Unix default) a mode-only worktree change renders the mode header and a `--summary` `mode change` line, while `false` does not diff mode-only differences (plan-20260918 FM-04) Intent-to-add entries diff as new files in the worktree views and are omitted from `--cached` (issues/476 WT-06). | (issues/524: `--dirstat-by-file[=<param>]` is supported as the by-file form of `--dirstat`; `--stat-name-width`, `--inter-hunk-context`, `-I/--ignore-matching-lines` and `-B/--break-rewrites` are recognised and refused with `LBR-UNSUPPORTED-001` naming the missing feature instead of an unknown-option error.; the global `--color[=<when>]` accepts a bare `--color` (= always) and never swallows the subcommand; `--cc` and `-W/--function-context` are likewise classified with `LBR-UNSUPPORTED-001`.) All patch surfaces emit Git-canonical hunk headers (omit `,1`, empty sides anchored at the preceding line, `-0,0` for new files) and only truly deleted files render `deleted file mode` + `+++ /dev/null`; `apply`/`am` accept canonical and legacy explicit-count headers.
| diff-tree | partial | `diff-tree <tree-a> <tree-b> [-- <path>...]` diffs two trees by delegating to the shared engine (`diff --old a --new b --no-renames`); as Git plumbing it does not inherit porcelain `diff.renames` or default rename detection. Default output is raw, like Git; `-p`/`--patch` switches to a patch, plus `--raw`, `--name-only`, `--name-status`, `--exit-code`, `--quiet`, `-s`/`--no-patch`, `-r` (accepted no-op; always recurses), and `-c`/`--cc` (accepted; combined rendering is a documented narrowing). ADR-PL-09 exit contract: 0 by default even with differences; `--exit-code`/`--quiet` turn differences into exit 1 (`--quiet` prints nothing); 128 outside a repository; 129 (`LBR-CLI-002`/`LBR-CLI-003`) for invalid arguments or an unresolved name. Path limiters require a `--` separator (stricter than Git's bare paths). Single-commit `diff-tree <commit>` (vs parent), `-t`/`--stdin`, and the remaining porcelain flag surface are not exposed |
| diff-index | partial | Explicit `text`/`eol` converts worktree content for `-p`; raw/name output and `--quiet`/`--exit-code` remain stat-based (an empty clean-equivalent patch can exit 1). Stored index/tree bytes are unchanged; reads do not refresh index or rewrite files, and patch content/attribute failures are fatal. Legacy symlink worktrees use resolved metadata for info attributes during content comparison; attribute-file symlinks are rejected. `text=auto` uses binary detection and historical CRLF index protection, with bounded local evidence reads (see add); stored objects stay raw. `diff-index <tree> [-- <path>...]` diffs a tree against the working tree via the shared engine (`diff --old <tree> --no-renames`); as Git plumbing it ignores porcelain `diff.renames`. `--cached` compares against the index instead. Default output is raw; `-p`/`--patch`, `--raw`, `--name-only`, `--name-status`, and `-s`/`--no-patch` are supported. ADR-PL-09 exit contract: 0 by default even with differences; `--exit-code`/`--quiet` turn differences into exit 1 (`--quiet` prints nothing); 128 outside a repository; 129 (`LBR-CLI-002`/`LBR-CLI-003`) for invalid arguments or an unresolved name; the worktree side is stat-based like git — a stat-dirty entry reports `M` with an all-zero worktree sha without hashing (issues/479 DF-01, TC-0044); the status letter follows ENTRY PRESENCE, never content emptiness, so an empty tracked file whose stat was invalidated by `read-tree` is `M` (not `A`) and a worktree copy truncated to zero bytes is `M` (not `D`). Path limiters require `--`. `-m` and the remaining porcelain flag surface are not exposed |
| diff-files | partial | Explicit `text`/`eol` converts worktree content for `-p`; raw/name output and `--quiet`/`--exit-code` remain stat-based (an empty clean-equivalent patch can exit 1). Stored index/tree bytes are unchanged; reads do not refresh index or rewrite files, and patch content/attribute failures are fatal. Legacy symlink worktrees use resolved metadata for info attributes during content comparison; attribute-file symlinks are rejected. `text=auto` uses binary detection and historical CRLF index protection, with bounded local evidence reads (see add); stored objects stay raw. `diff-files [-- <path>...]` shows the index-vs-working-tree diff via the shared engine (`diff --no-renames`); as Git plumbing it ignores porcelain `diff.renames`. Default output is raw; `-p`/`--patch`, `--raw`, `--name-only`, `--name-status` (intent-to-add rows report `A` — issues/476 WT-06), and `-s`/`--no-patch`, `--exit-code`, and `--quiet` are supported. ADR-PL-09 exit contract: 0 by default even with differences; `--exit-code`/`--quiet` turn differences into exit 1 (`--quiet` prints nothing); 128 outside a repository; 129 (`LBR-CLI-002`/`LBR-CLI-003`) for invalid arguments or an unresolved name. The status letter follows entry presence: an empty tracked blob whose stat `read-tree` invalidated is `M` (not `A`), and a worktree truncated to empty is `M` (not `D`), reported with an all-zero worktree sha and no content hash (issues/479 DF-01, TC-0044); path limiters require `--`. Stage selection (`-1`/`-2`/`-3`) and the remaining porcelain flag surface are not exposed |
| fast-export | partial | Read-only `git fast-import` stream export for default HEAD, multiple revisions, `A..B`/`^A`, or `--all`; all refs share marks, annotated commit tags retain tag records/marks, Libra notes become N records, and paths use Git C quoting. Commits use `deleteall` + full M trees (larger than Git parent diffs); excluded parents remain literal prerequisites. Note targets outside the exported graph fail closed instead of disappearing. Exit 0/128. Symmetric ranges, marks files, anonymize/filtering, and annotated non-commit targets are deferred; commit signing headers cannot be represented and are omitted |
| fast-import | partial | Imports counted/here-doc streams from stdin, a positional `<file>`, or `--input`; `--dry-run` parses and reports without updating refs (objects are still written). Commands: blob; blob; commit mark/author/committer/data/from/merge/M/D/C/R/N/deleteall (M/N inline); annotated tag; reset update/delete; checkpoint/done; and lenient feature/option/progress. Quoted UTF-8 paths and mode↔object types are validated. Branch/tag/notes changes buffer and publish atomically per flush; Git tree-shaped notes commits translate to Libra notes rows; unsupported ref namespaces fail closed. Bounds: 1 GiB total (`fastimport.maxInputSize`, invalid/unreadable/zero config fails), 1 MiB command line, 1,000,000 top-level objects by default, hash-kind IDs, unique marks, readable typed targets. Exit 0/128. cat-blob/ls/get-mark, marks files, non-UTF-8 paths, and true multi-GiB payload streaming are deferred |
| grep | partial | tracked/index/tree search with common match/count/list/line flags, shared pathspec magic for repository searches (honors global --literal-pathspecs) (plain prefixes, default wildcards, `:(top)`, `:(exclude)`, `:(icase)`, `:(literal)`, `:(glob)`), Git grep exit contract (0 match / 1 no match / 2 command error), `-A`/`-B`/`-C` context, `-E`/`-G` regex aliases, explicit `-P` rejection (exit 2), `-a`/`-I` binary controls, `--heading`/`--break`/`-z` output grouping, `-m`/`--max-count`, `-o`/`--only-matching`, `--column` (1-based byte column of the first match), `--and`/`--or`/`--not` boolean combinators (line-level; last and/or wins), `--recurse-submodules`/`--no-recurse-submodules` (accepted no-op), `--untracked` (also search untracked, non-ignored working-tree files; `--no-exclude-standard` additionally includes ignored ones), `--no-index` (recursively grep the filesystem without a repository, including ignored files by default; `--exclude-standard` applies standard ignore rules — inside a repository the worktree root is the decision root, outside a repository each traversal root is its own decision root reading only ignore files under it plus `core.excludesFile`; keeps filesystem path roots, not repository pathspec magic), `--[no-]exclude-standard` (only valid with `--no-index`/`--untracked`; using either for tracked contents is a usage error with Git's message, exit 2), repository-mode searches with no positive pathspec scope to the current directory and display paths relative to it with `--full-name` restoring worktree-root-relative paths (JSON `path` stays worktree-root-relative; `:/` restores whole-tree search; root behavior unchanged), and `--max-depth <DEPTH>` (descend at most DEPTH directory levels below each pathspec — or below the current directory / search root with no pathspec; negative means no limit) supported; function display is not exposed |
| blame | partial | file blame with `-L` ranges (numeric `N`/`START,END`/`START,+COUNT` plus `/regex/` start/end endpoints; a single endpoint spans to end-of-file, matching Git), ignore-rev inputs, `--porcelain`/`-p`, `--line-porcelain`, `-e`/`--show-email`, the display flags `-l` (full hash), `-s` (suppress author/date), `-t` (raw timestamp), `-f`/`--show-name` (show the filename after the hash), `--abbrev <n>`, `--no-abbrev`, `--root` (accepted no-op — Libra never prefixes boundary/root commits with `^`), `-w`/`--ignore-whitespace` (ignore-all-whitespace line attribution), `-M`/`-C` (one-step parent-tree scan for moved/copied lines — a documented narrowing of Git's recursive detection; `-C` repeatable), `--date=<mode>`, and `--incremental` (porcelain shape with per-line headers) supported; `-L :<funcname>`, reverse, and complete porcelain boundary/previous metadata are not exposed; history walks treat `.libra/shallow` commits as roots (corrupt shallow metadata fails closed as `LBR-REPO-002`; issues/474 CL-03) |
| revert | partial | single/multi-commit revert, generated commits use current author/committer metadata (including `GIT_AUTHOR_*`/`GIT_COMMITTER_*` date/identity overrides) and derive `Revert "<subject>"` from the target commit's de-signed message body, `-n/--no-commit`, `-m/--mainline` merge-commit revert, `-s/--signoff`, `-e/--edit` (open the editor — `$GIT_EDITOR`/`core.editor`/`$VISUAL`/`$EDITOR` — on the generated revert message; unlike Git, Libra's revert does not open an editor by default, so `--edit` is opt-in and mutually exclusive with `--no-edit`; carried through a conflict via `--continue`), `--no-edit` (accepted no-op — the default), `--cleanup=<mode>` (`strip`/`whitespace`/`verbatim`/`scissors`/`default`, persisted across conflict/continue), repeatable last-wins `-X/--strategy-option ours|theirs` (favor only conflicting hunks while retaining clean changes; persisted across conflict/continue/skip), `--no-rerere-autoupdate` (accepted no-op — rerere exists as a standalone command but is not yet auto-integrated), shared `merge.conflictStyle=merge|diff3|zdiff3` text rendering (default refined merge style; unknown values fail before index/worktree writes only when a content conflict needs rendering, while clean merges ignore the irrelevant presentation setting; uniformly CRLF inputs produce CRLF markers), conflict `--continue`/`--abort`/`--skip`, and multi-commit auto-continuation (a conflict stores the pending commits in `RevertState.remaining`; `--continue`/`--skip` drain the rest) supported; positive `--rerere-autoupdate` and custom merge strategies remain incomplete. lore.md 2.6: participates in the symmetric cross-op sequencer mutex — starting a `revert` is refused (`LBR-CONFLICT-002`) while any OTHER sequence is unresolved (and vice-versa). A new revert refuses to start on an unmerged index (exit 128, `LBR-CONFLICT-001`, as in Git) before any write. As in Git, a later reset that clears unresolved index stages ends a stopped single-commit revert; a multi-commit sequence keeps its remaining commits with the stopped commit marked concluded. Reset preserves ambiguous common-storage revert state and warns with `libra worktree doctor`; it does not infer ownership from a reset. `revert --abort` restores the pre-revert state, discarding later tracked changes; `--skip` applies the remaining commits on the reset target. | A later commit ends a stopped single-commit revert the same way. `--continue` skips a stop concluded outside the sequence. Conflict markers use Git-form labels: `<<<<<<< HEAD` / `>>>>>>> parent of <abbrev7> (<subject>)`; under `diff3` the ancestor is `<abbrev7> (<subject>)`. a later reset ends a stopped single-commit revert a later commit ends a stopped single-commit revert --continue skips a stop concluded outside the sequence conflict markers label the reverted side as parent of <abbrev> (<subject>) worktree materialization is mode-aware — files are created with the entry mode's permission bits (`100755` executable, `100644` plain) under the process `umask`, atomically replaced, and the index/tree entries keep the mode (plan issues/470 FM-02). |
| replace | partial | `replace [-f] <object> <replacement>` records an object substitution, `-d <object>...` deletes it, and `-l [<pattern>]` (the default) lists replaced ids. The peel is applied in `load_object`, so `log` / `show` / `rev-parse` peeling transparently honour it (not just one call site); types must match unless `-f`, an existing replacement needs `-f`, self-replacement is rejected. Stored as loose refs under `.libra/refs/replace/<oid>`. Exit 0 / 128 (no repo, invalid object, missing replacement, type mismatch or existing replacement without `-f`, IO). `-l` prints object ids only (Git's default short format) and filters by substring rather than glob. Listing through `show-ref`/`for-each-ref`, `--format`, `--edit`, `--graft`, and `--convert-graft-file` are deferred | (issues/481 MX-14/MX-15: `-l --format=short|medium|long`, `--graft <commit> [<parent>...]`, `--edit` (graft alias), `--convert-graft-file`, and the `Deleted replace ref '<oid>'` line on `-d`.)
| rerere | partial | Records conflict resolutions and replays them on the matching normalized conflict. `rerere` (no subcommand) records preimages / replays known resolutions / records postimages for tracked files that have been resolved; `status`, `remaining` (lists tracked conflicts still unresolved in the index/worktree), `diff`, `forget <path>...`, `clear`, `gc` (60-day resolved / 15-day unresolved TTL) supported. Storage: the reusable resolution cache under `.libra/rerere/<id>/{preimage,postimage}` is repository-shared, while `MERGE_RR` (the currently-tracked conflicts) is per-worktree in the local gitdir since W2 (§C.4.3 — one worktree's clear/auto-update never touches another's tracking; a legacy shared `MERGE_RR` is read/migrated only by a single-worktree main and left untouched as ambiguous once linked worktrees exist). `<id>` is SHA-256 of each complete hunk's lexicographically ordered sides with diff3 base omitted; no-marker files retain a whole-file hash fallback, and old whole-file cache entries coexist without migration. Replay uses the normalized preimage, current normalized conflict, and recorded postimage in a three-way merge; a non-clean replay leaves the worktree unchanged. Exit 0 / 128. Automatic integration with merge/rebase/cherry-pick is implemented and gated on `rerere.enabled` (default off → those commands are byte-for-byte unchanged): a conflict auto-records the preimage and replays a known resolution, and resolving + committing / `--continue` auto-records the postimage. `rerere.autoUpdate` (or an effective `--rerere-autoupdate` on any of those commands) additionally stages a replayed file |
| remote | partial | `add`/`remove`/`rename`/`-v`/`show`/`get-url`/`set-url`/`prune`, `set-branches [--add]`, `set-head`, `remote update`, and `add -f` are supported. No-argument `remote update` resolves non-empty `remotes.default` first, expanding both names and `remotes.<group>` members; only an empty default falls back to every configured remote. `update -p` fetches every resolved remote before destination-aware `remote prune`, so a later fetch error cannot strand an earlier destructive prune. Rename transactionally migrates exact remote/SSH config sections, refspec destinations (not sources), branch upstreams, tracking refs, remote HEAD, and tracking reflogs; a repository-scoped `remote.pushDefault` and any `branch.<b>.pushRemote` naming the old remote are rewritten, while a global/system-scoped `remote.pushDefault` naming it is left unchanged and warned about (Git parity); any target namespace conflict rolls back every change. `remote show` is live by default and `--no-query` is offline/cached. Cold `add` supports `-t`, `-m`, `--tags`/`--no-tags`, and `--mirror[=fetch|push]`: without `-t` it writes the default `+refs/heads/*:refs/remotes/<name>/*` fetch refspec (Git parity); `--mirror=fetch` writes `+refs/*:refs/*`, `--mirror=push` writes only the `remote.<name>.mirror=true` marker, and bare `--mirror` writes both plus a deprecation warning — `-m` with any mirror and `-t` with a push mirror are rejected (exit 128), an unknown mirror mode is a usage error (exit 129). prune contacts the remote and fail-closes on object-format mismatch with `LBR-REPO-003` / exit 128 (blake3-extension hint when either side is blake3); wire kind comes from `object-format` capability, not OID length (intentionally-different removal of length fallback). |
| hash-object | partial | Hashing for files, `--stdin`, and `--stdin-paths` (hash each newline-separated path from stdin); `-t blob/commit/tree/tag` typed hashing whose object id matches Git byte-for-byte, with `--literally` to skip content validation; `-w` writes the object; `--path` and `--no-filters` are accepted for raw-byte hashing. Path filters/attributes and arbitrary `--literally` type strings are unsupported; blake3 repositories produce 64-hex Blake3 blob OIDs (`hash-object -w` round-trips via `cat-file`). Malformed typed content and unsupported type strings exit 129 (`LBR-CLI-002`), intentionally different from Git's 128 |
| write-tree | partial | Writes the index out as a nested tree object (one tree per directory, modes preserved, hash kind followed) and prints the root tree id; an empty index yields the canonical empty tree; `--json`/`--machine` supported. Before writing, stage-0 index entries for regular/executable/symlink blobs and tree-mode entries are validated for object existence/type and fail closed with `LBR-REPO-002`; gitlinks (`160000`) are not validated because they reference submodule commits outside this object DB. `--missing-ok` tolerates absent blob objects (Git parity; mistyped/unreadable objects and missing subtrees still fail closed, and the valve never reaches the commit/merge/cherry-pick paths); `--prefix` not exposed (deferred); `--index-file <path>` (lore.md 1.15, Libra flag standing in for Git's GIT_INDEX_FILE env) redirects the index read/write to a scratch file — a missing scratch file acts as an empty index — so revision composition never touches the shared `.libra/index` Intent-to-add entries are skipped (never tree content) (issues/476 WT-07). |
| read-tree | partial | Reads a `<tree-ish>` (tree id, commit/ref/tag/`HEAD`, including shared typed-peel syntax such as `HEAD^{tree}`) into the index, replacing it; index-only (the working tree is never touched), `--json`/`--machine` supported. Git's `-m`/`-u`/`--reset`/`--prefix` and multi-tree merges are not exposed (deferred); `--index-file <path>` (lore.md 1.15, Libra flag standing in for Git's GIT_INDEX_FILE env) redirects the index read/write to a scratch file — a missing scratch file acts as an empty index — so revision composition never touches the shared `.libra/index` `-m`/`--merge` merges the tree into the current index instead of replacing it (tree entries are added/updated, index entries the tree does not mention are kept, and replaced entries keep their v3 extended flags); `-u`, `--reset`, `--prefix`, and multi-tree merges remain unexposed (plan issues/490 SW-04). |
| update-index | partial | Modifies the index directly: `--add`/`--remove` (re)stage or drop working-tree paths (`--add` records symlinks as mode `120000` with link target bytes and does not follow the target), and `--cacheinfo <mode>,<object>,<path>` registers an entry from an object id without reading the working tree (object need not exist at registration time; mode ∈ 100644/100755/120000/160000; oid length must match the hash format; absolute/`..` paths rejected). Later `write-tree` / `commit` validate blob/tree object existence and type and fail with `LBR-REPO-002` if a cacheinfo entry remains unresolved; `--json`/`--machine` supported. `--force-remove` drops the positional paths regardless of whether the working-tree file exists and removes every stage of an unmerged path (unknown paths are a no-op; wins over `--add`/`--remove`); `--skip-worktree`/`--no-skip-worktree` set/clear the skip-worktree bit on tracked stage-0 entries without reading the working tree (marks win over `--force-remove`; untracked paths fail with `Unable to mark file`/128; a marked entry short-circuits the add/remove handling — an explicit `--remove` drops the entry, anything else is a no-op — matching git's `process_path`; marking rewrites the index as format v3 and clearing returns it to v2; downgrade note in `docs/commands/update-index.md`); `--refresh`/`--really-refresh` re-stat the index against the working tree (stat-only drift is refreshed silently; a modified or missing file prints `<path>: needs update` and exits 1 — `-q` suppresses the line and the exit code, `--ignore-missing` skips missing files, `--unmerged` skips unmerged entries, which otherwise print `<path>: needs merge`; `--really-refresh` equals `--refresh` because Libra has no assume-unchanged bit; unlike Git, both clear a leftover assume-unchanged `CE_VALID` bit written by releases before issues/637); `--chmod=(+|-)x` flips the executable bit on the positional/`--stdin` paths that follow it until overridden (positional git t2107 matrix; non-regular or untracked-without-`--add` paths fail 128); `--info-only` records object ids without storing blobs through the staging path; `--verbose` prints `add '<path>'`/`remove '<path>'`/`chmod (+|-)x '<path>'` action lines; `--index-info`/`--stdin` read records from stdin (`-z` for NUL) and must be the last argument — `--index-info` accepts `<mode> SP <oid> TAB <path>`, `<mode> SP <type> SP <oid> TAB <path>`, or `<mode> SP <oid> SP <stage> TAB <path>` (mode `0` removes every stage; a stage-0 write removes higher stages while stage-1/2/3 writes keep stage 0, matching git; a malformed line exits 128 with the index byte-identical), `--stdin` processes one path per record like the positional paths; `--assume-unchanged` not exposed (deferred); `--index-file <path>` (lore.md 1.15, Libra flag standing in for Git's GIT_INDEX_FILE env) redirects the index read/write to a scratch file — a missing scratch file acts as an empty index — so revision composition never touches the shared `.libra/index` |
| update-ref | partial | Updates/creates/deletes a `refs/heads/<branch>` ref with an optional compare-and-swap (`<oldvalue>`; the all-zero id means "must not exist"), `-d` delete, `-m <reason>` reflog message, and `--json`/`--machine`. The ref read, write/delete, and `update-ref` reflog entry run in one SQLite transaction (the `<oldvalue>` operand is never written to the reflog). Scoped to `refs/heads/*`: `HEAD`, `refs/tags/*`, `refs/remotes/*`, arbitrary namespaces, and symbolic (`ref:`) values are rejected/deferred (use `symbolic-ref`/`switch`/`tag`); `--stdin [-z]` (issues/479 UR-01) accepts git's batch protocol — `update`/`create`/`delete`/`verify`/`option no-deref`/`start`/`prepare`/`commit`/`abort` — where `-z` separates the command's **arguments** with NUL (`create <ref> NUL <newvalue> NUL`, `update <ref> NUL <newvalue> NUL <oldvalue> NUL`), so a value may contain spaces without quoting (`:/fix bug`) and `update` requires the `<oldvalue>` field; applies every update in ONE SQLite transaction with per-ref reflog entries (an `-m` reason applies to the whole batch), prints `<cmd>: ok` lines as the commands are processed, refuses multiple updates of one ref (128) and out-of-scope refs/`symref-*` commands (129/`LBR-CLI-003`) with the whole batch untouched, and an `abort` applies nothing; `--no-deref` and `option no-deref` are accepted no-ops (no symbolic refs exist in scope);  protect/archive branch metadata is now enforced INSIDE its txn for refs/heads/* updates AND deletes (LBR-POLICY-001, fail-closed — update-ref is no longer a policy bypass; it remains plumbing-sharp otherwise and may still move the checked-out branch, like git update-ref). The `<newvalue>` operand accepts any revision expression the shared resolver understands (branch, tag, `HEAD`, `~`/`^` navigation, abbreviated id, `<rev>^{commit}`) with **no implicit peel**: what the expression names must itself be a commit, so a lightweight tag is accepted and a bare annotated tag is refused naming the resolved type (`LBR-CLI-003`) — the same rule Git applies. `<oldvalue>` goes through the same resolver but is **not** type-checked — it asserts what the ref points at now, so the resolved id is compared verbatim and naming an annotated tag there is an ordinary CAS mismatch, as in Git. Branch protect/archive metadata is still read inside the same transaction on every spelling, and a metadata read that fails still refuses the write (`LBR-REPO-003`) |
| open | supported | |
| commit-tree | partial | Plumbing (lore.md 1.15): wraps tree+parents+message into a commit object with ZERO index/worktree/HEAD/ref side effects; `-p` repeatable (duplicates warn+ignored, parents must load as commits), `-m` repeatable paragraphs + `-F`/`-F -`/bare-piped-stdin (`-m`+`-F` combine in group order — argv interleaving not preserved); tree operand uses the shared strict tree-ish resolver, including commit/tag/ref and typed peel such as `HEAD^{tree}` (Libra superset). Intentionally different: empty messages refused (D-empty-message), always unsigned in v1 (git honors commit.gpgsign), no date-override envs yet (non-reproducible OIDs, recorded follow-up), TTY-without-message is a usage error. Exit 0 / 128 / 129 |
| config | partial | vault-backed local/global config is supported; GPG signing keys are generated or imported as a Libra-only extension (`generate-gpg-key` versioned vault key, `import-gpg-key [--list|--key|--file|--passphrase-file|--replace]`, `export-gpg-key [--fingerprint|--out]`, `remove-gpg-key --force`; imported secret keys are stored encrypted and redacted on every read path, `--reveal` is refused); section operations `--remove-section <name>` and `--rename-section <old> <new>` (transactional; use Git's section/subsection identity, so `--remove-section branch` deletes `branch.<key>` but not the `branch.feature.*` subsection; rename preserves each value's encryption flag and refuses an existing destination section; missing section exits 128, identical rename exits 2) are supported; `-z`/`--null` NUL-delimited output (`value\0` for get/get-all, `key\nvalue\0` for `--get-regexp`/`--list`, `key\0` with `--name-only`, `origin\0` prefix with `--show-origin`; rejected with the Libra-only `--ssh-keys`/`--gpg-keys`/`--vault` views) is supported; type canonicalization `--type=<bool|int|path>` and the `--bool`/`--int`/`--path` shortcuts (bool variants → true/false, int k/m/g 1024-based multipliers, path `~`/`~/` expansion) applies both when reading (get modes) and when setting (the value is validated/canonicalized before storage, matching `git config --type`: `yes` → `true`, `1k` → `1024`; an invalid value errors without storing); a non-get/non-set mode is rejected (exit 129) is supported; the `--system` scope (`/etc/libra/config.db`, overridable via `LIBRA_CONFIG_SYSTEM_DB`; lowest cascade precedence; vault-encrypted secrets rejected in this scope) is supported; explicit `--system`/`--global` reads match Git on store errors (#475 CF-07, ADR-CF-06): a missing store makes `--get*` exit 1 silently (or print `--default`) and `--list` exit 128 `fatal: unable to read config file '<path>': No such file or directory` (`LBR-IO-001`), while an unreadable store prints `warning: unable to access '<path>': Permission denied` and keeps `--get*` at exit 1 (or default) with `--list` exit 128 `fatal: unable to access ...`; cascaded reads keep skipping missing global/system stores and unreadable/unsupported system stores; the `upgrade.*` namespace is intentionally-different (reserved, Libra-only: stored in `{LIBRA_HOME}/upgrade/settings.json`, only single-value `set`/`get`/`unset` with `--global` are accepted, `unset` resets `upgrade.mode` to `off` keeping the file, other scopes/multivalue/type/section/conflicting-action spellings and `--get-regexp` patterns matching `upgrade.mode` fail closed as usage errors (`LBR-CLI-002`; a corrupt or unreadable settings file is `LBR-UPGRADE-001`), and stale SQLite `upgrade.*` rows are suppressed from `list` and non-matching `--get-regexp`); editor round-trip and includeIf are incomplete; the per-worktree config overlay exists and is written only by `worktree add`/migrate probe — the public `config --worktree` write surface is deferred (plan-20260714 DEFER-09), and overlay values tighten but never loosen the repository scope; bare `libra config <key>` reads the stored value like `git config <key>` (last value wins for a multi-valued key; unset exits 1 with `LBR-CLI-002`; an encrypted value renders `<REDACTED>` exactly as `config get` does), except that **intentionally-different: bare read of a protected key keeps the interactive assignment path** — for keys `is_sensitive_key` classifies as protected, `libra config <key>` still prompts for a new value instead of readingand reports the protected-key error with exit 2 when no terminal is available; the global scope stores its SQLite database at `<XDG_CONFIG_HOME or ~/.config>/libra/config.db` (defaulting to `<home>/.config/libra/config.db` on every platform, macOS included) and keeps the legacy `<home>/.libra/config.db` as the active file while it is the only store present, so reads and writes never split across two databases; the first command that reads or writes global configuration migrates that legacy database to the XDG path exactly once (directory lock, read-only `VACUUM INTO` snapshot, `integrity_check` plus receipt/row-count verification, same-directory `rename`), leaving the legacy file byte-identical as a downgrade backup — a migration that cannot run keeps reads on the legacy file with a warning and fails writes closed with `LBR-IO-002`, and `LIBRA_CONFIG_GLOBAL_DB` disables the migration entirely; `config path --json` and `doctor --global-schema` report `path_source` as `LIBRA_CONFIG_GLOBAL_DB`, `xdg`, `home` or `legacy`, plus `legacy_path`/`legacy_exists`/`migration_pending` (additive; `report_version` stays 1), and are diagnostics that never migrate; the global vault unseal key follows the same directory (`<config dir>/libra/vault-unseal-key`, 0600 inside a 0700 directory) with the same copy-once-and-keep-the-backup rule, is never rotated (two differently-valued key files fail closed, and an unreadable or malformed key is an error rather than a reason to generate one), while per-repository key material (`~/.libra/vault-keys/<repo-id>`) and the vault temp directory (`~/.libra/tmp`) stay in the Libra home `core.filemode` is read case-insensitively (Unix default `true`): with `false`, staging keeps an existing entry's mode and records new paths as `100644`, while `--chmod`/`--cacheinfo` stay explicit; an invalid value fails `add`/`status` closed before any write; with `core.filemode=true` (Unix default) a mode-only worktree change (owner-execute bit differs, content unchanged) is reported by `status`, rendered by `diff`, staged by `add`/`commit -a`/`update-index <path>`, and stashed by `stash push`, while `false` ignores mode-only differences and entry-type changes (regular file ↔ symlink) stay visible (plan issues/470 FM-03, FM-04).  Local-scope `core.objectformat` mutations (`set`/`--add`/`--unset`/`--unset-all`/`--remove-section core`/`--rename-section` involving `core`/`import` carrying the key, including case variants) refuse with `LBR-CLI-002`; the key remains writable only via init/reinit. Global/system rows for that key are not consumed by repository commands. Ordinary encrypted `config set` replacements stay encrypted (new ciphertext) for exact and ASCII case spellings; `--plaintext` on an existing encrypted value refuses with `LBR-REPO-003`/128 without modifying rows. Explicitly `unset-all` the ordinary key using its stored spelling/scope before resetting it as plaintext; this does not repair older damaged values. Issue #475 CLI-surface parity: `--get-regexp` takes a regexp operand (dotless patterns such as `^user` work; no match exits 1; formatted as `key value` in insertion and scope order), `--name-only` outputs only key names for `--list` and `--get-regexp` (`name\0` with `-z`), when scope is omitted `--list` and `config list` list all scopes in system → global → local order (each scope in insertion order, file-backed `upgrade.mode` following global entries) while an explicit scope restricts output to that scope, `--get`/`--unset` accept Git's value-pattern operand (regexp over stored values; a patterned unset with no match exits 5), `--replace-all` is supported, a single-value overwrite of a multi-valued key refuses with Git's exit 5 and the `--add`/`--replace-all` guidance, `--show-origin` prefixes `file:<database path>` (Libra stores config in SQLite, not INI) and `--show-scope` prefixes the scope name for both reads and `--list`, `--file <path>` reads and writes a Git-style INI file (reads: get/get-all/get-regexp/list; writes: set/--add/value-pattern/--replace-all/--unset/--unset-all/--remove-section/--rename-section with Git placement and quoting, atomic `<path>.lock` + rename preserving permissions; stdin and vault options are refused), and `--type` additionally accepts `bool-or-str`/`bool-or-int` (with k/m/g int suffixes) `color` (renders Git's ANSI escape sequence on read, validates and stores the original spelling on write, and `--list --type=color` keeps only parseable values), and `expiry-date` (renders a Unix timestamp on read from relative/special-word/absolute forms, validates on write, stores the original spelling, and `--list --type=expiry-date` keeps only parseable values). Key/pattern errors are CLI-class (no more LBR-INTERNAL-001). Still declined: `--blob`, `--type=color`/`expiry-date`, and text-editing `edit` (SQLite storage; the refusal now points at `config set`). |
| credential | partial | Vault-backed Git credential helper: `get`/`store`/`erase` speak the Git credential key/value protocol on stdin/stdout (`fill` is a legacy alias for `get`; an unknown operation is silently ignored-ish, exit 0 — Git helper convention), storing secrets AES-256-GCM-encrypted in the repo config keyed by a SHA-256 digest of protocol/host/path (no clear-text host/username at rest). `fill` is side-channel free (hit and miss both exit 0; miss prints nothing) and works outside a repository; `store`/`fill`/`erase` also work outside a repository via a user-level (global config) encrypted store (issues/480 HP-14), so the helper is usable as a global `credential.helper`; entries carry an expiry (`password_expiry_utc`, default 30 days) and expired entries are a miss; `store` rejects an already-expired timestamp. Secrets are never logged/traced/echoed in errors. Exit 0 / 128 (`store` missing fields, expired timestamp, or no vault). `credential-cache`, multiple usernames per host, and the consumer-side `credential.helper` chain are not exposed (Libra *is* a helper); `fill` now falls back to the global `libra auth` token store (https only, silent misses, username pinning) — `store`/`erase` never manage auth tokens |
| op | intentionally-different | Libra command-level Operation v2 history, restore, undo/redo/revert, and multi-worktree reconcile extension, not a Git command. Sequencer control actions appear in `op log`; `op restore` refuses them with `LBR-CONFLICT-002` because their index, worktree, and sequencer state cannot be replayed by a refs-only restore. `op restore` also refuses a `running` operation, including a claim left by a crashed command. Undo a control action with that command's own `--abort`. The v2 runtime is the sole operation fact source; the former v1 wrapper/service/table namespace is retired by migration `2026091801`, and the feature branch does not include a version release. Read-only query forms do not record operations — see `docs/commands/op.md`. That includes branch list / `--show-current` / list filters and an already-configured idempotent `branch -u`; tag list (including `--no-column <pattern>`) and `--verify`; `remote -v` / `show` (live query and `--no-query`) / `get-url` / `prune --dry-run`; reflog show / exists / `expire --dry-run`; and notes list / show / `get-ref` / `prune --dry-run`.Agent hook callbacks (`libra hooks <provider> <event>` and the hidden `libra agent hooks` alias) are dispatched before the operation boundary and the global configuration schema policy, so they are never recorded in `op log` and are never an `op undo` / `op restore` target; their capture state lives in the capture catalog and on `refs/libra/traces`. Operation views still snapshot that ref, but `op undo` / `op redo` / `op revert` / `op restore` keep the Libra-owned capture and history branches (`traces`, legacy `agent-traces`, `intent`, `libra/intent`) at their current values instead of rewinding them, and the `op restore --dry-run` preview lists them as kept. |
| reflog | supported | `show`/`delete`/`exists`/`expire` subcommands; bare `reflog` is `show HEAD` and accepts the same flags (`-n`/`-<digits>`, `--grep`, `--since`/`--until`, `--author`, `--date`, `-p`, `--stat`, `--no-abbrev`), plus a positional ref. `expire` prunes by time + reachability + `--stale-fix` (`--all`/`--expire`/`--expire-unreachable`/`--rewrite`/`--updateref`/`-n`/`-v`), reads `gc.reflogExpire`/`gc.reflogExpireUnreachable` (90/30-day defaults, never written). Intentional differences: no-ref expire is an explicit error (exit 128) vs Git's silent no-op; `--stale-fix` checks only that the new value loads as a commit (no transitive object walk); `--updateref` skips symbolic `HEAD` / remote-tracking refs Preview (`--dry-run`/`-n`) writes no objects and records no operation (ADR-CO-05 #483). |
| worktree | intentionally-different | The named CLI is `add <NAME> [TARGET] [--path <PATH>]`: the default is the common storage root `.libra/worktree/linked/<NAME>`, independent of caller cwd or linked scope; explicit `--path` remains a complete external destination relative to the caller. Names match `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9._-]{0,63}`, excluding trailing dots and Windows device names. Names are shared across ordinary/FUSE/detached/tombstoned entries and resolve to stored paths after a move. There is no automatic relocation or name backfill for existing data. `lock`, `unlock` and `remove` select `<NAME>` or `--path <PATH>`; `move <NAME> --path <DEST>` keeps the name and identity, and `move --from-path <SRC> --path <DEST>` selects an unnamed source. `repair [<NAME>]` or `repair --path <PATH>` selects one entry; no selector keeps global repair. `doctor` still selects a workspace id and `umount` an actual path. `remove` keeps disk dir by default (no implicit data loss); since W3-s1b (§C.7) the keep-dir form DETACHES instead of dropping: the entry moves to `detached_from_registry`, the worktree's scoped DB rows (HEAD/reflog/sequencer/dirty/layer/sparse) are PRESERVED — deleting them would leave a directory that still operates but lost its HEAD — and a gitdir marker fail-closes every command inside the directory (actionable re-add/delete hint) until `worktree add <NAME>` re-attaches it without checkout (identity and common-storage pointer checked; `TARGET`, `-b` and `--detach` refused; optional `--path` must match the stored path) or `--delete-dir` finishes the removal. The name remains reserved through detached and tombstone states and is released only after directory and scoped-state cleanup complete. `--delete-dir` gives Git-style removal, refuses on a dirty worktree, deletes + fsyncs the parent BEFORE cleaning scoped rows, and on a cleanup failure keeps a `tombstone` entry that `worktree repair` retries. Both remove modes refuse while the worktree has an in-progress rebase/cherry-pick/bisect. `prune` only handles entries whose path stat says NotFound (a permission error never classifies a worktree as missing), skips tombstones (repair's job) and scopes with active sequencer state. add/move/remove/prune record a durable intent journal row before mutating (SQLite cannot join a filesystem rename into one transaction — a crash is rolled forward/back by `worktree repair`, which also retries tombstones and reconciles detached markers; recovery never deletes directories). Every MUTATING repair action (no-arg, `<path>`, non-dry-run `--migrate-layout`) requires `--confirm` — without it the command is refused with `LBR-CONFLICT-002` before any registry lock, database write or filesystem touch — and records exactly one operation-log audit row per executed action (`libra op log`); `--migrate-layout --dry-run` stays read-only and confirmation-free, and `repair --path <PATH> --resolve-identity` keeps its dedicated `--yes` The down path of migration 2026072402 refuses while any detached/tombstone/journal state or linked-scope sequencer/rebase/bisect state exists; live workspace leases block via migration 2026072501's own down, which every deeper rollback passes through first. lore.md 2.1: linked worktrees now have ISOLATED HEAD + index + HEAD-reflog — each has a real `.libra/` (a `commondir` pointer to the shared db/objects/hooks + a stable `worktree_id`) and its own `index`; a commit/switch/reset in one worktree never moves another's HEAD or touches its index. The canonical add surface is `worktree add <NAME> [TARGET] [--path <PATH>]` / `--detach` / `-b <new> [<start>]` (W3-s2 §C.7). WITHOUT a target the new worktree is created DETACHED at the source commit — intentionally different from Git, which creates and checks out a basename-named branch (influenced by `worktree.guessRemote`). A branch target checks the branch out ATTACHED, refused before any side effect when ANY scope (including the invoking worktree) already has it out; a nonexistent branch fails closed — Git's remote-branch DWIM (auto `-b --track` on a unique remote match), `worktree.guessRemote`, and `--track`/`--no-track` are deferred. A commit target (and `--detach`, which also forces branch targets detached) seeds a detached HEAD populated from THAT commit. `-b` creates and checks out a new branch from the start point (default: source HEAD) with FULL rollback on any later failure — no branch-only or orphan-registry residue; in standard builds, `add -f/--force` reuses a non-empty destination and `remove -f/--force` bypasses the dirty-worktree refusal (`--delete-dir --force` deletes uncommitted changes); `worktree-fuse` builds use `-f` for `--fuse` and do not expose `--force`. `-B` (resetting an existing branch), `--lock [--reason]` (use the separate `worktree lock`), `--orphan`, and `--no-checkout` are deferred. Shared: the object store, `refs/heads`/`refs/tags`/`refs/remotes`, stash, config. `worktree list --porcelain` emits each worktree's OWN `HEAD <sha>` plus a `branch <ref>`/`detached` line (resolved from that worktree's scoped HEAD row); an entry with no resolvable HEAD (legacy layout / missing scope) omits those lines rather than being mislabeled. Deferred (see the deferred-features list): per-worktree ref namespaces (`refs/bisect`/`refs/worktree`) and pseudo-refs (ORIG_HEAD/MERGE_HEAD/…). The ENTIRE sequencer family — `cherry-pick`, `am`, `revert`, `merge`, `bisect`, and `rebase` (v0.19.42) — IS allowed in a linked worktree: their state is fully worktree-scoped (`cherry-pick`/`am` via the `sequence_state` row keyed by `worktree_id`; `revert` via `revert-state.json`, `merge` via `merge-state.json`/`merge-autostash.json`, all in the local gitdir; `bisect` via the `bisect_state` row keyed by `worktree_id`, with its checkouts/`reset` moving only that worktree's scoped HEAD and files; `rebase` via the `rebase_state` row keyed by `worktree_id` plus the worktree-local `rebase-aux.json` (exec queue / update-refs plan / rewrites / held autostash), with GC tracing every scope's state rows and sidecars as reachability roots; the editor buffers `CHERRY_PICK_MSG`/`REVERT_EDITMSG` are local too), and the start-time sequencer mutex resolves each per-worktree, so two worktrees can run them on their own branches concurrently without interfering. The dirty-set cache is worktree-scoped since W1 (§C.4.1.1, migration 2026072302): `dirty` and `status --scan/--cached/--check-dirty` run in any worktree against that worktree's own rows and freshness meta. The layer registry is worktree-scoped since W1 too (§C.4.1.1, migration 2026072303): every `layer` subcommand runs in any worktree against that worktree's own registrations/ownership (same name and destination may exist independently per worktree; removing a worktree purges its layer rows only when the directory is deleted too — a retained directory keeps its ownership rows so the still-materialized overlay files stay un-stageable). The sparse view is worktree-scoped since W1 as well (§C.4.1.1, migration 2026072304): every `sparse-view` subcommand and the `ls-files`/`diff`/`hydrate` gates act on the current worktree's own patterns and toggle (worktree remove/prune GCs them under the same directory-gone rule as layer rows). The stash stack completes the set since W2 (§C.4.3): the stack itself stays deliberately repository-shared while push/apply/pop act on the acting worktree's own index/workdir under a stack lock with by-id CAS deletion — so `stash` (all subcommands) and `pull --rebase --autostash` run in linked worktrees too, and NO command remains refused in a linked worktree on repository-global-state grounds (`pull`'s fetch phase writes only repository-scoped state; merge/rebase modes run on scoped state as before). `maintenance run` gc/repack run in multi-worktree repositories since W2 too (§C.4.3 typed `GcObjectSource` inventory): reachability roots span every worktree's private index (all stages), every scope's sequencer/rebase/bisect rows, every gitdir's held-autostash + merge/revert/rebase-aux sidecars, the shared refs/reflogs/stash reflog, note blobs, undo view snapshots, agent-run findings manifests, and AI capture checkpoints — with a schema-scan guard test that fails when a new OID-bearing column ships un-inventoried. The inventory is typed: `ReachabilityRoot` keeps objects alive, `AntiRoot` (obliteration tombstones) must never be resurrected, `Boundary` (`.libra/shallow`) stops the traversal without demanding the graft's absent parents, and `IndexOnly` (`object_index` and the ordinal/dirty caches) keeps nothing alive and is invalidated before the deletion it describes. `FETCH_HEAD` is deliberately a NON-root (matching Git): fetch records advertised tips that are already up to date and have no local destination, so rooting it would pin objects nothing references; freshly fetched objects are protected by the one-hour prune grace window instead. `fetch` is allowed in a linked worktree — its `FETCH_HEAD` is now worktree-local, and its other writes (`refs/remotes/*` and the object store) are repository-scoped by design; fetching into a branch checked out in another worktree is still refused. The on-disk ordinary registry is `{schema_version: 4, entries: [...]}` and adds an optional `name`. Known v2/v3 registries remain readable without bulk rewrites or relocation; normal mutations write v4 while preserving stable `worktree_id`, v3 `epoch_counter`/entry `epoch`, and registration history. The main and existing unnamed entries remain unnamed. The independent `worktree.list` wire schema is version 2 with optional names and actual absolute paths. Older binaries are refused rather than allowed to discard names. Registration generations still fence `libra service` dirty marks, and identity collisions still refuse mutations until explicitly repaired. FUSE defaults use `.libra/worktree/fuse/<NAME>/workspace` plus sibling `upper`; its versioned registry uses `{schema_version: 1, entries: [...]}` so old parsers fail closed. FUSE and non-FUSE builds honor the same namespace. The overlay hides `.libra/worktree` before mounting, and explicit FUSE targets inside lower/upper/common/task directories are rejected; FUSE move remains unsupported. `repair [<NAME>]` / `repair --path <PATH>` diverges from Git's `worktree repair [<path>...]` by design: Git rebuilds the bidirectional gitdir↔worktree links (and must sometimes run from the moved worktree itself); Libra's no-arg form dedupes the single-file registry and re-ensures the main entry, while `repair --path <PATH>` restores a linked worktree's missing/corrupt `.libra/worktree_id` + `commondir` from the registry's PERSISTED stable id — identity is never guessed, a commondir validly pointing at a different storage is refused without touching either file (never re-homed), unregistered paths, main, and a still-v1 registry (no persisted identities — the no-arg repair upgrades it first) are refused, and it always runs from wherever the shared storage resolves (typically main). BARE repositories (`init --bare` / mirror layouts, detected config-first via the recorded `core.bare` so even a bare directory literally named `.libra` is caught) refuse the ENTIRE worktree family with a stable error (`LBR-REPO-003`) before any registry IO (repository migrations may already apply at the CLI preflight, as for any repository command using the standard schema preflight — not a worktree side effect) — Git allows `git worktree add` from a bare repo, Libra defers bare worktree semantics by design (§C.4.1). Legacy symlink-layout worktrees (W3-s3 §C.6): read-only commands keep working through the shared HEAD/index (no regression), but the worktree-STATE mutation surface (add/commit/switch/checkout/restore/reset/merge/merge-file/rebase/cherry-pick/revert/am/bisect/stash/rm/mv/clean/pull/dirty/hydrate/rerere/read-tree/update-index, the mutating `sparse-view` and `layer` subcommands, the `symbolic-ref` write form, and non-dry-run `op restore`) refuses with `LBR-REPO-003` and a migrate hint — committing there would silently move MAIN's HEAD. `worktree list` (JSON + a new porcelain `layout` line) reports each entry's layout: `main`/`linked-v2`/`legacy-symlink`/`missing`/`corrupt` (plus `task-fuse` for FUSE task worktrees). `worktree doctor` (Libra extension, no Git equivalent) reports per-worktree scope diagnostics — layout, lifecycle state, whether the worktree's own identity is still one the registry knows, and what to do about each finding — and is STRICTLY READ-ONLY: registry, database, lease state and filesystem are byte-identical before and after. Repair actions are separate explicit subcommands, so no hint promises that a bare `doctor` will fix anything. JSON uses the `worktree.doctor` envelope with `schema_version`/`diagnostics[]`/`next_cursor`, an opaque cursor that pages `diagnostics[]` (`workspace_id` ascending, default limit 50, cap 500) per the W4 machine interface. `repair --migrate-layout [--dry-run]` with a name or `--path` selector migrates legacy worktrees from the MAIN worktree under the registry lock via a journaled state machine (migration 2026072403 admits the 'migrate' intent; its down refuses while one is in flight): the target `.libra` must be a symlink resolving exactly to this repository's storage (no-follow verified); a prepared journal-stamped gitdir is installed by atomic renames with the legacy link kept as an identity-named backup until verification passes; the new worktree seeds a DETACHED HEAD at the shared snapshot and rebuilds its private index from that commit — tracked/untracked FILES are never touched (they show as dirty/untracked afterwards) and SHARED staged state is never copied (its worktree ownership is unprovable — commit or stash it in main first); an unmerged shared index, an active main-scope sequencer, or an unreadable HEAD refuses before any rename; crash recovery in `worktree repair` advances or rolls back each journal window by IDENTITY (symlink target, journal-stamped marker), never by bare existence, and keeps materials + the journal on any mismatch. Target-oriented `worktree remove --path <PATH>` (both modes) and `worktree repair --path <PATH>` also refuse a legacy-symlink target with the migrate hint (their writes would route through the shared symlink into MAIN storage), and `worktree move` refuses an entry with a pending migration journal. `worktree doctor [<workspace-id>] [--limit N] [--cursor C]` (Libra-only, no Git equivalent) is the READ-ONLY diagnosis of Agent workspace scopes (`workspace_record`, §C.8): no invocation writes a row, registry entry, lease, or file. Without an id it pages `data.diagnostics[]` + an opaque `data.next_cursor` (`workspace_id` ASC, default limit 50, cap 500); with an id it returns the singular `data.diagnostic` and no pagination keys — combining the id with `--limit`/`--cursor` is `LBR-CLI-002`. Each diagnostic carries `workspace_id`/`repo_id`/`lease_state` (`none`|`held`|`expired`) and a `scope_diagnostics[]` of `{code,severity,detail}` findings (foreign repository identity, orphaned workspace, expired lease, missing path, missing/detached/tombstoned registry entry, corrupt or legacy-symlink layout). Records written under a PREVIOUS repository identity are visible here and nowhere else. A cursor this command did not issue fails closed with `LBR-WORKTREE-001` instead of restarting at page one, and a scope that cannot be read — unparseable registry, unreadable record, missing repository identity — fails closed with `LBR-WORKTREE-002` rather than reporting a partial diagnosis. Capture rows written before migration 2026080401 are `legacy_unknown` and excluded from capture/import/export writes until an operator explicitly attributes one through `worktree doctor <workspace-id> --adopt-capture-session <session-id> --confirm`. The command requires a live target workspace fence, converts every legacy capture row for that provider session across session/export/import tables, refuses if any scoped row already exists for the provider session, and appends an immutable audit record; it never changes the frozen read-only `worktree.doctor` JSON schema. | (issues/481 MX-16: `worktree remove -f` deletes a DIRTY worktree's directory and `worktree add -f` reuses an existing non-empty destination.)
| cloud | intentionally-different | Libra cloud backup/restore extension, not a Git command. Since B3-14, restore/sync/agent-capture repository kind comes only from D1 `repositories.object_format` metadata (written by B3-09 backup); OID width is never used to infer sha256/blake3. Legacy snapshots with no metadata and all-40-hex `o_id` rows remain sha1; any 64-hex (or mixed widths) without metadata fails closed with `LBR-REPO-002` / exit 128 and a re-backup hint. Minimum compatible client for new metadata-bearing snapshots: this release family (REL-B3-02 / B3-17) and newer — older clients that still guessed kind from OID width are unsupported against new backups. |
| mega2 | intentionally-different | Libra-only remote Mega2 browser: `libra mega2 browser --server <base-url> [path] [--ref <commit-or-tag>]` displays an anonymous, bounded left directory tree and right read-only file preview; tree expansion uses `GET /api/v1/tree`, previews use `GET /api/v1/blob` (256 KiB text / 1 MiB response cap), and syntax colors respect the normal color policy. Interactive mode requires TTYs and restores the terminal on every exit. `a` creates a directory, `d` deletes a directory after confirmation, `e` renames a directory, and `m` moves a selected file or directory through a separate destination-folder picker. That picker snapshots the source; `Right` only browses target folders, the first `Enter` chooses a target, the second submits the one `POST /api/v1/move-entry`, and `Esc` returns or cancels. File delete/rename remain inert. Mega2 rejects writes landing on `/`, so the picker restricts destinations to the source top-level root and rejects a directory's own subtree before any POST (for example, `/artifact/rust` cannot move to `/data`). `t` manages root tags. Write tokens resolve from `--token-file`, then `LIBRA_MEGA2_TOKEN`, then `--token`, and are never persisted or echoed. Non-interactive `--list`, `--create-dir`, `--delete-dir`, `--move-dir`, `--rename-dir`, `--list-tags`, `--create-tag`, and `--delete-tag` each execute their documented single request without reading stdin; operation flags are mutually exclusive, writes refuse `--ref`, and reads refuse token flags. Validation refuses unsafe URLs, paths, redirects, and hostile names; no local repository state is read or written. Git has no equivalent — compare local `ls-tree`. `libra mega2 traces` adds authenticated GET queries under `/api/v1/traces` and `fetch`, which reads one stream with Git upload-pack from `/.traces/by-scope/<uuid>.git` (HTTP token, or SSH login `traces`) and writes only `refs/remotes/<remote>/traces/<uuid>/<sha1|sha256>` plus its receipt config; it never touches `refs/heads/traces`, `refs/libra/traces`, `HEAD`, the index, or the worktree. |
| agent | intentionally-different | Libra external-agent capture extension, not a Git command. Grok is supported with `support_wave=grok` (not `first_batch`): automatic Thinking capture is paused; local `updates.jsonl` and `chat_history.jsonl` readers await authorized snapshot/shared-writer integration. Offline observer JSON is scratch state, not a checkpoint. Thinking text and unproven `encrypted_content` retention is the intended transcript behavior; Libra does not decrypt that field; default `checkpoint show` does not print that plaintext, and an explicit transcript path returns only content actually stored. Grok is not a review or investigate launcher. Legacy OpenCode ordinary checkpoint export and bound metadata reads strip the three opaque legacy keys only from reasoning parts and reserved metadata, preserving readable text and tool/user JSON; missing or mismatched session bindings and invalid, duplicate-key, unsupported or truncated nonempty OpenCode JSON fail closed without payload. Authorized `--raw --allow-raw` preserves original bounded bytes and the existing audit; show remains a safe summary, stored objects stay immutable, and newer state/native artifacts remain separately gated. The macOS real-content gate on the current OpenCode 2.0.26 machine remains deferred (DEFER-OG-OPENCODE-BOOT-01: private-server startup did not report readiness); source and synthetic gates do not certify that runtime or native reasoning. OpenCode export on macOS requires a Required Seatbelt server/export pair: both deny process-fork, use private HOME/XDG roots and a pinned binary copy, and communicate only on one authenticated loopback port. Trust, containment, readiness, bounded output or clean shutdown failure remains metadata-only; cancellation never signals an already-reaped process group. On Linux, content export requires trusted `bwrap` passing Libra's descriptor-native `--bind-fd`/`--ro-bind-fd` safety probe; an unavailable or legacy `bwrap` remains metadata-only, with no unsandboxed fallback. Historical Claude/Codex/OpenCode sessions can be backfilled through the consented, current-repository-scoped `agent import`; typed redaction, coverage/import fences, local erase tombstones, atomic no-clobber loose-object publication, and doctor-visible crash markers make replays idempotent and fail closed. `agent graph <session>` is a read-only capture projection (session → turn → revision → subagent), distinct from the former orchestrator-thread `libra graph` (removed in v0.23.0); its frozen JSON v1 uses a strict metadata whitelist, preserves shared checkpoint evidence, reports legacy captures as `unindexed`, and shows local tombstones as `erased` without resurrection. The interactive `agent graph` TUI entry was removed in the W5 breaking release (W5-08, shipped in v0.20.0): bare `libra agent graph <session>` fails with a usage error plus a migration hint, and the structured `libra --json agent graph` / `--machine` forms are unchanged. Current source builds additionally retain scoped Codex child thinking, opaque and unknown ordinary fields in independent content checkpoints through live capture, consented import and pending recovery. Root/current/immediate-parent/child identities remain distinct; unchanged or archived source replays do not create role-only revisions. Exact current-turn settlement proof is required for boundary association, while unproven or partial content remains exportable. UUID-session terminals keep known unsettled child claims pending; an available incomplete scan alone does not invent a required claim. Legacy raw labels retain their original parent-only live route without new child dispatch or claims; an already-elapsed discovery window can still add a fixed skip/incomplete-extraction diagnostic. The live predicate adds no identifier restriction to stored import/recovery, whose existing selector validation remains unchanged (public `--path` still requires a UUID filename). Claude behavior and wire remain unchanged. Unreleased UUID Codex metadata can include bounded `usage_attribution` with `scope=session_snapshot` and `as_of=live_capture` or `import_pass`: known parent/child subtotals are retained, while missing usage stays null. Exhaustive descendant discovery, current durable claims and complete operands permit a checked aggregate; otherwise it remains null/partial. Import captures children before parent metadata and preserves independent durable progress on failure; replays leave old snapshots unchanged. Older namespaces are excluded only after bounded role/source-commitment proof; unknown or capped records remain partial, and counts are bounded observations. Derived `sources[].parent` is null or a parent source commitment; ordinary captured native identities stay in child source data. New pending artifacts carrying this optional field require a compatible build for recovery; old artifacts/checkpoints are immutable. All new Codex tests stay feature-gated, ignored and local only. Readable summaries depend on what the provider returns; requesting a Codex reasoning summary does not decrypt opaque values. These source-build changes remain under local acceptance. Claude `<session>/subagents/*.jsonl` content is captured as independent source-revisioned `scope=subagent` checkpoints; hook boundaries remain distinct evidence, and association stays unresolved unless a provider-stable id matches exactly one boundary. The per-source import read cap consumes `agent.max_transcript_read_bytes` but never exceeds the 16 MiB adapter hard cap. `agent list --json` defaults to frozen schema v1; `--schema-version 2` opts into method availability. Deferred parity (non-goals for the current wave, tracked canonically in [`docs/development/tracing/agent.md`](docs/development/tracing/agent.md) 「还未实现的功能」 with each item's handling + restart condition): the unstable `agent add`/`remove` `--local-dev`/`--force` flags stay unpublished (canonical `enable`/`disable` + their `add`/`remove` aliases only); provider-specific transcript compaction/reassemble traits (the writer already stores large transcripts as manifest-relative chunks, but no provider-specific compactor exists yet); the optional capability traits (`ProtectedFilesProvider`/`TranscriptCompactor`/`HookResponseWriter`/`RestoredSessionPathResolver`, …) beyond the landed `DeclaredAgentCaps` matrix; external-RPC method families beyond the v2 `info`/capability gate (undeclared capabilities stay fail-closed); and the non-first-batch roster — `gemini`/`cursor`/`copilot`/`factory-ai` stay `supported=false` (unsupported, not hook-installable, not launchable for review/investigate), vs the first batch `claude-code`/`codex`/`opencode`; `agent workspace list|show` (Part C W4) is the read-only keyset-paginated machine interface over the `workspace_record` registry (states provisioning/active/releasing/released/orphaned, lease fence/expiry, canonical path; schema v1; lease mutation never exposed). `agent bridge --stdio` (plan-20260818 LB-01) is the repository-scoped DeepSeek Harness JSON-RPC 2.0 NDJSON ingress — the standard repository-scoped inbound write transport for Harness, distinct from the former `libra code --control stdio` session-control transport (removed in v0.23.0); it is not a Git command and does not alter hook-provider semantics. All 20 v1 methods are implemented as of v0.21.1: `diff.get` takes a closed `mode` (`worktree`/`staged`/`checkpoint`) plus validated repository-relative paths (never a free-form revision or pathspec) and forces `--no-ext-diff`/`--no-textconv`; `commit.create` commits only the current index and records provenance as association rows, not in the commit message; `checkpoint.restore` requires an explicit `expected_head` fence plus a clean index/worktree and never moves HEAD; `review.run` starts a supervised read-only review whose process groups are cancelled and drained when the bridge stops. A drifted fence is refused before any write with `LBR-AGENT-038`. Since B3-10, repository commit refs use Libra tagged `repo-commit:<kind>:<kind-native hex>` on `ai_index_task_run.base_commit_ref` and agent-bridge/traces/checkpoint stored ids accept that tagged form (or bare kind-native hex under the active repository kind); IntegrityHash remains SHA-256-only and never carries a repository OID; the removed `libra code` MCP `--stdio` surface is not restored. OpenCode parser vocabulary is pinned to 2.0.26: `session.status(idle)` is the compatible turn-end input, busy/retry are rejected, and the deprecated `session.idle` alias emits only a content-free `legacy_event_alias` tracing warning. The normalized legacy lifecycle payload now includes an explicit empty `"prompt":""` field when the managed plugin emits it. Legacy `message.updated` requires a prompt string; an empty string and an omitted role are accepted, an explicit role must be `user`, and other message fields never fill a missing prompt. Refresh with `libra agent enable --agent opencode` to install the current 2.0.26 `setup(ctx)` template. Node/Bun use the pinned absolute filename with `node:child_process` and `shell:false`; session/turn boundaries are synchronous and tool/compaction hooks observe asynchronously. Status requires the exact current rendered template and no managed duplicate; old marker-only or edited plugins report not installed. User inbox delivery is paired once, and legacy text-part pairing supplies text or an explicit empty string before the next unrelated event/cleanup. Child timeout is export deadline + 15 seconds with SIGKILL; cleanup/exit share a total deadline across sessions/instances. Stable-namespace path preflight and no-follow snapshot checks reject special paths; post-check relocation/leaf replacement remains deferred. The plugin is observe-only and `--pure` disables it; synthetic runtime conformance does not prove live/native capture. See the agent command docs for bounded buffer/truncation behavior.  OpenCode 2.0.26 export metadata projects user.text, assistant model.id and five-component tokens (input + output + reasoning + cache.read + cache.write), without adding session totals; classic assistant aggregates override deduplicated step-finish details. Successful edit/write/patch and snapshot files become project-relative paths; invalid/missing provenance or counters stays partial. This does not establish native reasoning origin or real macOS content export. Native/classic OpenCode coverage splits by human message; valid native idle settles pending capture even on failed/interrupted outcomes, while trailing/unknown/malformed/reasoning content stays Incomplete. Nine known classic metadata parts do not project; unsupported user attachments/skill text/tool files remain Incomplete without attachment bytes or URIs. Native error fallback projects error.message, never response.body. OpenCode user reminder filtering is shared by coverage and metadata extraction: exact nested marker blocks are removed, human remainder and independent usage are retained; malformed markers stay partial/Incomplete. This literal filter does not prove producer origin. OpenCode empty/invalid exports are partial with fixed payload-free warnings; recognized flat model/token metadata remains projected, and noncritical extraction failure does not block an otherwise admissible checkpoint or replace an existing checkpoint with malformed export bytes.  OG-06 doctor adds nullable per-event managed-template installation status, sanitized nonregular OpenCode config-slot diagnostics and a bounded read-only `opencode_legacy_reasoning_checkpoints` absence list (64 catalog rows, 64 KiB metadata each). Missing `reasoning_state` never proves Complete or native provenance; presence alone does not prove validity either. No transcript/artifact-body read or data migration is added; incomplete scans remain explicit. `agent mcp --stdio` is the new repository-operation MCP surface, distinct from the removed `libra code` MCP. Codex historical import scans active and archived rollouts; `--since` uses the bounded `session_meta` timestamp rather than mtime, reports missing time explicitly, and prefers an active duplicate. `agent push --mega2` sends the local traces branch to `refs/libra/traces` of a Mega2 TraceRepo (`/.traces/by-path/…`, then `/.traces/by-scope/<uuid>.git` once the scope is confirmed) over HTTP(S) or SSH login `traces`; it is fast-forward only, never forces, opens a new epoch only with `--new-stream`, never rewrites the stored remote URL, and never falls back to an ordinary code push. |

| logout | intentionally-different | Libra host-scoped HTTP auth extension (session-token logout), not a Git command |
