| review | intentionally-different | Libra read-only agent review extension (AG-22), not a Git command: fans a review prompt out to external reviewer CLIs (claude-code/codex/opencode) in an isolated workspace and records run state, redacted logs, and untrusted findings under `.libra/sessions/agent-runs/<run_id>/`; `--checkpoint <id>` scopes the review to a captured agent checkpoint (PD-02) — the reviewers' workspace is the checkpoint's own content materialized read-only at `.libra/worktree/tasks/<run-id>/<attempt-id>/checkpoint-input/` (no worktree snapshot), missing/non-materializable checkpoints fail closed before any run exists, and the materialization shares the run's retention/doctor surface. Every saved spec is revalidated against the explicit catalog and manifest/tree ordinary roles before creating a fresh input attempt. Exact closed artifact path/id tuples in a legacy full-list spec are excluded without opening their payload; altered or unproven entries fail closed. Supported wrapped checkpoints without a manifest remain compatible. A saved id that is not a blob, or whose bytes do not hash to that id, is refused before creating a fresh input attempt. A zero setup budget ends as the existing infrastructure error and does not launch reviewers; cancel stays cancelled. Scoped clean --run/--all proves own-kind terminal metadata before bounded readonly-input cleanup; foreign/nonterminal/unknown/corrupt bulk candidates survive and removed counts real successful deletions. Specific corrupt-state recovery requires a valid own terminal manifest; metadata conflicts refuse. No new cleanup flag or stable error code. A proved own-kind terminal state remains cleanable when its matching manifest has no terminal marker after an interrupted state-first finalization. Identity/schema/common-field conflicts and differing non-null terminal values remain refused. Bulk qualification I/O, link or size refusals return an error; already-removed earlier runs are not rolled back. A proved own-kind terminal state also remains cleanable when its manifest is missing. A terminal review infrastructure failure may have no reviewer outcome rows while its manifest retains the requested reviewers; that supported shape is cleanable. An unparseable manifest is still refused even beside a proved terminal state: preserve the run and repair its metadata before retrying.  New ordinary task workspaces use `.libra/worktree/tasks/<run-id>/<attempt-id>/workspace/` under common storage; session metadata stays in its existing directory. Investigate continue uses a fresh attempt without overwriting prior input. New run state and manifests use schema version 2 with an ownership descriptor; known version 1 remains readable without bulk migration, and unknown versions refuse writes/cleanup. Cleanup validates ownership and removes the owned workspace before run metadata; failures preserve retry records. `agent doctor` reports unowned managed task directories without deleting them. |

A saved id that is not a blob, or whose bytes do not hash to that id, is refused before creating a fresh input attempt. `reasoning/encrypted/<64 hex>` is not rematerialized. Scoped clean --run/--all proves own-kind terminal metadata before bounded readonly-input cleanup; foreign/nonterminal/unknown/corrupt bulk candidates survive and removed counts real successful deletions. Specific corrupt-state recovery requires a valid own terminal manifest; metadata conflicts refuse. No new cleanup flag or stable error code. A proved own-kind terminal state remains cleanable when its matching manifest has no terminal marker after an interrupted state-first finalization. Identity/schema/common-field conflicts and differing non-null terminal values remain refused. Bulk qualification I/O, link or size refusals return an error; already-removed earlier runs are not rolled back. A proved own-kind terminal state also remains cleanable when its manifest is missing. A terminal review infrastructure failure may have no reviewer outcome rows while its manifest retains the requested reviewers; that supported shape is cleanable. An unparseable manifest is still refused even beside a proved terminal state: preserve the run and repair its metadata before retrying.  New ordinary task workspaces use `.libra/worktree/tasks/<run-id>/<attempt-id>/workspace/` under common storage; session metadata stays in its existing directory. Investigate continue uses a fresh attempt without overwriting prior input. Cleanup validates ownership and removes the owned workspace before run metadata; failures preserve retry records. `agent doctor` reports unowned managed task directories without deleting them. |
| maintenance | partial | `run` / `register` / `unregister` / `status` / `start` / `stop` exposed; `start`/`stop` install/remove an OS scheduler entry (launchd LaunchAgents plist on macOS, cron fragment elsewhere; dir overridable via `LIBRA_MAINTENANCE_AGENT_DIR`); `gc` recursively traces SQLite refs/reflogs (both endpoints and annotated-tag targets), every index stage, every file-backed stash reflog entry, and held merge/rebase autostash sidecars before loose-object deletion, failing closed on malformed/unreadable roots or reachable objects; the reachability walk enumerates EVERY worktree's private index (all stages), each scope's sequencer/rebase/bisect rows, and each gitdir's held autostash and merge/revert/rebase-aux sidecars, so loose-object pruning is enabled in repositories with linked worktrees (the earlier blanket skip is lifted); a shallow clone's `.libra/shallow` entries are traversal **boundaries** — the boundary commit is kept and its absent parents are not demanded — and unreadable roots, unparseable shallow metadata, or a live `alternates` borrower all fail closed before anything is deleted; the `commit-graph` task writes a Git-compatible v1 commit-graph file (OIDF/OIDL/CDAT chunks + topological generation numbers; blake3 repositories skip the `commit-graph` task (no Git CGPH hash_version; warning + leave existing files; history walks use object traversal); octopus merges are written via the EDGE chunk, and SHA-256 repositories via 32-byte OIDs + a SHA-256 header version/trailer); the `prefetch` task fetches all configured remotes via the normal fetch path (refreshing standard remote-tracking refs rather than Git's `refs/prefetch/` namespace — intentionally different; skipped when no remotes are configured) |
| hooks | intentionally-different | Repository hooks use `.libra/hooks`, never `.git/hooks` or `core.hooksPath`. Supported lifecycle: `pre-commit`, `prepare-commit-msg`, `commit-msg`, `post-commit`, `post-checkout`, `pre-rebase`, `pre-merge-commit`, `post-merge`, and `post-rewrite`; automatic merge commits run the message/post-commit hooks, and pull shares its selected merge/rebase lifecycle. Canonical extensionless names win over `.sh` (Unix) / `.ps1` (Windows), with symlinks and non-executable Unix files rejected fail-closed. Hooks run from a private copy in the required workspace-only, network-denied sandbox; repository metadata is protected except the commit message file for message hooks, and caller environment secrets are removed via an explicit process/locale allowlist. Blocking pre/message hooks abort before the associated history mutation; post hooks are advisory; quiet/JSON/machine suppress captured hook output. `commit --no-verify`, `merge --no-verify`, and `LIBRA_NO_HOOKS=1` are explicit policy bypasses. `.git/hooks` bridge remains deferred under D3. Customized Windows hooks currently fail closed because the restricted-token backend is not implemented; the exact shipped no-op template is skipped. See [repository hooks](docs/commands/repository-hooks.md). |
| archive | partial | Creates tar/tar.gz/tar.bz2/zip archives from a committed tree; `--format`, `--output`, `--prefix`, `--list`, `-v`/`--verbose` (lists archived paths on stderr), `--add-file=<file>` (inject an untracked working-tree file at its basename under the prefix, bypassing the pathspec filter; repeatable; nothing is written to the object store), `--compression-level <0-9>` (compression level for tar.gz/tar.bz2/zip — Git's `-0`..`-9`, which clap cannot model as bare numeric flags; plain tar ignores it, and bzip2 has no level 0 so 0 is treated as 1), `--mtime <time>` (set every entry's modification time — same date formats as `--since`/`--until`; without it the archived commit's committer time is used, instead of epoch 0), `export-ignore` filtering from the archived tree's `.gitattributes` / `.libra_attributes` blobs (uncommitted working-tree attributes do not affect an existing `TREEISH` archive), and `TREEISH <path>...` pathspec limiting supported (matched through the shared pathspec engine: plain names, wildcards, and `:(glob)`/`:(literal)`/`:(icase)`/`:(exclude)`; absolute and `..` paths are still refused); tar/tar.gz/tar.bz2 entries whose archive path (including `--prefix`) or symlink target exceeds the fixed 100-byte header fields use interoperable GNU LongName/LongLink extensions rather than failing, with no claim of byte-for-byte pax parity with Git; `export-subst` is not implemented | (issues/481 MX-10: an omitted `--format` is inferred from the `-o` extension (`.zip`/`.tar`/`.tar.gz`/`.tgz`/`.tar.bz2`/`.tbz2`/`.tbz`, else `tar`).)
| check-attr | partial | reports Git/Libra attributes for pathnames from `core.attributesFile`, per-directory `.gitattributes`, `.libra_attributes`, and the worktree-local `info/attributes` (`.libra/info/attributes` in the current worktree's own gitdir, plus `.git/info/attributes` in a Git- or dual-layout tree; per-worktree rather than `commondir`-shared, intentionally different from Git) (same-directory `.libra_attributes` overrides `.gitattributes`); supports `check-attr <attr> <path>...`, `--all` (all set/unset/value attributes), `--stdin`, `-z` (NUL framing), and `--json`/`--machine`. Intentional difference (see [docs/development/commands/_compatibility.md#d5-git-lfs-gitattributes-filter--hooks-bridge](docs/development/commands/_compatibility.md#d5-git-lfs-gitattributes-filter--hooks-bridge)): this is a read-only query and does **not** run Git `.gitattributes` smudge/clean filter drivers. Output `<path>: <attr>: <value|set|unset|unspecified>`; exit 0 always (128 only on usage/repo error). Git's `--cached`, `--source`, and attributes macros are not exposed | (issues/481 MX-11: `--cached` reads attribute files from the index and `--source=<tree>` from a tree (peeled to its tree).)
| check-mailmap | partial | Resolves `Name <email>` contacts (arguments or `--stdin`) through the worktree `.mailmap` and prints the canonical `Name <email>`; supports all four mailmap line forms with `(name,email)` rules taking precedence over email-only rules (email match is case-insensitive); `--json`/`--machine` emits `{ contacts }`. Exit 0 / 128 (no repo, no contacts, or a contact missing `<email>`). `mailmap.file`/`mailmap.blob` config and the `log`/`blame` author-display integration are deferred |
| check-ignore | partial | reports pathnames excluded by Git/Libra ignore sources (`.gitignore`, the worktree-local `info/exclude` — `.libra/info/exclude` in the current worktree's own gitdir, plus `.git/info/exclude` in a Git- or dual-layout tree; per-worktree rather than `commondir`-shared, intentionally different from Git — `core.excludesFile`, and `.libraignore`; same-directory `.libraignore` overrides `.gitignore`); `<pathname>...`, `--stdin`, `-z` (NUL input/output framing), `-v`/`--verbose` (`<source>:<line>:<pattern>\t<path>`; the line number is recovered by scanning the source ignore file because the matcher engine does not expose it), `-n`/`--non-matching` (requires `-v`), and `--no-index` (report a pattern match even for a tracked path) supported, plus `--json`/`--machine`. Exit 0 when at least one path is ignored, 1 when none are, 128 on a usage/repository error. Git's `--exclude`/`--exclude-from`/`--exclude-per-directory` and full pathspec magic are not exposed |
| cat-file | partial | `-t`, `-s`, `-p`, `-e`, AI object modes (`-p` prints commits byte-for-byte, including `gpgsig`/extra headers, and tree entries with Git's octal modes and `blob`/`tree`/`commit` types), and the `--batch-check` / `--batch` / `--batch-command` stdin modes (with optional `=<format>` atom expansion) supported. Git object modes share the strict resolver: full refs, `@`, parent/ancestor, typed/recursive peel, numeric reflog selectors, and `REV:path`; batch reports only unresolvable/nonexistent names as `missing` and fails closed on ref/storage corruption. Batch stdin is streamed line by line (each response flushed before the next line is read unless `--buffer`), and non-UTF-8 lines are answered `<input> missing` instead of aborting the batch. `--batch-command` dispatches `info`/`contents`; `--buffer` block-buffers output and queues `--batch-command` commands until `flush`; `--batch-all-objects` enumerates loose + packed objects in id order. `-e --json`/`--machine` emits `{ exists: bool }` while preserving present 0 / absent 1. Unresolvable object names exit 129 (`LBR-CLI-003`), intentionally different from Git's 128 |
| fsck | partial | object/ref/index/reflog/connectivity checks supported with JSON/machine output via `--json`/`--machine`; `--strict` adds commit email/timezone (format-only `±HHMM`, no range check — matching Git), commit tree/parent existence+type, and tree entry existence/type/sort-order checks; findings carry severity per Git's msg-id table (zero-padded file modes are a `zeroPaddedFilemode` warning by default and escalate to an error under `--strict`; `--json` messages carry `severity`) (intentionally narrower than Git: `.gitmodules`/pathname-charset checks and `fsck.<msg-id>` severity config are not implemented); `--full`/`--no-full` packfile verification supported (on by default, like Git — each `.pack` is checked against its trailing checksum and each `.idx` via the shared index parser, without decoding pack objects, so a body-corrupt pack is reported rather than crashing the decoder); `--heal` (Libra extension, not in Git) re-fetches missing/corrupt objects from the configured durable tier (`LIBRA_STORAGE_*`), verifies each fetched payload hashes to its OID before writing (never fabricates), skips objects marked intentionally absent, and emits a repair summary (`heal` object in `--json`); heal runs before the checks so the exit code reflects the post-repair state, and with no durable tier configured every candidate is reported unrecoverable; on a repository that has (or had) linked worktrees `--heal` refuses up front with `LBR-REPO-003` (W0 hard gate — its discovery does not cover the per-worktree object inventory; use `libra maintenance run` for the inventory-complete reachability walk, or remove linked worktrees first); default / `--connectivity-only` / `--strict` connectivity walks report Git-style `broken link from` / `to` plus `missing <type>` for absent commit parents, trees, and tree entries, except parents of commits listed in `.libra/shallow` (corrupt shallow metadata fail-closed as `LBR-REPO-002`); `--json` adds `broken_links` and `missing_objects`; blake3 repositories verify loose objects and pack trailers with BLAKE3 digests |
| gc | partial | cleanup command combining pack-refs, reflog expiry (default `gc.reflogExpire`/`gc.reflogExpireUnreachable`), `repack -a -d`, unreachable loose-object pruning past `gc.pruneExpire` (default `2.weeks.ago`; `--prune=<date>`/`--no-prune`, with `now`/`never`/bare days/`N.<unit>.ago`/absolute date forms), and worktree prune, in `git gc`'s step order, reusing the maintenance reachability inventory (refs/reflogs/every index stage/stash+autostash sidecars/sequencer state) and the alternates borrow protection — reachable objects are never deleted and the deletion steps refuse while a live alternates borrower exists (`LBR-CONFLICT-002`), with a reachability re-check under the exclusive maintenance hold before unlinking; `--auto` gates the whole run on `gc.auto` (default 6700; `gc.auto <= 0` disables) with zero writes below the threshold; `--aggressive` is accepted and reported in the `--json` envelope (the enlarged delta window lands with `repack --window/--depth`, MX-09); `--quiet` and `--json`/`--machine` supported (`data.steps[]` summarizes each step with per-step counters); redundant-*pack* removal (MX-08), cruft packs, commit-graph, and bitmaps are not implemented |
| verify-pack | partial | validates one or more `.idx` files against matching `.pack` siblings using the repository `core.objectformat` (sha1/sha256/blake3; no layout guessing); outside a repository `--hash-kind` is required (`LBR-CLI-002` if omitted); `-s` / `--stat-only` supported; `--pack` is available for a single explicit pack path |
| count-objects | partial | read-only loose/pack census with Git's exact field names and order (`count`, `size`, `in-pack`, `packs`, `size-pack`, `prune-packable`, `garbage`, `size-garbage`, then `alternate:` lines), `-H` humanised sizes, `--json`/`--machine` structured output (garbage entries capped at 100 with a truncation flag only above 100; raw UTF-8 alternate paths, with non-UTF-8 structured paths refused); works in bare/subdir/worktree, exit 0 on success. Zero-write: no `.idx` rebuild (idx-less packs are garbage, not repaired), no schema migration, no maintenance lock, no operation record; unreadable shard/pack dirs fail closed (`LBR-IO-001`). Intentional differences: garbage/alternate paths absolute, Operation Log snapshot objects counted, local tier only; human alternate paths use Git default C-style escaping, garbage warnings capped at 100 |
| index-pack | partial | hidden plumbing command for pack file indexing; `--stdin`, `--keep[=<msg>]`, Git-style `--progress` / `--no-progress`, and `--fix-thin` (accepted no-op — Libra's pack decoder requires self-contained packs and never produces thin packs, so a pack that indexes successfully has no external delta bases to complete, matching Git's no-op on a complete pack; resolving external delta bases is not supported) are accepted |
| repack | partial | consolidates objects into one `pack-<checksum>.pack` (+ `.idx`) via the single shared pack writer used by `maintenance` (so the result round-trips through `index-pack`/`verify-pack`); `-a`/`--all` packs all reachable objects (default: only reachable-loose ones), `-d`/`--delete` prunes the loose objects now in the pack (existing packs are never removed, so nothing is left unreferenced), `-q`/`--quiet` and `--json`/`--machine` supported. Reachability comes from refs/reflogs/index (like the gc task). Always writes a single undeltified pack: delta compression, `--window`/`--depth`, geometric repacking, bitmaps, and redundant-*pack* removal are not implemented |
| pack-objects | partial | hidden plumbing command sharing `repack`'s writer; reads object ids from stdin (one per line, tolerant of `rev-list --objects`' `<id> <path>` form) and writes one pack into `objects/pack` (printing its `pack-<checksum>` stem) or streams raw pack bytes with `--stdout`. Intentionally minimal: no `--revs`/`--all` history walking, always undeltified, no thin-pack/bitmap options |
| checkout | partial | visible branch compatibility surface plus `checkout -` previous-checkout target shared with `switch -` (worktree-scoped HEAD reflog; missing/deleted/corrupt target fails closed), `checkout <commit>` / `-d`/`--detach` detached HEAD, `-b`/`-B <branch> [<start-point>]` branch creation/reset that leaves `HEAD` symbolic on the target branch, `--orphan <branch>` unborn branch creation (preserves index/worktree, first user commit has no parents; separate start-point currently rejected), `-t`/`--track` (accepted no-op — Libra always configures tracking for a remote-tracking checkout via DWIM), `--ignore-other-worktrees` (Part C W0, **intentionally-different from Git**: `checkout`/`switch` refuse a branch already checked out in another worktree since branches are shared, and this flag does NOT bypass the guard — Libra never allows the same branch checked out in two worktrees. The flag is accepted for CLI compatibility and is a silent no-op in a single-worktree repo; requesting it against a real collision is refused with a note that it is not honored), `--no-progress` (accepted no-op — Libra's checkout renders no progress meter), `--no-overlay` (accepted no-op — Libra's checkout is never in overlay mode, matching the Git default; `--overlay` is not implemented), and explicit `checkout -- <path>` restoration alias with shared pathspec magic and literal path/directory-prefix fallback (including symlink paths restored as symlinks via restore); prefer `switch` / `restore` for new code; patch modes still partial; same case-collision preflight as `switch` (lore.md 1.14) on both its branch and detached flows Remaining unsupported interactive options (`-p`/`--patch` and `--[no-]auto-advance`) fail with `LBR-UNSUPPORTED-001` (D15). Bare `--detach` detaches at the current HEAD instead of showing the current branch (unborn HEAD is refused with `You are on a branch yet to be born`, `LBR-REPO-003`, exit 128).; `LBR-UNSUPPORTED-001` diagnostic for unsupported interactive options; bare `--detach` detaches at HEAD branch/detached flows and `checkout -- <path>` materialize entries with their mode's permission bits under the process `umask` (plan issues/470 FM-01, ADR-FM-02). Explicit text/eol clean comparison is shared with add/status; representation-only CRLF/LF differences do not create tracked changes or false dirty preflight failures. Attribute changes invalidate prior clean results; mode/type and real edits remain visible. text=auto shares binary detection and historical CRLF index protection with add; see the add reference for renormalize, the 32 MiB local index evidence limit and remaining configuration boundaries. | (issues/522: `checkout <path>` and `checkout <rev> <path>` are accepted without the `--` separator (a token that resolves to no revision but matches the index is a pathspec; `--` always forces path meaning), `-B <current-branch>` re-points the checked-out branch like Git, and `checkout --orphan` carries a dirty worktree over instead of requiring a clean tree. `git checkout-index` remains a separate, unimplemented plumbing command.)
| auth | intentionally-different | Host-scoped HTTP token auth v1 (lore.md 1.6): `login` (token via hidden prompt or `--with-token` stdin ONLY — no `--token` flag by design), `status` (never prints secrets; `--host` scriptable exit 0-iff-valid), `logout`/`clear` (idempotent revoke, works after key rotation). AES-256-GCM at rest under the 0600 global vault key; `auth.token.*` locked away from `config get/set/list/unset`. Stored tokens attach only to matching https host:port scopes (http for loopback); https→http downgrade redirects are refused outright (reqwest strips credentials only on host/port changes); the interactive 401 prompt remains the process-global fallback; `credential fill` consults the store silently with username pinning. Git's nearest analogue is external credential helpers (credential-store = plaintext). Exit 0 / 1 / 128 / 129; lore.md 2.7: OS-keyring backend (`auth.backend` = file|keyring, file default; release binaries ship the keyring feature — Linux uses VENDORED static libdbus; `auth migrate --to <backend>` moves tokens with probe + verify-readback, idempotent; lookup dual-reads so flipping the backend is non-destructive; revocation reaches BOTH backends — a featureless build REFUSES to half-revoke a keyring-marked scope; status gains additive `backend` field + `unreadable` state, non-zero for `status --host`); interactive: non-TTY 401s fail fast with an `auth login` hint (never consume piped protocol data), TTY prompts show the hint once, and a PROMPTED attempt that genuinely succeeds (2xx only — 403 may be rate-limiting with wrong credentials) offers ONCE per scope to persist via the auth machinery (consent default No; `auth.saveOnPrompt` = ask|always|never; suppressed without TTYs) |
| bisect | partial | `start` / `bad` / `good` / `reset` / `skip` / `log` / `run` / `view` (with Git's `visualize` alias — a text state summary, not a gitk GUI, since Libra is terminal-native) and `start --first-parent` (restrict the candidate walk to first-parent history) supported; runs per-worktree — `bisect_state` is keyed by `worktree_id`, so each linked worktree bisects independently (v0.19.34); `replay` (see [docs/development/commands/_compatibility.md#d6-bisect-replay](docs/development/commands/_compatibility.md#d6-bisect-replay)) deferred. **MX-03 session safety (2026-10-20):** every `good`/`bad`/`skip` step preflights its candidate checkout BEFORE persisting state, so a refused step leaves the saved session untouched (`current` does not advance) and a later `reset` still restores the original HEAD; `reset` always ends the session — a refused restore clears the row and warns with the original target (ADR-MX-03 §2); marking `good`/`bad` after convergence exits 0 and re-prints `<oid> is the first bad commit` instead of an internal error; the culprit subject skips vault `gpgsig`/`gpgsig-sha256` header blocks; without a session `bad`/`good`/`skip`/`log`/`view`/`run` are unified on `LBR-BISECT-001` (exit 128) and `reset` prints `We are not bisecting.` (exit 0). **MX-04/MX-05 start args + terms (2026-10-20):** `bisect start` accepts Git's positional `<bad> [<good>...]` (`--good` kept as a compatible alias) and only refuses on TRACKED staged/unstaged changes — untracked and ignored files (including `.libraignore`) no longer block the start (intentionally-different from Git, ADR-MX-05 §3; a colliding candidate is caught by the per-step preflight); `bisect terms [--term-good|--term-bad]`, `start --term-old/--term-new`, and `new`/`old` plus custom-term mark subcommands are implemented (D7 closed) with terms persisted in repo config (`config_kv`, worktree-scoped) and rendered by `log`/`view`; without a session `terms` also reports `LBR-BISECT-001` (exit 128) |

| rev-parse | partial | The complete pseudo-ref set is `ORIG_HEAD`, `MERGE_HEAD`, `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD`, `REVERT_HEAD`, `REBASE_HEAD`, `FETCH_HEAD`, and `AUTO_MERGE`. `AUTO_MERGE` alone is accepted, and only while a non-squash conflicted merge sidecar projects its automatic result tree (including conflict markers); `rev-parse` prints that tree id, while `cat-file` and positional `diff` are the only other opt-in readers. `merge --continue`, `--abort`, or `--quit` removes the sidecar and the name again; clean and squash merges never create it. The six historical names remain NOT accepted as revisions. Global object resolution and write paths, including `update-ref`, remain unchanged and reject every pseudo-ref. |

### Push Advertised-Have Object Selection

Push pack generation treats every server-advertised OID that is available in the local object database as a shared negative have, matching Git send-pack rather than limiting reuse to the destination ref's old value. A new branch or tag at an already-advertised commit therefore sends zero objects; annotated-tag peeled commits and direct advertised objects are reusable, while invalid or locally unavailable advertised OIDs are ignored conservatively. Real zero-object updates still transmit a valid empty pack (32 bytes for SHA-1, 44 bytes for SHA-256). This changes no refspec, force, permission, transport, or output-schema contract.

### Shallow Transfer Integrity (P0-03)

`clone --depth`, `fetch --depth`, and `pull --depth` are supported only for Git
transports that can advertise shallow boundaries. A local Libra source cannot
send `shallow <oid>` metadata, so these commands fail closed before object
transfer with `LBR-REPO-002` instead of leaving refs that point at commits whose
parents are missing. `rev-parse --is-shallow-repository` is supported and reports
whether `.libra/shallow` contains at least one boundary. Decision (2026-07-23,
plan-20260714 PD-06 / register D20): local-Libra shallow negotiation stays
**declined** — fail-closed is the accepted end state, not a pending gap; see the
development compatibility register D20 for rationale and restart conditions.

### Local-path clone object encoding (CLH-01)

A `libra clone <local-path>` of a large repository (thousands of commits / tens
of thousands of reachable objects) encodes the pack through the shared
`pack_writer::encode_pack_bytes` encoder that `repack`/`pack-objects`/
`maintenance` already use. This eliminates the deterministic deadlock where the
local-path fetch hung at “Fetching objects” (0% CPU) once the reachable object
set exceeded the bounded encoder channels. No command option, output field or
error code changed; the fix only routes local-path fetching through the single,
correctly-bounded encoder (ADR-CLH-01 / issues/496; supersedes issues/487
ADR-IG-01's adjacent-scope fix).

Diff prefix configuration is part of the `diff` compatibility surface:
`diff.noPrefix`, `diff.mnemonicPrefix`, `diff.srcPrefix`, and `diff.dstPrefix`
honor the strict local → global → system cascade and Git precedence. Mnemonic
pairs cover index/worktree (`i/w`), commit/worktree (`c/w`), commit/index
(`c/i`), and commit/commit (`c/c`); `-R` swaps the pair, `/dev/null` remains
unprefixed, external-driver output remains verbatim, and `commit -v` uses the
built-in staged diff as Git does. The shared plumbing wrappers inherit these
display prefixes while continuing to suppress porcelain rename defaults.
Unreadable local/global stores fail closed; unreadable or unsupported system
scope is skipped under the established strict-config contract.

### Diff review metadata and pickaxe (P1-08a/P1-08b)

The `diff` row above remains `partial`, but the script-facing metadata slice now
supports `--raw` (including `-z` rename fields and mode/type changes),
`--compact-summary`, `--diff-filter` include/exclude/`*` semantics,
`--full-index`, and CLI `--src-prefix`/`--dst-prefix` overrides. `--summary` now
includes mode changes. Raw live-worktree object IDs are zero while repository
sides retain their IDs, including same-content mode-only changes. Pickaxe
`-S <STRING>` filters per file pair by changed non-overlapping occurrence count
(textconv output when active, raw bytes otherwise), while `-G <REGEX>` filters
by matching added/removed hunk content; invalid regexes fail before scanning and
both filters run before an external diff driver. P1-08c also supports
`--color-words[=<regex>]` as the redirected-output-aware color shorthand and
`--word-diff-regex=<regex>` as the standalone custom tokenizer; global
`--color=never` still wins, and `--word-diff=plain` remains bracketed under tty
or forced color. P1-08c now also exposes real Myers (the truthful default),
Myers-minimal, Patience, and Histogram backends through `--algorithm` plus
`--minimal`/`--patience`/`--histogram`. Repeatable `--anchored=<text>` adds
Git-compatible anchored Patience: qualifying lines must be unique on both sides
and start with a supplied prefix; selector retention/clearing follows Git.

### Merge-driver dispatch (MG-08 / MG-09)

`merge`, `merge-file`, `cherry-pick`, and `revert` share the path-level
`merge` gitattribute dispatch. Bare `merge` and `-merge` directly select text
and whole-file binary handling. Named `text`/`binary`/`union` values select the
matching built-in only when no external driver of that name is configured;
union retains ours then theirs in each overlapping region, with binary input
falling back to a marker-free whole-file conflict. Any other unconfigured name
falls back directly to text. Only
an absent attribute consults `merge.default`, whose unset or unknown value also
falls back to text. `merge-file` outside a Libra repository deliberately has no
attributes/config source and always selects text.

`libra merge` additionally executes `merge.<name>.driver` from the strict local
→ global → system cascade when a named attribute or `merge.default` selects it;
external configuration overrides a same-named built-in, while boolean
`merge`/`-merge` remains direct built-in dispatch. Git-compatible placeholders,
shell quoting for `%P/%S/%X/%Y`, `%A` readback (including empty output), and the
0 / 1..128 / >128 result split are supported. Inputs are private unpredictable
files in a mode-0700 worktree-local directory and are cleaned by RAII; errors
do not echo the trusted shell command. Native path bytes are preserved without
lossy UTF-8 conversion. As a security hardening, `%O/%A/%B`
remain raw only for shell-safe absolute paths and are single-quoted when an
unsafe worktree directory would otherwise become shell syntax. Commands run via `sh -c`, including on
Windows (where a compatible `sh` must be available). Recursive folds reuse the driver with
temporary-branch labels, but `merge.<name>.recursive`, sandboxing, timeouts, and
external-driver execution from `merge-file`/`cherry-pick`/`revert` remain
unsupported. A fatal result stops Libra from publishing merge-driven repository
state, but direct mutations made by the trusted unsandboxed command are outside
Libra's rollback boundary.

## Git commands intentionally absent from `src/cli.rs`

| Command | Tier | Notes |
|---------|------|-------|
| submodule | unsupported | intentional product boundary (see [docs/development/commands/_compatibility.md#d1-submodule-子命令族](docs/development/commands/_compatibility.md#d1-submodule-子命令族)) |
| sparse-checkout | unsupported | no public sparse checkout command (see [docs/development/commands/_compatibility.md#d10-clone---sparse-与顶层-sparse-checkout-命令](docs/development/commands/_compatibility.md#d10-clone---sparse-与顶层-sparse-checkout-命令)); the underlying skip-worktree INDEX BIT is delivered though — `update-index --skip-worktree`/`--no-skip-worktree`, `ls-files -t`/`-v` `S` tags, and status/diff/add honoring are supported (plan issues/490 SW-01..07, D10 partial reopen); sparsity rules, materialization, and `clone --sparse` remain deferred |
| send-email | unsupported | P2-04 / D19 policy: Libra exposes no mail-transport command and never reads `sendemail.*` configuration, SMTP credentials, or contacts a mail server. Generate messages with `libra format-patch`, then validate or send them with stock `git send-email` or another mailer (see [docs/commands/send-email.md](docs/commands/send-email.md)). |

## Hooks

Private agent recovery erasure is repository-only and keyless for attributable
local catalog-PK/incarnation associations. The tombstone commits before history
prune; headers/chunks/aliases and the session are deleted together afterwards.
Foreign or ambiguous corrupt evidence remains private and consumes lost
capacity, with a content-free post-commit diagnostic; doctor/retry provides no
force-discard. Sensitive DB-copy backups are not anonymized and restoring an
old full backup can undo local deletion/tombstones. Receipt completion removes
the matching artifact and only unreferenced aliases in the same transaction.

Agent doctor terminal-finalizer diagnostics are bounded read-only keyset
pagination (32 sessions/page; 512 sessions/128 receipt findings/run; 1 MiB
ledger SQL byte cap). Malformed rows continue with content-free notes;
skipped keys/truncation mean incomplete coverage, not clean/repair success.
`pending_source` remains visible after stop/resume; superseded and
session-quarantined receipts are manual-only and never overwrite current
state. Artifact-bound retry exhaustion only quarantines the header and keeps
the original receipt revision/status/counters/generation/source unchanged.
Eligible authenticated artifacts may be replayed by `agent doctor --repair`
after alias/receipt/marker/coverage fences are revalidated. A command invocation
counts at most five replay attempts; automatic replays share one 2-second
cooperative deadline and each audited manual attempt gets its own fresh
2-second deadline; deferred findings are not counted and remain unrepaired
until a later invocation. Repair is reported only
after durable checkpoint publication and strict original-receipt completion.
An expired artifact receives one audited manual replay attempt after
quarantine; failure remains visible and never resets the receipt policy.

Agent hook terminal recovery is `SessionEnd`-only: a trusted `SessionEnd`
retains a redacted pending artifact before the checkpoint ref CAS, a later
`SessionStart` may launch a detached source-free worker, and turn-level events
never create artifacts (providers are not assumed to redeliver an interrupted
turn, so turn-level rewind granularity can be lost). While an artifact is
retained, a native `SessionEnd` redelivery exits non-zero with a content-free
retryable message and its coverage claims are not taken over until replay or
explicit erase; a trusted `SessionEnd` from a working directory that differs
from the recorded one exits non-zero as explicitly incomplete. A hook run
outside any Libra repository is never a trusted `SessionEnd`: Codex
acknowledges it (exit 0) and Claude, the hidden alias and Gemini return
`LBR-REPO-001`. A damaged active repository is not outside one: Claude, the
hidden alias, both Gemini entries and an installed Codex `SessionEnd` report
the repository preflight's path-free `LBR-REPO-003` (unresolvable linked-worktree
storage, remedy `libra worktree repair --confirm --path <worktree-path>`), `LBR-REPO-002`
(missing database or unsupported `core.objectformat`) or `LBR-IO-001`
(unopenable database — including one whose schema a newer Libra wrote or whose
pending schema upgrade could not be applied — or unreadable
`core.objectformat`), never `LBR-INTERNAL-001`; nonterminal Codex callbacks
stay advisory. Like every repository command, these entries apply pending
repository-schema migrations when they open the database; pending object-index
repair markers are replayed by the next ordinary repository command. On hosts
other than Unix or Windows, managed capture fails closed before creating
key files with the fixed path-free Unix-host remedy (`LBR-UNSUPPORTED-001`):
nonterminal Codex callbacks print it and exit 0, while Codex `SessionEnd`, the
installed Claude surface and the hidden Claude Code / OpenCode alias exit
non-zero with it. The hook budget is cooperative, not a strict wall-clock
guarantee. Claude Code hook names come from one registry: safely installable events are distinct from the control-sensitive holds `WorktreeCreate`, `WorktreeRemove`, and `PreModelSwitch`, and from the deprecated aliases `SessionStop`, `ModelUpdate`, and `Compaction`. Unknown names and unknown fields are partial and the diagnostic does not include the payload. `libra agent enable` installs every safely installable Claude event. `SessionEnd` has no timeout. `FileChanged` has no matcher and does not emit `watchPaths`. The control-sensitive events stay uninstalled. `libra agent doctor` lists known, installable, configured, and excluded events. An authorized Claude transcript keeps non-empty `thinking` text in the ordinary snapshot. `thinking.signature` and `redacted_thinking.data` stay in those bytes and are Unproven without native proof. Empty thinking is distinct from a missing block, and an empty shell is not a successful read. Authorized `checkpoint show --json` and ordinary export return the retained thinking text and signature. List and graph do not print that body. Live capture, import, and snapshot recovery read those attached fields from the original JSONL, with provider, session, turn, record, and content index. A duplicate key rejects that record and leaves retained bytes in place. A mismatched session file is not associated. Ordinary retention does not create a native artifact. An explicit `libra agent doctor --repair` appends one coverage revision for an authorized legacy Claude checkpoint. The revision key binds that checkpoint and the ordinary transcript content. Repeating the repair with the same content leaves the revision and the stored objects in place. Erase and the cloud mirror keep that replacement together with the old checkpoint reference, and a tombstone blocks revival.

Native Windows managed capture supports local NTFS storage with no-follow
handles, protected current-user/SYSTEM key ACLs, exclusive initializer locks,
and atomic no-replace key publication. OpenCode 2.x content export requires an
explicitly trusted executable; it runs in a kill-on-close Job Object with a
30-second deadline and a 16 MiB accepted output cap, without an OS filesystem
or network sandbox. Windows has no write-time output limit; the parent polls
and rejects oversized output. Unix containment requirements are unchanged.
Windows raw reasoning-artifact file export and doctor object/findings scans
remain unavailable. See [Windows capture setup](docs/commands/agent.md#native-windows-capture).

Durable agent checkpoint metadata no longer retains provider-supplied `model`,
`tool_name`, `source` or `session_ref` values (`model` is recorded as
`unknown`), `provenance.hook_event_name` records the canonical event kind (for
example `session_end`) instead of the provider's native name, and lifecycle
JSONL lines use schema version 2 with a required `identity_scheme`.

Agent capture read and sync surfaces are content-free by contract:
`agent checkpoint show` retains its fixed human-readable safe summary and its
no-artifact JSON fields; JSON show adds only a typed `reasoning_artifacts[]`
metadata whitelist for declared artifacts, without reading artifact bodies.
Opaque bytes require `--reasoning-artifact <sha256> --allow-raw` with a nonblank
`--justification` and `-o` new private destination, independent
`reasoning_artifact_export` audit first, complete identity/digest/length checks
and a 32 MiB hard cap. macOS/Unix publishes 0600 without overwriting; platforms
without this private permission primitive refuse with `LBR-AGENT-013`.
The Codex/Claude Code/OpenCode `ReasoningDecryptor` registry remains `None`.
`agent checkpoint rewind --apply` restores working-tree files only and never truncates or
rewrites the agent's own transcript; `agent session show --extract-transcript
--json` reports only `output_path` and `bytes` (no provider `source_path`);
`cloud sync` refuses a local `agent_session.agent_kind` outside
`claude_code`, `cursor`, `codex`, `gemini`, `opencode`, `copilot`,
`factory_ai`, and `grok` with `LBR-CONFLICT-002` before any remote write.
`hermes` is outside that list. `--force` does not bypass the refusal.
The list is the one the D1 `agent_kind` CHECK is built from, so any later
kind is refused too; the hints say the refusal persists while local-only
capture exists, until a release adds cloud support for the kind, and point to
an external backup of the whole repository meanwhile.
Before its first remote write, `cloud sync` also reads the `agent_kind`
CHECK of existing D1 `agent_session` and `agent_capture_session_v2` tables
(read-only), on every sync, and refuses with `LBR-CONFLICT-002` when a table
created before `grok` support would reject a local kind or when the CHECK
cannot be read; recovery is a new D1 database plus `libra cloud sync --force`.
`cloud sync` then freezes the objects, refs and agent-capture catalog it
uploads into an owner-only spool under `.libra/cloud-sync/snapshots/<run>`
before any remote write; objects or refs written after the snapshot wait for
the next one, and the frozen catalog is published only if the current catalog
still matches it after the object upload and every existing publication check
passes, so catalog rows erased, tombstoned or changed after the snapshot
refuse that phase instead of being published (a commit
during the snapshot retakes it, up to three attempts, then stops before any
remote write; refs are streamed into the spool page by page and the
metadata JSON is uploaded from it in bounded chunks, byte-identical to the
whole-collection form; a sync that fails anywhere never prints `Sync complete.`
or emits `cloud_sync.complete`, and its phase events are
`cloud_sync.objects.finished` / `cloud_sync.agent_capture.finished`), and the spool
is removed when the run ends (if removal fails, sync names the directory, the
snapshot files it holds and how to delete it by hand); leftovers of earlier runs are removed at the next
sync only when their owner marker proves the owner exited on this host, boot
and PID namespace, and are otherwise kept and reported (always on Windows).
Repository migration `2026103001` accepts local `agent_kind=hermes` and keeps the previous kinds, including `grok` and `opencode`; it does not add `pi`. The internal reader keeps official JSONL rows when `active` or `compacted` is true and can fall back to a read-only SQLite view. `libra agent import --path <session-id>.jsonl --agent hermes --yes` imports one such export locally, keeps thinking unproven, and does not scan a Hermes home. The bounded snapshot marks that thinking partial, refuses retained thinking text above 8 MiB, and does not treat it as the assistant answer. The live bridge runs `hermes sessions export --format jsonl --session-id <id> <output-file>` only under the export-job lease, with a 2 second deadline. `libra agent rpc trust hermes` pins that executable and does not accept OpenCode trust. `libra agent hooks hermes observe` accepts the proven observers. `on_session_end` records a turn and does not close the session. `on_session_finalize` is the teardown. Control hooks and subagent events are not registered. Hook install rewrites only a Libra-owned plugin whose bytes contain the `libra capture` marker and match the recorded sha256. Review and investigate launch stay disabled. `cloud sync` still refuses `hermes`.
Repository migration `2026103101` accepts local `agent_kind=pi` and keeps the previous kinds, including `grok`, `opencode`, and `hermes`. `pi` is supported locally with `support_wave=pi` (not `first_batch`) and is hook-installable: `agent list` shows it, `agent enable --agent pi` installs the owned `.pi/extensions/libra-hooks.ts` project extension, `agent disable --agent pi` removes only that owned file, a bare enable skips it when no `pi` executable is on `PATH`, and `agent doctor` reports asset, trust, subdirectory-launch and version coverage gaps. Pi is not a review or investigate launcher; an external `libra-agent-pi` binary is refused as a built-in impersonation (`LBR-AGENT-006`). The receiver stores lifecycle metadata for the session start and tool events. Local content writers are the observe checkpoint (one per non-aborted `agent_settled`, from the session file selected by id and cwd) and the explicit, local-only import, `agent import --agent pi --path <timestamp>_<session-id>.jsonl --yes`: it reads that one file once after consent (no scan, no `--session`/`--all`/`--since`), requires the header `id` to match the file name and its `cwd` to be inside the repository, and stores one checkpoint per turn through the shared import coordinator. The snapshot is stored as partial and unproven, each thinking block (text, signature, and redacted flag as written) is kept beside the turns as retained data and never joins the answer, the last turn stays incomplete because Pi records no session end, a re-import adds no checkpoint for a stored turn, and a session the Pi worker frame refuses stores nothing. Down is refused while a `pi` session exists, an older binary refuses the migrated repository without writes, and `cloud sync` still refuses `pi`. The hidden `agent hooks pi observe` entry accepts only the Pi extension's frame, forwarded while the project is trusted, and stores lifecycle metadata for the session start, tool events, and the run terminal (a non-aborted `agent_settled` commits one checkpoint read once through a bounded helper from the session file selected by session id and cwd, keeping redacted prompts, answers and thinking as partial/unproven data; an aborted settle commits none; error or unknown outcomes keep the checkpoint but are reported as partial, never as a clean completion; a missing or refused session file leaves a metadata-only checkpoint with no transcript content); `agent enable` does not install the extension yet.
and `cloud sync --progress=json` reports agent-capture row failures as one
fixed `cloud_sync.agent_capture.warning` event without session/checkpoint ids
or remote D1 text (earlier releases emitted per-id `session_warning` /
`checkpoint_warning` events). Every other agent-capture sync failure is
likewise one such event carrying its fixed, content-free reason, and every
agent-capture sync failure ends with the shipped `LBR-CONFLICT-002` error
`agent capture mirror failed: <reason>`; agent-capture restore failures keep
`LBR-NET-002` (`agent capture restore failed: <reason>`). Repositories
upgraded from earlier releases keep syncing and restoring: legacy (unversioned
or V1) import-ownership fields are omitted from mirrored and restored session
rows, and legacy V1 `source/sha256` subagent claims/revisions (and their content
links) that the remote catalog does not already hold stay local-only — their
checkpoints are still mirrored, and the immutable checkpoint metadata blob
restore needs still carries that digest — with one fixed success-path
`cloud_sync.agent_capture.warning`; rows earlier releases mirrored remain
restorable. `agent import` rejects provider timestamps (and derived turn
times) more than five minutes past the host clock before they can affect
session chronology (`LBR-AGENT-018`, no durable state for that selection).
No JSON schema version or stable error code changes.

Same-checkpoint pending/quarantine collisions remove only owner-attributed
headers. Unknown/foreign headers and their shared chunks remain unchanged;
explicit erase reports retained capacity rather than proving every copy gone.

Unknown/mismatched incarnation for known same-PK aliases refuses final deletion
until consistent catalog metadata is restored; the first-phase tombstone stays.
A retained undecodable header blocks all new/re-delivered recovery artifacts
and destructive GC/root-walking maintenance, and may retain completion aliases.
Out-of-band erasure reads at most 17 headers/17 aliases; unexamined evidence may
still include the erased session's contents, so erasure warnings are not proof
of complete private-copy removal.

- Stock Git hooks at `.git/hooks` / `core.hooksPath`: `unsupported` (see [docs/development/commands/_compatibility.md#d3-git-hooks-bridge-作为核心特性](docs/development/commands/_compatibility.md#d3-git-hooks-bridge-作为核心特性))
- AI provider hooks: `intentionally-different` (see [docs/development/tracing/agent.md](docs/development/tracing/agent.md))

## LFS compatibility notes

- `libra lfs`: `partial` command compatibility. Libra uses built-in pointer /
  lock management and `.libra_attributes`.
- Git LFS filter bridge (`.gitattributes` smudge/clean filters + `git-lfs` hook
  install): `intentionally-different` (see
  [docs/development/commands/_compatibility.md#d5-git-lfs-gitattributes-filter--hooks-bridge](docs/development/commands/_compatibility.md#d5-git-lfs-gitattributes-filter--hooks-bridge)).
- Repository asset storage policy: current committed binaries remain inline.
  Optional future Git LFS rules in `.gitattributes` are tracked as a repository
  governance decision, **not** as the `libra lfs` command status.

### P1-05b history-default clarification

The command rows above additionally honor history-changing Git defaults through the strict local → global → system cascade: `merge.ff=true|false|only`, `merge.log=true|false|<n>`, `merge.verifySignatures=true|false`, and `commit.gpgSign=true|false`. Merge CLI overrides (`--ff`/`--no-ff`/`--ff-only`, `--verify-signatures`/`--no-verify-signatures`) win; `--no-gpg-sign` wins over `commit.gpgSign`, which in turn wins over Libra's `vault.signing` default. Invalid local/global values fail before history mutation. This clarification supersedes the older commit-row wording that described signing as driven only by `vault.signing`.

### MG-15 merge-commit signing clarification

The older `merge` row's statement that `--no-gpg-sign` was a no-op and that `-S`/`--gpg-sign` was deferred is superseded. `libra merge -S` / `--gpg-sign` force-signs every merge-commit path with the repository vault key; `--no-gpg-sign` disables signing and wins over `commit.gpgSign`, which wins over `vault.signing`. The resolved decision is persisted for `--continue` and `--restart`; a vault signing failure leaves HEAD unchanged and creates no merge commit. External GPG keyring and SSH signing remain deferred.

### MG-16 merge signoff clarification

`libra merge --signoff` appends `Signed-off-by: <committer name> <committer email>` after final message processing. The initial request is persisted across a conflicted or `--no-commit` `--continue`; an already-present matching Git-qualifying final trailer is not duplicated. `-s` remains reserved for strategy selection, so merge has no signoff short flag. `--signoff` is rejected with `--abort`, `--restart`, `--squash`, or `--dry-run`, which cannot produce a merge commit.

### MG-17 merge quit clarification

`libra merge --quit` adopts Git's checkout-preserving state-only merge exit: it removes merge state while preserving HEAD, index conflict stages, and conflict-marked working-tree files. A held autostash is saved into the visible stash list instead of being re-applied, so its only reachability root is not left hidden after the merge is forgotten. The action takes no branch or other merge option. Unlike Git's silent no-state no-op, Libra returns `LBR-REPO-003` when no merge is in progress and tells the user how to start one.

## Sub-face compatibility grading (P0/P1-touched commands)

The single `Tier` column above is deliberately coarse: a command marked
`partial` or `supported` can still hide a broken conflict view, an unparseable
`--porcelain` stream, an ignored config default, or a non-Git plumbing output.
This section splits the compatibility promise of every command reached by the
[plan-20260708](docs/development/plan/plan-20260708.md) P0/P1 work into a fixed
enumeration of **sub-faces**, each graded into one of the four tiers so
`supported` can no longer mask a defect. This is the machine-checked surface
(guard `compat_subface_labels`).

**Fixed sub-face enumeration** (a guard rejects any label outside this set):

| Sub-face | What it promises |
|----------|------------------|
| `common-user-flow` | The normal, interactive human path works like Git. |
| `porcelain-machine` | `--porcelain` / `-z` / exit code / stdout-vs-stderr are script-parseable like Git. |
| `conflict-aware` | Unmerged index, sequencer state, and conflict-aware `status`/`diff`/`restore` are correct. |
| `config-aware` | Common Git config defaults (e.g. `status.*`, `diff.renames`, diff prefix controls, `pull.rebase`) are honored. |
| `plumbing-compatible` | Object / ref / rev-syntax / raw output is byte-close to Git. |

**Bucket meaning** (same four tiers as the top-level matrix): *supported* = solid
today; *partial* = works for common cases but a P0/P1 task is closing gaps;
*unsupported* = the Git-compatible behavior is absent today (a P0/P1 task will
introduce it) and MUST carry a governing plan/`D` number; *intentionally-different*
= Libra deliberately diverges (documented). A face not listed for a command in
any bucket is not separately graded (`—`). Every `unsupported` face is also
recorded, with its governing number, in
[`docs/development/commands/_compatibility.md`](docs/development/commands/_compatibility.md#子面兼容分级cg-01).

| Command | Supported faces | Partial faces | Unsupported faces | Intentionally-different faces |
|---------|-----------------|---------------|-------------------|-------------------------------|
| add | — | common-user-flow, config-aware, plumbing-compatible | — | — |
| archive | common-user-flow | config-aware | — | — |
| branch | common-user-flow | config-aware, plumbing-compatible | — | — |
| bundle | common-user-flow | porcelain-machine, plumbing-compatible | — | — |
| cat-file | common-user-flow | porcelain-machine, plumbing-compatible | — | — |
| check-attr | common-user-flow, config-aware | — | — | — |
| check-ignore | common-user-flow, config-aware | plumbing-compatible | — | — |
| checkout | — | common-user-flow, plumbing-compatible | — | — |
| cherry-pick | — | common-user-flow, conflict-aware | — | — |
| clean | — | common-user-flow, config-aware | — | — |
| clone | — | common-user-flow, config-aware, plumbing-compatible | — | — |
| cloud | — | config-aware, porcelain-machine | — | common-user-flow |
| commit | — | common-user-flow, config-aware, plumbing-compatible | — | — |
| commit-tree | common-user-flow | plumbing-compatible | — | — |
| diff | — | common-user-flow, porcelain-machine, conflict-aware, config-aware, plumbing-compatible | — | — |
| fast-export | common-user-flow | porcelain-machine, plumbing-compatible | — | — |
| fast-import | — | common-user-flow, plumbing-compatible | — | — |
| fetch | — | common-user-flow, porcelain-machine, config-aware, plumbing-compatible | — | — |
| for-each-ref | common-user-flow | porcelain-machine, plumbing-compatible | — | — |
| grep | — | common-user-flow, porcelain-machine, plumbing-compatible | — | — |
| hooks | — | common-user-flow | — | config-aware |
| init | — | common-user-flow, config-aware | — | — |
| lfs | common-user-flow | — | — | config-aware |
| log | common-user-flow | porcelain-machine, config-aware, plumbing-compatible | — | — |
| ls-files | conflict-aware | common-user-flow, porcelain-machine, plumbing-compatible | — | — |
| ls-remote | common-user-flow | plumbing-compatible | — | — |
| media | — | config-aware | — | common-user-flow |
| merge | — | common-user-flow, conflict-aware, config-aware | — | — |
| pull | common-user-flow | porcelain-machine, config-aware | — | — |
| push | common-user-flow | porcelain-machine, config-aware | — | — |
| rebase | — | common-user-flow, conflict-aware | — | — |

| restore | — | common-user-flow, conflict-aware | — | — |
