**t4 wave evidence (plan-20260729 CT-01, first batch).** The rows above for
`add`, `commit`, `diff`, `format-patch`, `hash-object`, `mv`, `rev-parse`,
`status`, `update-index` and `write-tree` are now backed by 77 migrated upstream
scenarios, one ledger row each, under
[`tests/compat-ledger/t4/`](tests/compat-ledger/t4/). Each row cites the surface
it proves and the doc anchor that documents it; the tests are
`command::t4_port_test::*`.

No tier was promoted on the strength of this wave. Migrating one upstream family
shows that the surfaces those scenarios touch behave like Git — it does not show
that a whole face is closed, and grading a face `supported` on that basis is
precisely the overclaim this ledger exists to prevent. The wave's own result
argues for the current grades rather than against them: running it surfaced two
real defects in commands already graded `partial` — `diff --check --exit-code`
discarded the "there is a difference" status bit, and `update-index --add
--remove` exited 0 having staged nothing. Both are fixed; both were invisible to
the previous coverage.


The graded command set is pinned to the plan's P0/P1 surface; the
`compat_subface_labels` guard fails if it drifts from that set or from
`src/cli.rs::Commands`, or if any cell names a label outside the fixed
enumeration.

## Change revision compatibility

Change IDs are stored in the sidecar projection and are not injected into new
Git commit headers. Rewrites preserve the stable Change ID through typed
predecessor edges; existing `change-id` headers remain import-readable for
compatibility and are not a new write dependency.

Genealogy edges are typed and ordered: `amend`, `rebase`, and `cherry_pick`
describe rewrites/imports, while `squash` may have multiple predecessors,
`split` may produce multiple independent visible Change IDs, and `duplicate`
creates a new Change ID with an explicit predecessor. AI causal links are
redacted rows keyed by stable operation ID, repository ID, Change ID, and
optional intent/run/invocation IDs; they never use a mutable commit OID or
store prompt/transcript/secret data. The legacy AI FileHistoryStore manifest
at the session root remains readable; new writes materialize the canonical
`file_history/manifest.json` path without introducing a new undo format, and
the first canonical write removes the legacy flat manifest so a downgraded
binary cannot read a stale copy.
## Merge strategy coverage (MG-13)

Single-head merge accepts `-s ort` (the default), its behavior-equivalent
`-s recursive` alias, conservative `-s resolve` (one deterministic base, no
recursive virtual ancestor or rename handling), and `-s ours`. For one target,
`-s` may be repeated: strategies are probed in order, the first clean result
wins, and all-conflict results use Git's `evaluate_result` score with the later
strategy winning a tie. JSON adds `selected_strategy` without changing the
historical `strategy` outcome field. Multi-head merge still uses automatic
octopus or one explicit `-s ours`; external strategies, `subtree`, and explicit
`-s octopus` remain unsupported.

## Rerere autoupdate overrides (MG-22)

This section supersedes older per-command text that described the flags as
no-ops or unimplemented. `merge`, `rebase`, and `cherry-pick` each accept both
`--rerere-autoupdate` and `--no-rerere-autoupdate`; the last occurrence wins,
and omitting both inherits `rerere.autoUpdate`. An explicit choice is persisted
for an interrupted operation: merge state, rebase auxiliary state, and the
SQLite cherry-pick sequencer state respectively, so `--continue` preserves the
decision. Both flags are no-ops while `rerere.enabled` is false.

## pkt-line error classification

Detected pkt-line framing errors return `LBR-NET-002` (legacy exit 128) at the
fetch, clone, ls-remote and pull boundaries, including an empty HTTP(S)
discovery advertisement. Ordinary network failures, timeouts and packet-read
connection resets use `LBR-NET-001` (exit 128); clone discovery retains
`LBR-IO-001` for non-protocol IO failures. No new error code, command option or
JSON field is introduced. This classification applies when the transport/parser
reports a pkt-line failure; strict ASCII-hex validation is shared by all readers.

Pack completeness is a separate existing contract: clone reports an incomplete
pack ending at a clean frame boundary as `LBR-NET-001`, while fetch/pull use
`LBR-NET-002`. This card preserves that distinction.

### Push receive-pack status reports

Unexpected receive-pack status lines in push reports fail with `LBR-NET-002`.
A missing final flush uses the same code, including empty responses and
truncated unpack/`ng` rejections. Diagnostics use fixed reasons
without echoing unexpected status bytes. Ordinary transport failures remain
`LBR-NET-001`, while completely framed server unpack/`ng` rejections keep their existing protocol
code and hints. Validation failure does not advance local tracking refs and does
not establish whether the remote already applied an update.

### Git/SSH advertisement reader stage

Git/SSH pkt-line reader errors have typed `LBR-NET-002` classification for lengths
1–3 and incomplete headers/payloads. `git://` object-fetch advertisements already surface these errors through fetch,
clone and pull as `LBR-NET-002`, including zero-byte advertisements, with
`check that the remote serves Git data and that a proxy has not altered the response`.
Truncated advertisements previously returned `LBR-NET-001` with a network/transfer
hint; lengths 1–3 could panic. Git discovery now preserves protocol classification; SSH advertisement propagation
and cleanup are described below. All readers require four ASCII hexadecimal digits. Flush, empty payload and maximum-size
frame behavior is preserved, as are ordinary IO/idle classifications. HTTP(S) discovery
command-boundary behavior is unchanged.

### SSH advertisement error propagation

SSH advertisement lengths `0001` through `0003`, incomplete headers (including
zero-byte EOF), and truncated payloads return `LBR-NET-002`. The fixed protocol
reason and marker are retained without captured SSH stdout/stderr.

An incomplete required discovery header has two prioritized exceptions. A recognized host-key
diagnostic with local SSH exit 255 returns fixed guidance and `LBR-NET-001`; this
does not verify the fingerprint. A complete `Permission denied (<method-list>)`
whose list contains the exact `publickey` token, with direct exit 255 and no stdout,
returns a fixed message and `LBR-AUTH-002`. During migration, automation should accept that code
plus legacy `LBR-NET-001` for online `remote show`/`set-head --auto` or legacy `LBR-NET-002` for the other affected surfaces,
for at least 30 days after v0.24.1 is released and through the next patch, whichever is later. Forged stderr proves no cause; other missing advertisements stay `LBR-NET-002`, and raw diagnostics stay hidden.

After an incomplete required header, Libra allows up to 100 milliseconds to
observe the SSH exit status, then requests termination if needed. Other read
errors request termination immediately. The status window, direct-child reap and
output collection share a two-second cleanup deadline. Protocol, typed
host-trust and public-key authentication errors precede secondary cleanup warnings. Ordinary IO
and timeout errors keep their transport classification and may include a fixed
local cleanup warning. Termination can change the observed exit status. This
does not promise cleanup of arbitrary descendant processes.

Clone places targeted host-verification guidance in its structured hints. The
other command boundaries retain fixed host guidance in the message and their
existing `LBR-NET-001` network hint. Human, JSON and machine diagnostics omit raw
captured remote stderr in either case.

The `git://` discovery and object-fetch paths preserve the listed frame errors as
`LBR-NET-002`. All asynchronous readers reject non-ASCII/non-hexadecimal headers
with fixed protocol reasons. HTTP(S) discovery/advertisement framing is unchanged.

### SSH authentication and captured diagnostics

Libra invokes SSH with `BatchMode=yes` for non-interactive, non-TTY, JSON and
machine callers. In a human terminal with an effective `ask` policy
(`ssh.strictHostKeyChecking` unset or `ask`), a first connection to an unknown
SSH host shows a Git-compatible fingerprint confirmation prompt; accepting it
writes the host key to your OpenSSH known-hosts destination and the original
command continues, while rejecting, EOF or a changed host key fails closed.
Libra never prompts for a private-key passphrase - load or unlock an encrypted
key in `ssh-agent` before retrying. For host trust, verify the fingerprint
through a trusted provider console or another trusted channel before manually
updating `~/.ssh/known_hosts`, or make a separate interactive SSH connection and
compare the displayed fingerprint. For example, `ssh -T git@github.com` uses
GitHub; use the actual repository SSH user, host and port. Do not accept a
fingerprint that has not been verified.

`ssh.strictHostKeyChecking` retains its existing `ask`, `yes`, `accept-new` and
`no` values. The effective policy is resolved in precedence order: a non-empty
`LIBRA_SSH_STRICT_HOST_KEY_CHECKING` environment variable, then local
`ssh.strictHostKeyChecking`, then global, then system, then the default `ask`.
This cascade means a first `clone` outside any repository can adopt an explicit
global or system policy. `ask` leaves that SSH option to the user's SSH
configuration; `BatchMode=yes` still prevents interactive decisions. Explicit
values are forwarded to SSH. Choose a host-trust policy appropriate to your
repository.

SSH stderr is always captured, including in terminal sessions. It is drained
from process startup, retaining at most 64 KiB while counting and hashing the
remaining bytes. User-facing errors contain fixed text and a local exit status
when available. Raw remote stderr is neither printed nor logged. Debug diagnostics
contain only the status, total and retained byte counts, and a SHA-256 digest of
the collected stream. Failed or cancelled collection may prevent these metadata
from being reported; no completed digest is claimed in that case. Hashing work
is proportional to the number of bytes drained.

SSH reference advertisements and receive-pack responses each have a 16 MiB
aggregate limit. An oversized advertisement fails with `LBR-NET-001` and guidance
to use the repository’s HTTPS URL if available, or ask its maintainer to reduce refs. An oversized push response fails with `LBR-NET-001`
and guidance to push fewer refs; it is not accepted as a truncated success.
These limits can affect repositories with very large ref sets or updates. The
streamed fetch pack is not subject to this cap. A failed push response does not
prove that the server rolled back its refs: inspect the remote state before
retrying. Existing IO timeouts still apply.

After a complete discovery advertisement, Libra allows up to 100 milliseconds
for SSH to exit before requesting termination, within a two-second total
cleanup deadline. Captured-output tasks are cancelled when their owner exits or
their deadline expires, including when a descendant keeps a pipe open.

### SSH host identity and diagnostic collection

SSH host identity changes retain a distinct fixed warning: the change may
indicate interception or legitimate key rotation. Verify the new fingerprint
through a trusted channel before replacing an existing known_hosts entry; do not
bypass host-key checking. Unknown and changed host keys both use LBR-NET-001,
but their fixed messages and guidance differ.

A stderr collection timeout does not by itself discard complete protocol output
and an observed local exit status. Non-zero exit status and primary read errors
still fail the operation. Unavailable diagnostics produce only a fixed debug
notice, without fabricated empty-stream counts or digests. Stdout collection or
process-wait failures retain their normal error handling.

### SSH limits and host-classification boundaries

These fixed 16 MiB advertisement and receive-pack response limits apply only to
Libra's SSH transport. The HTTPS and Git transports do not impose this particular
cap. If the server provides an HTTPS endpoint, use its HTTPS remote URL when an
SSH advertisement exceeds the cap; this does not require a read-only user to
change the server's refs. Otherwise, ask the repository maintainer to reduce the
advertised ref set. The streamed fetch pack remains outside this aggregate cap.

Host-trust classification requires an incomplete first header with no stdout
bytes observed, local exit 255 and a recognized retained stderr pattern. Once
any stdout byte arrives, including a partial header, host-like stderr cannot
select host-specific guidance. Failures after a complete advertisement retain
fixed generic diagnostics. The pre-advertisement pattern remains a diagnostic
heuristic, not fingerprint verification.

A successful discovery whose child waits for a request normally incurs the full
100 ms native-exit observation window, once per discovery operation. This is
separate from the two-second direct-child cleanup budget; no benchmark or
arbitrary-descendant cleanup guarantee is implied.

### Task shell Rust toolchains (Libra extension)

On Unix, isolated task shells retain the user's existing default Rustup root when `RUSTUP_HOME` is unset and an absolute UTF-8 `~/.rustup` directory exists. Explicit `RUSTUP_HOME`, toolchain selection and sandbox permissions keep their existing meaning. HOME, Cargo cache, XDG and logs remain task-local; Rustup installation/update access remains subject to the existing policy. Configure `RUSTUP_HOME` explicitly when the default root cannot be derived. Windows USERPROFILE behavior is unchanged. See [code](docs/commands/code.md#rust-toolchains-in-task-worktrees).

### Strict pkt-line headers

A pkt-line header must contain exactly four ASCII hexadecimal digits (`0`–`9`,
`a`–`f` or `A`–`F`). Fetch streaming, `git://` advertisements and SSH advertisements
reject leading signs such as `+004`, whitespace, non-hexadecimal text and invalid
UTF-8. These failures return `LBR-NET-002` (exit 128), with fixed reasons that do
not echo the header or payload. A peer that previously sent a signed or otherwise
nonconforming header must send four hexadecimal digits before retrying.

Git discovery also preserves protocol classification for lengths `0001`–`0003`,
missing or partial required headers and truncated payloads. The same discovery
classification reaches clone, fetch, pull, ls-remote and push. Check the remote
Git service or proxy response. Their existing structured error fields remain;
push retains its own protocol hint and the other commands retain theirs.

Flush `0000`, empty-data `0004` and maximum-length `ffff` frames keep their existing
meaning. Ordinary network errors and timeouts retain their existing categories.
An empty fetch data stream before any complete pack remains a network failure;
EOF after a completed pack keeps the existing success behavior. The SSH host-trust
exception, captured-diagnostic limits and cleanup deadlines described above remain.

## Remote push rejection messages

When receive-pack reports `ng <refname> <reason>`, Libra first checks that the
refname is one of the local refs submitted for this push. A rejection for any
other ref fails with `LBR-NET-002` (exit 128) and the fixed reason
`receive-pack rejected an unexpected ref`; the unrecognized name and its reason
are not echoed. Its hint asks you to check the remote Git service or proxy.

For a recognized ref, the remote rejection remains readable. Both its name and
reason use the same sanitizer: Unicode control characters, including C0, DEL,
and C1/CSI, become literal escape text. Each displayed field is limited to 200
Unicode characters after escaping, plus `…` when truncated. An escape sequence
or UTF-8 character is never split, so the visible prefix can be shorter than 200
characters. Ordinary short rejection text is unchanged. These rules apply before
human, JSON, and machine rendering, including the decoded JSON message.

Known-ref rejection still returns `LBR-NET-002` / exit 128 with the existing branch
protection hint. JSON keeps the existing message/hints envelope; a separate
structured reason field is not introduced. Readable remote text is not a trusted
local assertion. A rejected response leaves local tracking refs unchanged; it
does not prove that the server rolled back a partial remote update. Inspect the
remote state before retrying when the server's result is uncertain.

## Whole-tree reset and stopped sequences

Revert currently stores textual conflicts as stage-0 blobs. A whole-tree reset also checks those staged blobs at the stopped revert's conflict paths: remaining `<<<<<<<` markers or an unreadable blob preserve the revert state and emit a recovery warning. This includes `--soft` even when `ls-files --unmerged` is empty. Resolving and staging the content (or removing the path from the index), or replacing the index with a clean `--mixed`/`--hard` reset, allows conclusion. Markers left only in the working tree do not prevent a mixed reset from concluding the stop. The existing revert conflict representation and `--continue` behavior are unchanged.

Whole-tree reset concludes stopped cherry-pick/revert state only after its resulting index has no unresolved conflict stages. Soft/merge modes that retain those stages, or a post-reset index read failure, warn and preserve the recovery state. Libra retains its existing unmerged soft-reset behavior; Git refuses that soft reset.

Reset recovery warnings are printed to stderr after the result in human, `--json`, and `--machine` modes, with the existing JSON schema unchanged; `--exit-code-on-warning` returns 9 after an otherwise successful reset. No stopped pick/revert means no sequence-recovery warning solely because the index is unmerged. Rebase and merge metadata remain outside this conclusion behavior.

Internal resets used by cherry-pick and am retain their sequence-state behavior. Their existing filesystem-cleanup warnings are now visible on stderr in structured modes too; their warning-exit tracking is unchanged.

## Empty-repository discovery framing

An HTTP(S) advertisement that declares an empty repository still has all remaining
pkt-line frames checked. A malformed header, an unsupported length 1..3, or a truncated
payload after the zero object ID returns `LBR-NET-002` (exit 128), with a fixed
reason that does not echo the remote bytes. It is no longer reported as a
successful empty response. Check the remote Git service or proxy response before
retrying. Valid empty repositories, supported SHA-1/SHA-256 advertisements,
existing command hints and structured error fields retain their behavior.

The default OpenCode export budget is 30 seconds; the export lease and plugin
forwarding timeout derive a 15-second margin (45 seconds). Run
`libra agent add opencode` again to refresh an older installed plugin. Schema v2
`importable` and `export_bridge` require a trusted exporter and a successful
platform sandbox probe: Seatbelt on macOS, the existing trusted bwrap probe on
Linux. Availability reports those prerequisites; it does not prove that OpenCode
boots, that a session exports successfully, or that reasoning fields have a
trusted native origin. Timeout still rejects partial output and preserves the
existing metadata-only fallback; the 16 MiB export cap is unchanged.


### Private native-origin issuer registration (macOS)

The installation challenge runs with fresh, owner-only home, configuration, data, cache, state and temporary directories inside the owned private installation. Its initial environment is constructed explicitly; caller `TMPDIR`, runtime-loading overrides and host HOME/XDG settings are not inherited. The temporary directory must be canonical, owned by the current user, mode 0700 and empty before the producer starts. A failed precondition or cleanup refuses registration with the existing fixed challenge error.

Scratch is retained for the child lifetime and cleaned after normal exit, failure or timeout; cancellation cleanup is best effort. Inherited process-group descendants are terminated while the leader PID is still reserved. A parent crash or SIGKILL can leave an owner-only scratch directory, which later challenges never reuse. This isolation applies to the Libra-launched installation challenge. Separately launched OpenCode sessions retain their own runtime requirements, and the current `.3` SOURCE/Source A and real-field archive gates remain unproven.

Ordinary `agent rpc trust opencode` pins an exporter binary; it does not register an issuer or prove native reasoning. The historical native path requires a separately installed, reviewed macOS arm64 `2.0.26-libra-native.1` or `.2` producer and its exact frozen v1 observer recipe, rendered for the current canonical Libra executable path. Prepare that observer using the reviewed private bundle's instructions in an isolated owned setup. Global OpenCode remains independent.

The legacy v1 observer is distinct from the current ordinary v2 observer. It lacks per-directory filtering: never use it with a shared multi-project OpenCode server. Prefer current ordinary capture; any legacy use must isolate one repository per server. `agent doctor`/observer status reports this old v1 plugin as not installed because current detection requires the v2 header. `libra agent enable --agent opencode` replaces a managed v1 plugin with v2 and breaks the old private recipe match. Generic reinstall or `agent enable` is therefore not a repair for legacy issuer registration. This compatibility fix does not change the ordinary per-directory observer or add a legacy installer.

The following registration example assumes the exact reviewed historical observer is already in place; it deliberately omits `agent enable`. The published-binary compatibility gate uses a fresh owned repository, HOME, XDG directories and epoch-0 registry with the original `.2` public descriptor and issuer/key unchanged. Only the measured producer's registration challenge may access that key. It runs no Session, account/model request or OpenCode server, and revokes the fresh registry during cleanup. A successful gate proves registration compatibility only, not Source A, a current `.3` producer or real native-field archival.

```sh
libra agent rpc trust --dir /absolute/approved-private/bin
libra agent rpc trust opencode
libra agent rpc trust opencode \
  --native-origin-manifest /absolute/private-installation/installation.json \
  --native-origin-expected-epoch 0
```

The two native options must be supplied together and only with the literal `opencode` slug. Initial registration expects epoch 0; later registration requires the current repository registry epoch. Inspect `libra config --get agent.native_origin.opencode.v1` locally before an explicit retry. A conflict refuses without changing trust or registry. The registry includes local installation paths and should remain local.

The public descriptor must be a regular, non-symlink `installation.json`, owned by the current user with mode 0600 in a canonical owned 0700 directory, at most 64 KiB and in the private producer's closed minimal JSON format. Libra does not read the private key. Existing exporter trust, actual approved producer bytes, the current canonical Libra executable path and exact installed `.opencode/plugin/libra-hooks.js` bytes must match. A descriptor or export cannot authorize its own path, public key, endpoint or build.

The actual producer must successfully answer a new random, domain-separated Ed25519 challenge within 30 seconds, with at most 4096 stdout bytes and bounded captured stderr. Child output is withheld on failure. After the challenge, one write transaction rechecks current exporter trust and trusted directories, performs expected-epoch CAS, and writes one complete registry row. Duplicate rows, case aliases, encryption, malformed records and size overflow refuse without repair. Limits are 64 KiB and 128 issuer records; revoked history is never removed to make space.

The catalog also contains one separately measured `2.0.26-libra-native.2` macOS arm64 candidate (build `78c1f19e9249da8d0143a079f90aec9a`, binary SHA-256 `28174e893a2b63b75f58af4c77212747f6e1eebce5dd0252cfe64847b342615c`). It adds only the exact first-party `anthropic-messages` endpoint `https://api.anthropic.com/v1/messages?beta=true` under endpoint descriptor v2; receipt and attestation domains stay v1. Its own fresh installation/key, measured binary, explicit trust and epoch-checked registration are required. Existing `.1` records remain valid history, and rotation makes the old active key verify-only. A mismatched protocol/endpoint pair, compatible provider, proxy or changed URL is rejected. `.1` remains the frozen historical NPR-18 harness baseline; `.2` has no real native-field archive acceptance yet. Account/model observations are manual macOS checks outside CI; `reasoningEncryptedContent` is deferred.

A new independent installation key becomes active; earlier active keys become verify-only. Existing issuer keys cannot be reactivated by registration or ordinary binary re-trust. Historical records remain immutable. Successful registration proves installation key/binary association only. Receipt signature, current issuer permission, source coordinates and original native field checks remain required before any archive admission; unknown or unproven sessions stay partial. JSON registration output includes the transient current Libra executable SHA256 for local audit; this measurement is neither a signed policy field nor a pinned historical Libra version.

An unapproved row manually created against the unreleased development `cd53…` observer policy remains rejected. Preserve and back up the entire registry and repository, and suspend affected native registration/archive operations pending a separate reviewed repair. Do not delete rows, automatically migrate them or treat them as approved. The bounded compatibility checks do not establish that every user repository has been scanned; supported ordinary capture can be used in a fresh isolated workspace without claiming the affected repository is repaired.



### OpenCode native-origin rotation and revocation

The human-readable OpenCode untrust message now also reports binary removal, the native revocation count and artifact retention. The existing exporter role label is preserved; use JSON for automation.

To rotate an installation key, prepare a new independent private installation and explicitly register it with the current epoch. The earlier active issuer becomes verify-only. Verify-only and revoked issuers cannot authorize new native capture, import or pending replay. The current permission reader requires one transaction snapshot, fresh independently measured producer provenance, matching exporter trust and a trusted directory; it returns installation data, not a verified receipt or native artifact.

`libra agent rpc untrust opencode` now removes OpenCode exporter trust and revokes every remaining active or verify-only issuer in one bounded write transaction. It preserves all records and already stored artifacts. It remains available while external RPC is disabled. Malformed or duplicate registry rows, capacity/epoch failures and write faults refuse without a partial trust or registry update. Check and preserve the local registry before retrying; it is never automatically repaired. A repeated successful untrust is idempotent and does not advance an already revoked registry epoch.

JSON retains `slug` and `removed` (`removed` means an exporter trust row was removed). OpenCode additionally returns `native_revoked` (the number newly revoked) and `native_epoch` (the resulting epoch, or null when no registry exists). Other slugs retain their existing output and behavior.

If combined untrust refuses because the registry is damaged, suspend exporter access with `libra config unset agent.trust.opencode`. This removes binary trust only and preserves the registry, including all tombstones. A still-active issuer can regain permission after the same approved binary is explicitly retrusted. Repair the registry while preserving its records, then successfully run combined `libra agent rpc untrust opencode` for permanent native revocation.

Automatic provenance revalidation after an unreadable or changed binary or withdrawal of its trusted directory still removes only exporter trust. Native admission pauses while that trust is absent or mismatched; it leaves the issuer registry unchanged. Explicitly trusting the same reviewed build again can restore an active issuer that was never explicitly revoked. Ordinary binary trust never restores a verify-only or revoked issuer. Re-registering an old issuer is refused: rotation requires a fresh independent key. Registration/revocation do not rewrite historical receipts, private installation descriptors or immutable artifacts. Successful lifecycle operations do not prove native field origin; unproven content stays partial.


### Current private OpenCode acceptance baseline (NPR-18)

The unique macOS arm64 acceptance build is `2.0.26-libra-native.1`, build
`bac21cc4027896b8d1836133c347d20f`, SHA-256
`547c36eb928da163ef61d019bad669860d519f2da0b67737c9a3920e831c9196`
and 187635570 bytes. It is based on official OpenCode `2.0.26` commit
`9b4ec5714d481559990db0a816d5dec19541a814`. That commit identifies the official
base; the separate source, patch, lock and build-input digests identify the
patched build. The public `native-origin-build.json` descriptor contains those
reviewed identities and no installation policy, issuer or private key.

Set `LIBRA_OPENCODE_NATIVE_BINARY` to the absolute path of that independently
installed private `opencode` before macOS local capture acceptance. The harness
requires the reviewed version, build digest and size before login or any model
request, bounds the version and login probes to 30 seconds, and puts the same
private binary directory first in each capture/export child's PATH. Missing
opt-in or drift fails the gate; it never falls back to global OpenCode.
The official global installation and existing observer ABI remain separate.

NPR-12's SOURCE_READY_GO covers this reviewed darwin-arm64 build and the current
OpenAI Responses transport mechanism. The existing official source record,
event/export fixtures and synthetic receipt/signature vectors remain historical
and unchanged. Source A is UNPROVEN: deterministic tests, a version string,
registration and this descriptor do not prove real native provider fields or
permit reasoning archival. Failed readiness or quota gates stay partial; the
only authorized paid smoke model is `volcengine-agent-plan/glm-5.3-flash`, with
no alternate-model fallback. This card makes no new model calls. Linux local
functional acceptance remains with the operator.


### OG-16 authorized reasoning carrier

OpenCode's assistant reasoning classifier runs on the authorized export before
ordinary redaction. Visible text remains in the ordinary redacted transcript;
reasoning-bearing turns stay Incomplete under coverage v1 and do not add a new
digest record. The ordinary copy drops
`messages[].providerContext.messages` and assistant reasoning metadata beyond
visible `id`, `type`, and `text`.
Provider metadata on every role's parts, message envelopes, and the export
document/session info is also removed before redaction, except
completed tool `state.metadata.files[].file` strings retain a minimal
modified-file projection. Other tool metadata is omitted. A Boolean
`state.metadata.truncated` is recognized and dropped without changing
completeness, including `false` on ordinary successful tools. A wrong-typed
`truncated`, any other completed-tool metadata sibling (including the
`outputPath` written for truncated output), or an unknown/wrong-typed
`files[]` child is dropped and keeps capture partial. `files[].diff` is
omitted without changing completeness; running/error metadata is omitted
without changing completeness unless it contains a named opaque hint. Such a
partial export is ineligible for live checkpoint writes (`SourceRejected`)
and verified imports (`ImportError::SourceAuthorization`); partial is not
merely a warning. Native completed/error tool `state.content`
keeps clean visible text after nested metadata scrubbing; a named opaque hint makes it partial.
Classic tool `state.content` is unsupported and omitted with partial status.
Known classic nonreasoning part identifiers,
step fields and timestamps, and safe tool title/time/file attachments remain;
native model variants, compaction models and safe error/retry headers also remain.
Schema-shaped native and classic fixtures must stay Ordinary with unchanged
coverage and extractor projections. Open provider response bodies/headers,
classic format/summary/structured payloads, and session fork, permission,
revert or share metadata are deliberately omitted with partial status.
This includes classic user `info.summary` objects and assistant summary flags;
nonempty native user `files`, native `skills[].text`, and additional native
location workspace identifiers are likewise omitted with partial status.
Message and part envelopes use closed field and type projections; unknown child
fields or wrong-type values keep capture partial, while nested provider metadata
and named opaque hints in user attachments, classic `info.model`, assistant
`model` or error objects cannot enter ordinary sinks. Unsafe nonassistant
parts are omitted while separate safe prompt parts remain. Native and classic
tool `state.input` are free-form ordinary content. Classic `state.output` is
ordinary content only when it is a String; an object or array is removed with
partial status. Same-named keys in free-form content are not native-origin evidence. Native
`state.output`, `state.raw`, `state.title`, `state.attachments` and
`state.time` are outside the 2.0.26 native schema and are removed with partial
status.
Named opaque fields, unknown state shapes and malformed/ambiguous exports stay
partial with no artifact. Assistant reasoning keeps its string `text` and unproven
`state`, `time`, `originProof`, `signature`, `redactedData`, and
`reasoningEncryptedContent` in the ordinary copy. Unsupported envelopes may yield a content-free
partial snapshot with the existing generic `SourceAuthorizationMismatch`
reason. Same-named SDK metadata on reasoning is still removed
and kept partial; it never proves native origin.
This does not change the public CLI or grant OpenCode native archive authority;
real installed-model acceptance is local macOS only and remains outside CI.
Capture accepts only one JSON export object with a `messages` array. Flat
records, JSONL, absent/wrong-typed `messages`, duplicate keys and malformed
JSON yield content-free partial with generic `SourceAuthorizationMismatch` and
cannot become ordinary checkpoints. Standalone OpenCode metadata extraction
still has best-effort flat/JSONL compatibility, which does not bypass capture.
Unknown root/message/classic-info keys in a parseable export are dropped with
partial status; independently safe visible text remains, with no payload in
the fixed diagnostic.

The pre-redaction capability remains None for the other promoted adapters; their ordinary snapshot/live/store bytes remain compatible. Nonordinary reasoning dispositions, including zero-artifact partials, cannot become ordinary complete imports or pending envelopes. Native bytes and proof remain one sealed owner; identity views contain metadata only. Production native issuers and OpenCode archival remain disabled, Source A UNPROVEN. No new public metadata enum value, CLI option, database schema or decryption capability is added.

OG-26 adds a private, non-authoritative OpenCode candidate within that snapshot boundary. Before materializing the strict JSON tree, a lexical pass bounds nodes to 65,536 and depth to 64 and records only original receipt byte offsets; a receipt is at most 8 KiB, and the existing 16 MiB source and 512-entry caps still apply. The candidate keeps decoded original values and exact source coordinates separately from the redacted ordinary copy. Missing mapping, malformed JSON, budget failure or unproved origin stays partial with no ordinary checkpoint; the candidate does not grant native provenance or archive permission. Authorized in-memory source bytes rejected before preprocessing, a raw source buffer split from a private candidate, the retained private candidate, and both retained and removed branches of the owned parse tree are explicitly wiped; Serde's internal scratch and partially constructed values during both successful and failed strict parses cannot be individually wiped here. Structural budget failures report `source_oversize`, not an authorization mismatch; a long session can reach the 65,536-node cap below 16 MiB and needs a smaller new capture session and inspection of the content-free partial reason before retrying. Synchronous deadline checks refuse after an overrun but do not preempt parser CPU time; the structural pass limits that work. OpenCode CLI/hook/export-to-Session integration acceptance, including deterministic fake-exporter runs, is explicitly ignored in CI and run locally on macOS. Pure parser and sandbox unit tests remain deterministic CI checks; installed-model tests are local-only as well.

### Coverage schema v2 codec preparation (COV2-01)

The new versioned coverage codec selects claim schema v2 only for a typed
`SemanticRecord::Reasoning` turn; turns without that record select v1 and
retain their frozen bytes and digest. An unknown version or an explicit v1
request with typed Reasoning fails closed. The four-field Reasoning canonical
shape and SHA-256 golden are documented in
[`coverage-v2.md`](docs/development/tracing/coverage-v2.md). The existing
`safe_turn_projection`, `NormalizedTurn` wire form, public `agent graph`
schema v1, default `agent list` schema v1, and explicit `agent list`
schema v2 remain unchanged. COV2-01 introduced the internal encoder;
Libra 0.30.50 includes COV2-11 runtime selection and version-preserving claims/replay.
See Coverage-v2 capture below for the first-stable-release boundary. Neither visible text nor a same-named provider field
grants native origin; unproved OpenCode candidates remain partial and are not
archived. OpenCode Session and model acceptance remains macOS-local, outside
CI.

### Coverage claim v1/v2 arbitration preparation (COV2-02)

The existing v1 reservation calls and `ReservedTurnClaim` /
`LiveClaimCommitPlan` struct literals keep their shape. A separate per-claim
version carrier lets internal DB arbitration distinguish the same logical
turn key in coverage versions 1 and 2. Equivalent same-version reservations
are idempotent, incomplete-to-complete advances only that version, and a
different complete digest conflicts without overwriting the other version.
Unknown or selector-mismatched versions fail before claim writes. Marker,
pending, import and production Session paths are not switched by this card;
COV2-03 owns the marker fence; COV2-04 owns the pending-v2 codec,
COV2-24/22 own sealing and persistence, and COV2-11 owns the Session switch.
No SQL DDL, public CLI change, native
issuer, or OpenCode Session CI integration is introduced.


### Private reasoning identity v1/v2 map carrier (COV2-14)

The repository-private `agent_reasoning_identity` namespace now admits the
existing `identity-v1` key beside a closed `identity-v2` key. V1 serialized
bytes and MAC purpose remain frozen. The v2 descriptor, record and map use
separate authenticated domains. The reader rejects unknown keys, malformed
or unauthenticated maps, and combined capacity above 4096 records or 1 MiB
of stored encoded values before mutating a claim. An invalid map now causes a
hard reservation error instead of a per-turn partial result; import records a
failed receipt rather than a partial/no-op outcome. Updating v1 preserves the
parallel v2 value byte-for-byte. An older v1-only binary can fail closed on
a dual-map Session and may be unable to read even its v1 turn until upgraded;
it does not silently downgrade v2. COV2-03 binds the versioned checkpoint
marker/ref fence; COV2-19/21 own the native v2 identity/retain producer and
COV2-11 owns the production OpenCode Session switch. Synthetic carrier tests
do not establish native provenance or archive rights.

### Versioned checkpoint marker and replay fence (COV2-03)

A marker-bound writer binds each claim's coverage version, logical key,
digest, completeness, next revision, owner, fence token, and reservation state
before persisting a marker. An invalid or duplicate `(version,key)` tuple, or
a stale claim field, aborts the registration transaction. Checkpoint, live
persisted-generation, and recovery constructors preserve the complete tuple;
a v2 write request must also bind its matching versioned claim plan. A later
claim fault rolls back the claim/revision/catalog/ref transaction as a unit.
The 4096-tuple ceiling applies to checkpoints containing v2 claims; larger
historical pure-v1 checkpoints remain replayable against their full revision set.

Committed checkpoint replay verifies the exact persisted revision set, even
for an empty expected set, before returning `AlreadyExists` or
`AlreadyCommitted`. A pre-coverage v1 database with both coverage tables
absent and no coverage-or-later migration receipt may replay only an empty
claim set; nonempty expectations, a partial schema, or missing tables after
the coverage migration fail closed, including no-write and terminal paths.
A completed terminal receipt with expected or persisted
v2 revisions requires a committed checkpoint with matching session, scope,
and exact revision set; an empty or v1-only request cannot acknowledge it by
downgrading. The legacy no-write/prebound path still settles nonempty v1
revisions but rejects persisted v2. A frozen v1 terminal receipt without an
action checkpoint remains valid. Rejected replay preserves a pre-existing
repairable cleanup marker for doctor/GC. The marker JSON stays at
`schema_version: 3`, independent of the claim version. This is an internal
checkpoint contract; it adds no SQL DDL or public CLI surface. Pending v2
replay, OpenCode Session capture and Source A native admission remain separate
later work.

### Sealed native v2 identity checkpoint commit (COV2-19)

A checkpoint carrying an existing verified `NativeProven` payload can now
prepare a version-keyed private identity plan. The source-derived claim set
must equal the submitted versioned sidecar, and the concrete store checks its
marker fences before handing a store-bound transaction adapter to
HistoryManager. That single ref-CAS transaction writes the private v1/v2
records and maps with the catalog, revisions, claims and object index, subject
to the shared 4096-record/1-MiB encoded budget. A replay of that native
companion requires authenticated matching private records as well as identical
coverage revisions. An ordinary versioned checkpoint with the same ID and
claims cannot be acknowledged as a native replay, including when it wins a
marker-registration race. If a later checkpoint supersedes the same
version/key slot in the current private session map, replay of the older
native checkpoint returns a non-retryable replay-payload conflict. Its
committed data remains intact and can be inspected with `libra agent doctor`.
An invalid or unauthenticated private map reports a store failure with a
content-free doctor diagnostic rather than being treated as another payload.
SQL or capacity failure gives no success ACK. The older v1 writer and its
canonical bytes remain unchanged.
This internal producer path does not activate OpenCode live/import Sessions,
durable v2 pending replay or native archival. A synthetic sealed payload does
not prove real provider Source A; unproven fields remain partial.

### Versioned live-reservation read verification (COV2-20)

The internal pending preparation seam can verify a sealed versioned claim plan
against the actual claim rows using its caller's database connection or
transaction. It checks each `(session, version, logical key)` independently,
including digest, completeness, owner, fence, previous revision and live
reservation state. An expired lease with unchanged ownership remains valid;
verification never retains or changes a lease. The v1 API and persisted bytes
stay unchanged. Transactional retain and durable v2 pending artifacts belong
to later cards; this read-only helper does not enable real native archival.

### Caller-transaction versioned reservation retention (COV2-21)

After the sealed versioned plan verifies on its caller-owned SQLite artifact
transaction, an internal helper can retain both v1 and v2 live reservations
there. Each conditional update uses the actual version and logical key and
rechecks digest, completeness, state, owner, fence and previous revision.
Lease expiry alone does not revoke an unchanged owner. A stale tuple or a
later SQL failure requires the caller to roll back the entire transaction;
the helper never opens a second connection or commits early. The frozen v1
retain path and persisted bytes remain unchanged. Pending-v2 sealing and
actual durable artifact failure handling belong to COV2-04/24/22; this
internal capability does not enable OpenCode Session capture or native
archival without provider Source A.

### Same-source native pending borrow (COV2-23)

An internal checkpoint entry now prepares a checked borrow from the exact
sealed `NativeProven` payload and its versioned companion. It verifies the
current capture binding, complete ordered `(version, logical key)` claims and
their source-bound identities before returning associations. Empty, oversized
or orphaned claims fail closed. While pending associations still carry bare
logical keys, a native key repeated across schema versions is rejected; the
bounded wire codec may represent the value without granting native writer
authority. This entry makes no artifact or lease mutation and does not prove
OpenCode provider Source A. The v1 pending writer and persisted bytes remain
unchanged; bounded codec, in-memory seal and durable persistence belong to
COV2-04/24/22.

### Bounded authenticated pending-v2 codec (COV2-04)

The private v2 pending codec represents ordered versioned claim and identity
tuples, including one v1 and one v2 tuple with the same logical key. It
refuses duplicate `(version, key)` tuples and unknown versions. The producer
bounds entry count before claim collection and encoded bytes before allocating
the separate outer-v2 envelope. Authenticated decoding requires the existing
exact-byte MAC witness before typed projection; it checks raw entry and byte
bounds, expected binding, ordered associations and canonical bytes. Historical
v1 envelope and MAC bytes remain frozen.

This codec cannot mint native provenance or a durable pending artifact. The
COV2-23 native same-bare-key guard still rejects such a native write; COV2-24
owns the in-memory sealed artifact and COV2-22 owns persistence and frozen
old-reader rejection; COV2-08 owns recovery/replay. No OpenCode native archive or
provider Source A is established by synthetic codec tests.

### Source-bound in-memory pending-v2 seal (COV2-24)

The private v2 sealer takes a complete redacted payload and its sealed,
source-derived versioned claim companion with a matching capture binding and
prepared alias. It calls the COV2-23 same-source gate, pairs every ordered
`(version, logical key)` claim with its checked inner identity and descriptor
MAC, then encodes bounded outer-v2 bytes through COV2-04. The existing header
version and exact-byte pending MAC domain authenticate the result. The v1
sealer and persisted byte format remain unchanged.

Sealing alone creates an in-memory artifact; it writes no pending row, claim
lease, marker, ref or success acknowledgment. COV2-22 owns transactional
persistence and frozen old-reader rejection, and COV2-08 owns replay. Native
cross-version bare-key aliases still fail closed until OG-29. These synthetic
checks do not establish OpenCode provider Source A or native archival.

### Original pending-v2 member integrity (COV2-25)

The private key owner verifies the existing whole-envelope MAC, capture scope,
storage binding, authorized root and deadline before bounded decoding. Caller binding,
redacted projection, optional source, ordered associations and coverage must
match the original canonical member slices and decoded ordered version/key
tuples. A final deadline check precedes local `PendingIntegrityToken` issuance.
The verifier reads the existing key and fails closed if it is missing; unknown
envelope/payload fields and duplicate JSON keys are rejected.

Canonical source omission has a domain-separated
`libra/pending-v2/no-reasoning-source\0` digest; explicit `null` is rejected.
These local integrity checks grant no native provenance, persistence or
checkpoint ACK, and synthetic positives do not establish provider Source A.
COV2-22 owns the separate private durable loader consumer, with replay in
COV2-08. Neither connects a production Session path at this stage. The v1
member API, bytes and pending MAC domain remain unchanged.

### Private pending-v2 transaction and deferred load (COV2-22)

The closed v2 artifact persists through one caller-owned SQLite transaction:
its pure guards reject all-v1 plans and native cross-version bare-key aliases
before SQL, then it verifies and retains exact versioned live claims before
writing bounded chunks, the header and authenticated private alias. Any error
requires a complete caller rollback. Idempotent delivery preserves original
chunks and manual/quarantine state; persistence issues no checkpoint ACK.

The separate loader authenticates the committed original bytes/MAC and the
COV2-25 exact original members before rebuilding redacted sidecars. The
source must be present, so the pure codec's absent-source integrity sentinel
cannot enter this native load lane. Its non-Clone, non-Serde deferred carrier
keeps the original bytes/MAC, source DTO, exact ordered version/key
associations and local integrity stamp. COV2-08 owns fresh replay fences and
lower native validation; this card connects no production Session/native
restore/ref/checkpoint publication or success ACK. The v1 format, pending MAC
domains and immutable old oracle remain unchanged. Synthetic fixture/storage
checks do not establish real provider Source A or native archival.

### OG-20 private import transport

The authorized descriptor helper can return a native-proven typed collection through an authenticated private import transport response. A one-request key binds request, authorized source, repository scope and collection before the parent restores the complete native proof and partitions owned items by reserved turn. An empty collection retains the previous helper response and source projection bytes; an unauthenticated or unproved collection fails closed without returning candidate bytes to ordinary import. Production provider capabilities and native issuers remain disabled. Source A remains UNPROVEN, and real native field admission is deferred. The transport adds no public option, database schema or decryption capability.

### Pending reasoning v2 codec (OG-17)

The private pending codec pairs its closed inner payload v2 with the live SessionEnd writer and authenticated reader. When an admitted producer supplies a bound native-proven collection, the writer seals its source record and explicit per-turn `verified_nonempty`/`verified_empty` identities. Ordinary pending payloads remain byte-for-byte v1; the outer header and envelope remain v1 with the existing MAC domain, 64 MiB envelope, 128 chunks and 512 KiB-per-chunk limits. A pre-v2 reader rejects v2 and retains its evidence. The current reader checks the original whole-envelope MAC, canonical member/claim association and current catalog, claim and marker fences before the lower reasoning issuer validates the fixed source proof. It restores only reserved-turn artifacts and commits their original stable identities with the checkpoint, claim and ref transaction. A failed append retains the pending artifact and claims; each retry reauthenticates the original bytes and current fences without reopening a provider source or minting a replacement key. Missing or damaged private-key/source evidence requires diagnosis and manual recovery. A decoded DTO or envelope MAC alone never proves native provider origin: unproved fields remain partial and are not archived. Production native issuers and real OpenCode Source A evidence remain disabled; synthetic tests exercise this transport without claiming real native archival.

### Private reasoning identity and garbage collection

Garbage collection excludes the reserved `agent_reasoning_identity` scope from generic metadata object-reference scanning. This applies to the entire stored value, including a damaged value containing a JSON object ID or a nested object ID; those values do not trigger object hydration. This exclusion requires no private reasoning key. Ordinary metadata object roots, private capture recovery exclusions, and capture pending parent roots retain their existing rules. Forward recovery must retain both the private rows and this exclusion while a reasoning identity writer is stopped or repaired. This guard does not create identity records, issue native provenance, or enable OpenCode reasoning archival.

COV2-07 freezes identity-v1 slot, descriptor, record and whole-map canonical bytes and MAC answers. Existing v1 callers keep their original key, HMAC prefixes and byte domains; the v1 map still uses the record purpose. Explicit identity-v2 descriptor, record and whole-map helpers use three distinct prefixes and HMAC byte domains under that same repository-private key. Unknown versions and missing keys fail closed, and a visible-prefix change cannot transplant a MAC between purposes. These helpers do not yet persist a v2 map or enable v2 claims; COV2-14 will bind them to closed v2 record/map bodies and shared capacity checks. A private identity MAC is not provider-native evidence.


## OpenCode native import directory compatibility

Native `info.location.directory` joins classic `info.directory` / `info.cwd`
in import scope discovery. Every string declaration is collected before the
existing canonical-directory and repository-storage gates; conflicting aliases
are rejected instead of choosing the first field. Fact collection does not coerce
non-string values, and existing source/schema validation remains required. Missing
or ambiguous paths retain `LBR-AGENT-016`, cross-repository paths remain rejected,
and same-directory symlink aliases retain canonical equivalence. No native
producer or reasoning source authorization follows from this ordinary import fix.


### OpenCode 2.0.29 idle error projection

The public 2.0.29 idle-message schema adds an optional `error`. An absent error
keeps the ordinary projection. A present diagnostic header requires string
`type` and `message`; optional HTTP `status` is an integer from 100 to 599.
Missing required fields, a wrong shape or an invalid status removes the whole
error and marks capture partial. A `response` body and unknown members are
removed and keep capture partial; an opaque hint removes the entire error.
Existing compaction and retry error handling is unchanged.

These are ordinary typed diagnostics, not native producer or field proof. The
new regression fixtures are synthetic. Actual idle-error output from a live
model Session remains unproven; a successful ordinary session does not prove
that failure path. Real OpenCode/account/model checks run locally on macOS
and stay outside CI. Native reasoning Source A remains a separate gate.


### OpenCode ordinary partial live checkpoints

Automatic live capture can save a safe ordinary copy of an authorized OpenCode
export when unsupported reasoning was discarded. The checkpoint remains
`Unproven` / `Partial`; saved content is not complete reasoning coverage or native
provider evidence. At least one known non-reasoning text or message part with
usable content must remain. A source containing only metadata or erased reasoning
skeletons is refused. This route accepts the current native `messages` export;
legacy classic `info` / `parts` exports remain excluded.

This storage-only route requires strict single-JSON parsing, successful exporter
authorization, exact session mapping, bounded redaction and repository-scoped
source HMAC binding. The source session's `info.id` must equal the selected
provider session. The owning carrier records `repo_id`, Libra session, provider
session and agent kind at bind time and checks them against the actual checkpoint
request. The HMAC is an opaque string; its format alone does not verify the scope
or prove native origin. Generic ordinary consumers stay closed to `Unproven`.

Unsupported reasoning state, including `reasoningField`, is written with the readable
associated reasoning text and time. The ordinary transcript keeps that text, `time`,
`state`, and the other unproven fields. Opaque hints include `signature`,
`redactedData`, `reasoningEncryptedContent`, `thoughtSignature` and `originProof`.
An original `originProof` key anywhere in the parsed source, including malformed
or unfamiliar nested values, or a sealed candidate prevents this fallback.
Only the completed scrub and redaction result can enter storage; a metadata flag
cannot grant this capability.

For this verified storage-only route, `source_authorization_mismatch` also means
reasoning or unrecognized data was discarded; exporter authorization has already
passed. Other paths retain their existing rejection behavior. The marker still
means `Partial`, and Source A remains UNPROVEN. No reasoning artifacts, private
reasoning identity, Complete coverage claims or native ACK are created.

The source input cap bounds each snapshot independently (up to 16 MiB).
Different idle actions can duplicate whole-source snapshots in content-addressed
storage. Cumulative retained snapshots have no per-session total cap and can grow
with idle count × source size, before redaction and storage overhead. This route adds
no cross-idle deduplication protocol; existing action, generation, lease, scoped
tombstone and ref-CAS fences remain in force. A successful ordinary lifecycle
receipt follows a verified durable partial checkpoint write. A failed direct
write stays dirty or leaves a content-free pending intent, without a successful
ACK or generation advance. There is no durable partial payload replay.

Explicit import of this unsupported-state source remains refused with
`LBR-AGENT-020`; this live fix does not enable import or native admission. The
stored ordinary payload remains a single redacted JSON document, with no new
public reason or metadata enum value. Checkpoint/export readers in this build use
that ordinary payload. Older-binary compatibility requires a separate actual
readback and is not inferred from the source shape; this does not relax the V2 import rule requiring partial
projections to have zero bytes and zero redaction metrics.

The official 0.30.49 release includes this ordinary live fix and can save its safe
ordinary copy under the closed rules above. Release completion does not prove native source
nor complete later COV family gates.
For historical OpenCode imports, sessions containing unsupported reasoning state
remain rejected with `LBR-AGENT-020`; their repair is deferred. Real
OpenCode Session/account/model checks run only locally on macOS and stay outside
CI; synthetic tests do not prove native source.


### Current official OpenCode ordinary source baseline

The current official ordinary source reference is OpenCode **2.0.29** at
`042d0832f5f3a73eaa039a58a7d565776f6d4290`. It uses an explicitly measured
official binary and a server/exporter of that same version for local acceptance.
The frozen 2.0.26 source record, existing JSON fixtures and separate private
`.1` / `.2` identities retain their original meanings. The old private `.1`
acceptance helper still accepts only its exact version, binary and build ID.

Official version/source matching does not approve a native producer, register
an issuer or prove any opaque field. Ordinary exporter trust is never native
producer approval or Source A. The separate native.3 registration and real
field-source gates remain independent and do not block ordinary capture;
151 mechanical producer cases are not
whole-producer source/full acceptance.

The 2.0.29 idle header requires string type/message and optional integer HTTP
status from 100 to 599; malformed or sensitive error members stay partial.
Offline synthetic tests cover that adapter; real idle-error failure output
remains unproven.

This ordinary baseline is scoped to automatic capture, a durable checkpoint and the
ordinary redacted export of that checkpoint, with prompt, model and token
projections compared to the authorized source. Unsupported reasoning state is
retained with its text. The source/snapshot status pair is `Unproven` / `Partial`: `Unproven`
is the internal source classification, while `Partial` is the persisted
completeness value. Readable reasoning text is retained. Associated reasoning time is retained with the unproven fields. The model comparison uses `extraction.model` from the
native assistant record. The top-level `model` is hook metadata and can differ.
Token totals use distinct native assistant records without counting the session
summary twice. A no-tool sample proves only empty-files consistency, not a
write/edit/apply_patch positive.

For historical OpenCode imports, sessions containing unsupported reasoning state
remain rejected with `LBR-AGENT-020`; their repair is deferred. This automatic
capture baseline does not enable historical import, pending replay or native
admission. Ordinary exporter trust is never native producer approval or Source A.
Source A remains UNPROVEN.

Reuse earlier automatic-capture evidence only after checking the final source
and configuration and verifying readback with the actual new binary. Unverified
checks remain pending. A successful ordinary sample does not establish native
fields, complete reasoning, real idle.error failures, automatic terminal
completion or full-suite acceptance. Real OpenCode Session/account/model checks
run locally on macOS and stay outside CI. Metadata-only, empty output and skipped
checks do not count as content acceptance.


### COV2-08 同版本 pending 恢复边界

外层 v2 恢复保留同一已提交 artifact 的原有序 `(coverage_schema_version, logical_turn_key)`、原 bytes/MAC 和 typed deferred source；不按 discovery header、裸 turn key 或错误文本猜测版本。V1 loader 的临时错误或总 deadline 到期直接稍后重试，不降为其它版成功。早期 redacted payload 的认证关联在 sidecar 被丢弃时仍阻止普通落库；完整 sidecar 也只代表本地完整性，不能生成 native 来源。现有 ordinary versioned direct writer 与 verified ordinary partial 的 direct-only 规则保持，未获 native 证明的保留 payload 不降成 ordinary checkpoint。

catalog 在同一 writer transaction 复查 alias、scope/workspace、tombstone、receipt/source/marker 与每个版本的 owner/fence/revision；manual repair 的 one-shot/audit 失败仍全回滚。marker 登记后由原 closed checkpoint owner 重新认证原 bytes/MAC 及 COV2-25 原始成员，lower issuer 仍独立判定 native；各版本 identity 从本次 lower 结果重新计算并精确对拍，再用 store-owned permit 与原 versioned claims/refCAS 一起提交。已提交后的中断重试只在原 receipt、source、exact versioned revision 和新鲜 native identity 核对通过后做 no-write settlement；不能只看 checkpoint 存在或把 committed claims 重新要求为 reserved。失败/deadline/cleanup_pending 保留可修复原证据，不 ACK。

这是本地机制路径，不增加 OpenCode Session/model CI 或证明真实 Source A；同裸 key 跨 schema 的 native pending 在父 OG-29 正例与真实 Source A 前仍 fail closed。COV2-08 的正式验收/家族 C/D 以计划实际收据为准。

### Versioned prepared ordinary import (COV2-05, Libra 0.30.50)

The library entry `import_prepared_with_coverage_versions` accepts prepared ordinary turns, an index-aligned coverage version sidecar, a nonzero budget of at most 300 seconds and an optional absolute path to a caller-trusted Libra binary. It checks input size/depth, chronology, privacy-v2 ownership, timestamps and redaction before any lease. The capture scope must use the connected database repository ID, and the absolute storage root must contain that same SQLite database; a mismatched database/storage pair rejects before object writes. Newly matched sensitive content is rejected. This explicit entry alone may choose v2 for the four ordinary record kinds. COV2-11 separately activates typed runtime selection: actual capture/import chooses v2 only when a turn contains supported typed Reasoning, and ordinary turns still choose v1. It neither constructs typed/native reasoning nor proves OpenCode Source A.

Sidecars travel with their turns through sorting. One request rejects unknown versions, length mismatches, repeated bare keys (including across versions), repeated ordinals and missing chronology before writing. Separate requests may explicitly commit independent v1/v2 claims for the same ordinary key once each. An authenticated native association in the other schema still rejects; a version sidecar never supplies native provenance.

Import reservation and binding retain the exact `(coverage_schema_version, logical_turn_key)` through the opaque typed checkpoint adapter. Its prebound versioned branch is private to that adapter; marker generation, scope/deadline, claim owner/fence/revision, import lifecycle/identity and ref CAS remain transaction fences. Failed binding constructs no imported object; stale markers cannot complete the cursor. Committed-source lookup uses batches of at most 64 requested tuples rather than loading session history. Every requested tuple for an ordinal must be committed before the contiguous cursor can pass it.

All-v1 source digest and import identity preimages retain frozen legacy bytes. A source containing v2 uses the explicit `libra-agent-import-source-coverage-v2\0` hash domain with per-turn versions. Privacy identity version is independent of coverage version; mutable privacy-v1 writes stay rejected. One source retains one import identity row whose committed digest/cursor describe its latest request; exact versioned claims, rather than that scalar digest, provide idempotence when requests alternate versions. The library API and its lower gate/store seams add no CLI flags or website command behavior. Pure normalized-turn/temp-DB tests run without an OpenCode Session/exporter/model; actual account capture remains local to macOS, outside CI.

The explicit binary selects only the existing checkpoint object-I/O helper within this import task. Selection checks availability and Unix containment before acquiring any identity lease, restores on return/error/cancellation, and cannot affect other tasks, source readers, discovery, preparation or index repair. `None` uses the existing `helper_program()` (main registration in production; the established private override in unit tests); no host executable, PATH or environment fallback is inferred. This is caller-authorized execution configuration, not binary attestation or Source A. The shared fixed helper argument also serves VerifyReasoningArtifacts in cloud/doctor; those operations are unreachable and forbidden within this ordinary-only scope. Production VerifySnapshot uses the caller-trusted helper response; write OIDs are compared to their expected content, while snapshot verification is not an independent binary/source proof.

macOS real DB acceptance builds the same-source Libra binary before the four exact tests and records its SHA256 before and after. Lib tests locate that binary only in cfg(test) code; the external direct DB test supplies compile-time CARGO_BIN_EXE_libra, including production VerifySnapshot. Existing Unix containment limits these object-helper DB paths to Unix; other platforms reject this opt-in before lease mutation. Pure legacy gold remains portable. Windows compilation/security and four-platform release checks remain required; Linux functional acceptance follows separately.

## Versioned graph/doctor readers (COV2-09, Libra 0.30.50)

The graph reader decodes each selected claim and revision through the existing
closed `CoverageSchemaVersion` enum before joining or skipping unmatched rows.
Both revision history and current claims use `(logical_turn_key, coverage_schema_version)`;
v1 and v2 rows sharing a key/checkpoint remain distinct. The public graph JSON
schema remains 1. Unknown stored versions return the existing
`LBR-AGENT-009` error with upgrade/doctor guidance, without an indexed-success
response or candidate content. No version coercion, catalog mutation or blob
read is added. An unmatched revision is still validated before it can be ignored.

Doctor already joins conflicts by both key and version; its production code is
unchanged. Each conflict remains a distinct content-free, manual-required finding
in JSON and human output, with or without `--repair`. Synthetic catalog fixtures
check version separation, envelope stability, unknown-version failure and raw
identity/content canaries. They do not run an OpenCode Session, exporter or model,
and confer no native-origin proof. These reader guarantees require Libra 0.30.50 or later; upgrading the installed
binary is necessary before relying on them.

## Inert OpenCode visible reasoning classification (COV2-06, Libra 0.30.50)

`classify_opencode_coverage_source` is a crate-private, pure in-memory parser
seam with no capture/live/import callers before COV2-11. The explicit COV2-10
pure-memory preview may consume it. It consumes the original
bounded export before state/proof erasure. Closed native/classic assistant
reasoning parts with nonempty visible text become gated typed Reasoning
records, retaining source message/part IDs and ordinals. Duplicate or missing
coordinates, contradictory session/message references, empty/whitespace text,
state/proof-bearing or unsupported parts remain partial without a typed record
for that part. Malformed/duplicate-key/unknown export structure and budget
failures return payload-free `Partial(error)` when parsing cannot continue.
Safely retained parts with unsupported siblings instead return
`Parsed(source)` with `source.partial = true`; neither case permits cursor
completion from unproved content. A separate valid visible part never clears
a partial flag.

The source/tree byte, node and depth budgets run before materialization; at
most 4096 visible parts are emitted. The existing reviewed preprocessor runs
as a second bounded pass to preserve its closed envelope classification and
owning candidate separation. Both parse passes are bounded; this is not a new
unbounded scan. IDs and the original parse tree wipe on drop. The carrier has
no Clone/Serialize/Debug, and ProviderVisibleText retains its serialization
barrier and content-free Debug. It grants neither native provenance, an issuer,
an opaque artifact, nor capture authority. COV2-10 performs typed redaction in
the explicit preview before COV2-11 connects any persistence path; the retained
ordinary projection is unredacted input until that bounded redaction stage.

Actual production normalization is unchanged: reasoning keeps the turn
incomplete, contains no typed Reasoning record and selects v1. The COV2-06
contract test uses a complete ordinary control to distinguish that decision
from unrelated malformed input, and pins the absence of the new parser/preview from
live/import and the actual authorized capture entry. The separate COV2-05 ordinary versioned library
API remains available. These tests use synthetic buffers only, with no Session,
exporter or model; no user-visible command or website behavior changes here.

## Redacted in-memory coverage preview (COV2-10, Libra 0.30.50)

`CaptureSnapshotService::preview_redacted_coverage_v2(agent_kind, bytes, policy)`
accepts caller-owned memory and returns `(RedactedBytes, serde_json::Value)`.
It opens no source, runs no exporter or model, and writes no checkpoint, claim,
artifact or cursor. The JSON envelope remains 1, with each turn's explicit
coverage version, digest and safe projection. This library data API grants no
source/capture authorization, native issuer or opaque-artifact capability.
Production live/import activation is a separate COV2-11 change; this preview
must not replace its authorized source boundary.

For OpenCode, classification precedes state/proof erasure. Original session,
message and part coordinates are checked before redaction; text equality only
confirms the located part. Ordinary bytes and decoded visible reasoning are
redacted before any v2 canonical encoding, digest or safe projection. Decoded
JSON strings are also redacted before reserialization, so JSON escape spellings
cannot reveal a secret during canonicalization. Additional matches outside the
visible reasoning field conservatively make the result partial. Readable parts
retain source order alongside text and tools; they do not settle a turn without
the normal supported completion event. Unsupported siblings keep it partial.
Unproved state/proof candidates supply no ordinary or typed plaintext: the
preview consumes the separated ordinary projection and destroys the owning
sealed candidate, native and partial-permit authority. Its only retained signal
is a boolean that makes the result partial. Candidate absence is checked using a non-secret-shaped
canary, independently of the redactor's matching rules.

Source bytes, parse depth/nodes and visible-part count remain bounded. Original
parse trees are dropped before the preprocessor's second bounded parse. The
redactor retains its output/working-buffer limits; those limits are not a claim
about total process RSS. Canonical/JSON output also has a checked byte budget,
and the optional absolute deadline is checked between stages and turns. Errors
are payload-free. COV2-10 initially retained an ordinary Rust String.
COV2-11 explicitly wipes `ProviderVisibleText` on drop and before redaction
replacement, and the prepared ordinary owner uses `Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>`.
This does not guarantee erasure of Serde scratch, allocator copies or all
process memory. Existing Claude Code/Codex
adapter preprocessing, ordinary bytes, v1 digest and safe projection are checked
against their established pure-memory path. All these checks run without an
OpenCode Session, exporter, account or model; account capture remains local to
macOS and outside CI. No command, flag or website command behavior changes.

<a id="coverage-v2-capture-unreleased"></a>

## Coverage-v2 capture (Libra 0.30.50)

From the first stable release containing coverage-v2, OpenCode export capture
and historical import retain supported provider-visible reasoning as typed,
redacted turn content. Turns containing that content use coverage schema 2;
ordinary turns keep schema 1 and their existing canonical bytes. Redaction runs
before either digest or checkpoint projection. This does not authenticate
`signature`, `redactedData`, or `reasoningEncryptedContent`: unproven native
fields remain partial and cannot be archived as trusted artifacts.

After upgrading, an interrupted action whose checkpoint was already committed
with v1 (or an empty legacy revision set) first settles that existing receipt
without adding claims or rewriting its checkpoint. A subsequent **new action**
may capture the visible reasoning once under v2; repeated capture then deduplicates.
The old v1 objects, digests, and claims remain readable and unchanged.

If source classification is rejected after that session already has v2 claims,
the entire new capture/import attempt stops, including new ordinary turns.
Its pending receipt/export work stays retryable; no fallback claims or success
acknowledgment are created. An unchanged over-limit or unsupported source can
keep that session blocked. Keep the recorded data, inspect `libra agent doctor`,
and start a new OpenCode session to continue supported capture. A candidate or
unverified reasoning source cannot turn this failure into complete coverage.
These changes add no command flags and require Libra 0.30.50 or later. OpenCode Session/exporter/model acceptance runs locally on
macOS; it is not a CI integration test.
